# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented here.

Format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/). This project doesn't follow strict semantic versioning (it's a classroom tool, not a library), but version numbers roughly mean:

- **Major (v1.0.0 → v2.0.0):** big changes, possibly a new data format
- **Minor (v1.0.0 → v1.1.0):** new features
- **Patch (v1.0.0 → v1.0.1):** bug fixes only

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **The seating chart gets its own present/late/absent picker, borrowed straight from the segmented Present/Late/Absent buttons on the Attendance tab.** Arming Attendance and tapping a boy's whole card already worked, but it only *cycled* — one tap meant "advance to the next state," in whatever order `ATT_CYCLE_ABSENCE`/`ATT_CYCLE_PRESENCE` happens to walk, with no way to set a status directly or see the other two options without tapping through them. Every student's card (seating chart and the List fallback, since both are built by the same `fillSeatLiveCard`) now shows three small P / L / A taps instead, color-coded and highlighting whichever status is current — tap Absent and he's marked absent in one touch, no cycling through Present and Late first. Writes through the exact same `setAttendance()` every other attendance path uses (the tab's own buttons, "Mark the rest Present," a scanned QR), so it can't disagree with them about what got recorded, and "Undo last scan" reverses a direct pick the same way it reverses a cycling tap. The whole-card tap-to-cycle still works too — this adds a second way in, it removes none. No data model change, no `DATA_VERSION` bump.
- **Class Points (off by default, `Run → Settings → Class Points`): the class earns points of its own, counted separately from the boys'.** A rebbi who wants to reward the whole shiur for something — a quiet seder, a good week — had no way to record it except giving every boy points individually, which makes it a boy's score rather than the class's. Turned on, each class gets its own pool that behaves like an extra "student": arm an activity and scan the class's own printed QR card, or tap the new **🏆 Class** button on the Record tab, and the pool goes up by that activity's value — deliberately kept out of the Leader Board so a class total is never mistaken for a boy's. The button itself carries the running total (see below), so there is nothing else to check.
  - **Class scans are inside the class gate, on both sides of it.** A printed class card names its own class, so scanning Shiur Alef's card while Shiur Bais was selected used to score Shiur Alef anyway — the one scan in the app that ignored which class was on screen. Every other surface refuses that (`award()`, `awardWith()`, the personal `PA:`/`PC:` codes, passes, attendance, freeze), and being the exception was worse than useless: the points landed on a class the rebbi wasn't looking at, while the pill, the 🏆 buttons, Recent scans and Undo all correctly refused to show them. `awardArmedToClass()` now refuses it with the same wording the student guard uses, through the same `studentInCurrentClass()` predicate rather than a second copy of the rule — so one rule covers boys and classes alike, and "All classes" still passes for both. The other side of the gate is `logEntryInCurrentClass()`, which now resolves through `subjectById()`: without that, a class-pool row falls through its "no student found, so keep it" branch and one class's scans show up in every other class's Recent scans, History and scan-bar dropdown — **and are undoable from there**, so `↩ Undo` under Shiur Alef would have reversed Shiur Bais's most recent class scan. One line, and it covers every surface that reads the log.
  - **The 🏆 Class button carries the class's running total** — it reads **"🏆 Class 34 pts"**, not just "🏆 Class", on the Record tab and on the seating chart alike. The number belongs on the thing you press: on the chart especially, which is often the only thing on screen (full-screen pop-out, projector), a rebbi about to award the class wants to see what it already has without looking away. Goes through `fmtScoreShort()`, so it reads as coins in Shulchani Mode rather than a bare pruta count. Every copy is relabelled **in place** when the total changes — a full `renderTapPanel()` on each award would rebuild the whole chart, and any drag or drawer state with it, for the sake of one number.
  - **🏆 Class is on the seating chart too, beside ⚙ Display.** The chart is where a rebbi scores boys by tapping, so "and one for the whole class" belongs on the same surface rather than only on the Record tab. Built into `renderTapPanelInto()`, so it appears in the Dashboard's class panel *and* the full-screen pop-out from one place, and in List mode as well as Seating chart (⚙ Display is in both). It sits **in front of** ⚙ Display rather than after it, because the Display drawer's contents flow into that same row (`display:contents`) and would push anything appended after the ⚙ button along when opened. Brass rather than a plain `.seat-modebtn`, and never taking `.active`, because everything else in that row changes what you are *looking at* while this one changes a score — a toggle-looking button among real toggles would read as "class mode is on".
  - **Spending: the Prize Store now sells class prizes too.** A prize can be marked **for the whole class** (a pizza party, a trip) when it's added; class prizes get their own "For the class" section above the student picker, paid for out of the pool, while the student list below shows only student prizes. The purchase log, the "not yet picked up" tracking and Refund all work on a class purchase exactly as on a boy's, because it is the same `storeBuy()` — it just takes a pool where it used to take a student.
  - **Everything a scan already does, a class scan does too.** It's an ordinary `data.log` entry, so **Undo** reverses it (from the printed Undo card, the ↩ buttons, or `CTRL:UNDO`), it appears in Recent scans, the scan strip and History under the class's own name, and it pushes to the Google Sheet Log like any other scan. Contests are deliberately **not** tagged: a contest ranks boys against each other, and a pool isn't a competitor — that separation is the point of the feature.
  - **Where it deliberately does NOT appear:** the Leader Board, the raffle, the picker, the seating chart, attendance, the Gradebook, the printed class sheets, the Sheet's roster, and (since the rebuild) the class's Firestore document and the admin Class Book. That isn't a list of filters that had to be added — it's a consequence of how the pool is stored, below.
  - **New field, purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` bump: `data.classPointsEnabled` (boolean, defaults `false` on new installs *and* existing saves).** There is deliberately **no new store for the balances**. A pool is a reserved key inside `data.scores` — `"CLASS:<class name>"` — because `data.scores` is a plain lookup map that nothing in the app ever enumerates (every list is built by walking `data.students`). That means the pool inherits `score()`, the scoring arithmetic, `undoEntry()`'s reversal, `storeBuy()`/`refundPurchase()` and the rebuild-from-log path for free, with no second scoring mechanism to keep in step with the first — and it round-trips through every existing backup and restore with nothing added. Prize items likewise gain `for:"class"` **only** when a rebbi picks it; a prize with no `for` field is a student prize, which is exactly what every prize that already exists is, so there is no backfill for those either. Nothing can be lost here because nothing is converted.
  - **A phantom student was the obvious alternative and was rejected.** Putting a fake entry in `data.students` would have bought the same reuse and then leaked into all 45 places that walk that array — every surface in the "does NOT appear" list above would have needed its own filter, and the one that got missed would have shown a phantom boy to a rebbi or to an administrator. Storing the pool outside the roster means there is nothing to filter.
  - **Renaming a class strands its pool.** A class has no id in this data model — it is only ever `s.group` — so "renaming" one means moving every boy to a new group name, and the old pool keeps its own name. Nothing is lost (the balance stays in `data.scores`), and `orphanedClassPools()` can detect it — see the header-pill removal below for why nothing in the UI currently surfaces that. Same treatment for a `CLASS:*` pool left over from before a roster was split into classes.
  - **Turning it off never deletes anything** — the 🏆 button and the class prizes hide, the pools and the class prizes stay exactly as they were, and turning it back on restores all of it. Class prizes stay visible (and tagged) in the manage list while it's off, so they don't read as having been deleted.
  - **The 🏆 button is hidden on "All classes"** when a rebbi has more than one class: there is no single pool to charge, and a button that had to guess would put points on the wrong class's board in front of the room. The printed card names its own class, so scanning keeps working from any class selection.
  - **Browser-verified end to end** against a seeded two-class roster: the card scan and the 🏆 button both award the pool while leaving every student score untouched; undo reverses the pool and the pill follows (a stale-display bug found in the browser and fixed — the arithmetic was already right, only the display lagged, which no static check could have caught); the Leader Board contains no pool row; switching class re-points the pill and scopes Recent scans; a class prize is bought from the pool and refundable; the printed Activity Menu carries the class card; the dropdown lists every class, highlights the current one, dims the orphan and explains it; the dropdown updates live while left open during a scan; the pill reads "3 classes" on "All classes" (where the 🏆 button is hidden, since there is no one pool to charge); and a real click on the real Settings checkbox toggles it with the totals preserved across off→on. `test-migration.html` gained 9 assertions for the new field and the pool keys and passes 358/358, corrupted-data scenarios included. No console errors.
  - **Merge note: `sellPrizeTo()`'s class-prize guard now lives inside the shared function, not just the armed path.** #350 (merged first) extracted `sellPrizeTo(s, it)` out of `awardPrize()` so the armed-scan path and the personal `PP:<student>:<prize>` card share one sale. This branch's "a class prize may not be charged to a boy" check moved with it, into `sellPrizeTo()` itself — the personal-card path (`processScan()`'s `PP:` handler) calls `sellPrizeTo()` directly and would otherwise have let a class prize through unblocked on that path alone.
  - **Merge note: `applyGlobalScanBarVisibility()` and the document-level outside-click handler each gained code from two different PRs independently — #344 (shelves) and this one (the class-points dropdown) — and both are kept.** The tab-switch function now computes `barHidden`, syncs the shelves, *and* redraws the class-points pill; the click handler closes the class-points dropdown *and* closes an open shelf, neither one short-circuiting the other.
  - **Follow-up, before release: the header pill (and its dropdown) is removed as redundant.** It duplicated the 🏆 Class button's own label one line down — both showed the same class's running total, the button's copy immediately beside it in the armed-bar. Removing it means the "every class at once" dropdown goes too, which was the only place an orphaned pool (a class renamed out from under its points) could be discovered — `orphanedClassPools()`/`livePoolKeys()`/`classPoolsToShow()` are left in place, unused, as the starting point if that visibility needs a new home later. Nothing about the pool data changed: same reserved `data.scores` keys, same `classPointsEnabled` flag, same award/undo/spend paths. Browser-verified: the header shows no pill with the feature on, the 🏆 Class button and its seating-chart copy still award and relabel correctly, and the Settings copy no longer describes a pill that isn't there.

- **Customize layout can now hide a section, resize it, or park it in a shelf that slides out of the side of the screen — and the scan bar's two pills open those same shelves.** ⚙ Customize layout could only ever *rearrange* four sections across two or three columns: everything had to be on the page, and everything had to be the same width as whatever else shared its column. On the Dashboard a rebbi actually uses, that meant the seating chart — the thing he taps all shiur — was permanently squeezed into half the page by a leaderboard he only glances at.
  - **A new default arrangement: Activities top-left, seating chart top-right, Recent scans bottom-left, Leaderboard bottom-right.** The two things a rebbi actually touches mid-shiur now sit side by side on the top row — pick an activity on the left, tap the boy on the right — with the two he only reads underneath. Previously the Leaderboard sat above the seating chart, which pushed the chart down the page on the one tab it is most used from.
    - **It replaces what you have only if you never arranged anything yourself.** Two independent tests have to agree, and either one alone is enough to leave a Dashboard untouched: (1) you have never used the Customize layout controls, and (2) your arrangement is still exactly the old default with no width, shelf, or hide choice on top of it. So a rebbi who moved one panel, hid the Leaderboard, changed a width, or parked anything in a shelf keeps his Dashboard exactly as he left it — including the case where his own deliberate arrangement happens to match the old default, because from this release on the app records that you touched it rather than trying to infer it from the shape of the layout. It runs once, and there is always ↺ Reset layout if you want the new default anyway. Activities, Recent scans, Leaderboard and Class list each get a Hide button. Hidden means *not drawn*, never deleted — nothing about the section's data, order or settings is touched, and it comes back exactly where it was. A hidden section still shows while you're customizing, dimmed and badged **🚫 Hidden**, and the button that hid it now reads **👁 Show**, which puts it back: pressing the same button again is the first thing anyone tries, so that is what un-hides it. (This is the same hide-don't-remove discipline the nav's `navHidden` seeds use, applied per Dashboard tile.)
  - **A 1-column option, alongside 2 and 3.** One full-width stack — which is what the Dashboard already becomes on a phone, now available deliberately on any screen, and the natural pairing with the shelves below: park the three side sections and the seating chart has the page to itself in a single column. Reducing the column count folds every column that is about to be hidden into the last one that stays, highest first, so nothing is ever stranded out of sight and the sections keep the top-to-bottom order they were already reading in. With one column the ⇄ (next column) and ◀ ▶ (width) buttons retire rather than sitting there doing nothing when pressed — there is no other column to send a section to, and the single column is the whole page by definition, so its width reads 100%.
  - **◀ ▶ Width.** Each section shows its current share of the page as a percentage and adjusts in eighths, from half a column to two and a half. Width lives on the **column**, so two sections stacked in one column share it — which is what "column" means — but with one section per column, or with the others shelved or hidden, it is per-section in practice. A column whose sections all left now gives its width back instead of sitting there as a wide blank margin.
  - **Four permanent shelves, one per job: pick an activity top-left, the Prize Store bottom-left, Recent scans top-right, Leaderboard bottom-right.** The two a rebbi *acts* with are on the left, the two he *reads* are on the right, and each edge carries one handle up top and one at the bottom so they never overlap. **The Prize Store shelf arms a prize the same way the activity picker's Prize Store section does — through the same rows, extracted into one place so a change to how a prize reads cannot land on one surface and miss the other.** Arm a prize, and the next boy scanned or tapped buys it; the shelf closes on the pick so the seating chart is clear for that tap. An empty store says so rather than showing a blank drawer. Their handles sit on the screen edges at all times — the two right-hand ones at opposite ends so they never cover each other — and each shelf does its job whether or not you have ever opened Customize layout: park the real Dashboard section in it and you get that, park nothing and you get a compact list that does the same thing. The Leaderboard shelf draws the *same* board the Dashboard does, from one shared renderer, so the two can never drift apart on ranking or coin formatting.
    - **The two right-hand shelves open and close independently, and whichever you picked last sits on top.** They cover the same strip of screen, so pressing Leaderboard while History is in front brings the Leaderboard forward rather than doing nothing; pressing it again, now that it *is* in front, closes it. A closed shelf's handle always stays clickable above an open one, so opening one never strands the other.
    - **Each shelf has an Open tab → button** through to the tab that holds the whole story — Activities, History, Leader Board. A shelf is a glance, not a replacement for the tab.
    - **A new Leaderboard button on the scan bar**, between Undo and History, opens the Leaderboard shelf from anywhere the bar is.
    - **Clicking off a shelf closes it — without eating the click.** Arm an activity in the left shelf and tap a boy on the seating chart: that one tap scores him *and* puts the shelf away. This deliberately does not swallow the click, which is the only reason close-on-outside-click is worth having here at all.
  - **A "🪑 Seating chart only" button in Customize layout, next to Reset layout.** One press for the layout most rebbeim end up wanting once they trust the shelves: one column, the seating chart with the whole page to itself, and Activities / Recent scans / Leaderboard each parked in the shelf that already does its job. It parks rather than hides, so nothing is taken away — the placement buttons are right there to move any of them somewhere else afterwards.
  - **The Full width setting is now also in Customize layout**, next to the column count — the same single setting as Settings → Full width, in the place where you are actually looking at how much room your sections have. Ticking either checkbox moves the other.
  - **◧ ◨ ▤ Shelves.** Activities, Recent scans and the Leaderboard can be parked in a drawer that slides out of the left or right edge of the screen, reached by a tab on the edge and closed by its ✕. Park Activities on the left and the Leaderboard and Recent scans on the right and the seating chart gets the entire page — arm from the left shelf, tap boys on the chart, glance at the right shelf. **A shelf deliberately does not close when you click away from it**, because arming and then tapping a name is one motion and a close-on-outside-click would eat the first tap every time. **It lies over the page and never reflows it** — nothing on the Dashboard moves when a shelf opens, so the tile you were about to tap is still exactly where you were looking; a strip down one edge is covered until you close it, which is one tap. The Class list has no shelf option — a seating chart needs the page's full width to stay tappable.
  - **The scan bar's activity pill and History button now open those shelves instead of small dropdowns.** Same drawer, so there is never a menu covering a shelf, and the drawer is a workable size for a list rather than a 280px popover. If you have parked the real Activities or Recent-scans sections there, the pill shows you those; if you have not, the shelf falls back to exactly the activity picker and scan history the dropdowns used to show — so the pills behave the same way whether or not you have ever opened Customize layout. The shelves are scan furniture, so they go away on tabs that have no scan bar (Settings, Students, the print pages) and come back untouched when you return.
  - **New fields, all additive, no `DATA_VERSION` bump.** `data.scanLayout` also gains `touched` (has this rebbi ever changed his own layout) and `defaultGen` (which default generation has already been offered, so the swap above runs exactly once and never re-fires). `data.scanLayout` gains `widths` (one width weight per column), `place` (per section: on the page / left shelf / right shelf / hidden) and `open` (which shelves are open). `columns` is unchanged and still holds **every** section, shelved or not, so nothing can be stranded and un-shelving lands a section back exactly where it was. They are normalised in `ensureScanLayout()` — the one gate every reader of `scanLayout` already passes through, and where its earlier field migrations live — so a layout saved by an older build simply picks up the new fields the first time it is used, and a corrupted one is repaired field by field rather than throwing the rebbi's whole arrangement back to default. `migrateData()`/`load2fix()` are untouched. Verified in the browser against a seeded class: a saved layout with no `scanLayout` at all renders exactly the pre-change Dashboard, with no shelves and nothing new on screen.
  - **Ported from #328 (Now-board round 2, superseded by this PR's shelves): the seating chart's context bar, plus a real bug fix.** #328 built its own slide-in shelf independently of this PR — once this one covers the same ground, #328's shelf work is redundant, but two things it built are not: a "who still needs a turn" context bar, and a Recent Scans refresh bug it found and fixed. Both are ported here so nothing is lost when #328 closes.
    - **Attendance armed:** the "✓ Mark the rest Present" toolbar button on the seating chart now shows a live count — "✓ Mark the rest Present (3)" — using `attUnmarkedCount()`, the exact same "who's still unmarked" test `attFillUnmarked()` itself fills against, so the number on the button can never disagree with what pressing it does.
    - **Any other tracked item armed** (e.g. Chazan) — a new "◍ Show only these (N)" toggle dims every tile that's already had a turn this arm, leaving "never" tiles at full brightness. The count and the dimming both read from `trackedTileBadge()`, the same function the tile's own badge renders from, so the toggle and what's on the tiles can never drift apart. Neither control appears for points, Freeze, or the warning flash. Session-only state, not persisted — resets on reload, matching how armed state itself already behaves.
    - **Bug fix, pre-existing, not introduced by this PR:** undoing a tracked log entry (`undoTrackedLogEntry()`) never refreshed the Dashboard's own Recent Scans feed — only `refreshLiveViews()`, which re-renders whichever *tab* is active and has never touched the Dashboard's feed directly. An undo from Recent Scans left the strikethrough showing everywhere except the list you were probably looking at when you pressed it. Now calls `renderRecent()` directly.
    - **No data model change, no new stored field, no `DATA_VERSION` bump.** `trackFocusNever` is in-memory session state only, exactly like the armed activity it depends on.
  - **Three cleanups from browser review, all no data model change:**
    - **Prizes no longer list a second time in the Activities shelf.** `populateActivityDropdown()` (the left shelf's fallback content) used to append the Prize Store's rows below the activities — sensible before this PR, when there was nowhere else to arm a prize from the shelves, but redundant now that Prize Store has its own dedicated shelf right below it. The shelf's fallback function is only ever called from that one shelf, so nothing else lost the prizes.
    - **The "See all N in Standings →" link is gone from the bottom of the Leaderboard**, both the Dashboard panel and the shelf (one shared renderer, `renderMiniBoardRows()`) — it duplicated the shelf's own "Open tab →" (and the main nav, for the panel), so past its 8/12-row cap the list now simply stops rather than growing a second link to the same place.
    - **Fixed a real bug: the full-screen seating chart's grid could force the whole overlay to scroll even when the seats would have fit.** `#seatsBody`'s height in fullscreen was set two ways — `fitNoScrollSection("seatsBody")` in JS, which *measures* the chart's real on-screen position (exactly the "don't guess a pixel offset" design the code comments right above it already call for), and a CSS rule with `!important` that instead *guessed* a flat `-46px` below the overlay's own top. The guess didn't account for the header, the List/Seating-chart toggle, or the Rows/Cols/Print row sitting above the grid — chrome that grows with screen width and with the controls this PR added — so on a screen where that guess came up short, the grid got a fixed height taller than the room actually left for it and the whole overlay scrolled, independent of how many rows were configured. Dropped the CSS rule's `!important` so the accurate JS measurement (already wired up on open and on resize) wins; the CSS calc stays as a same-frame fallback for the instant before it runs. Verified in the browser: with the real gap between #seatsBody and the true chrome height simulated, the outer overlay no longer overflows and the seat grid still gets its existing few-pixel internal-scroll safety net for the genuinely-too-many-rows case.
  - **Two more from a second browser pass — the `!important` removal above exposed a latent timing bug, plus a real shelf-stacking bug found while chasing it:**
    - **Fixed: opening the full-screen chart could land it at roughly half height with a big scrollbar.** The pop-out's FLIP-in animation scales `#view-seats` up from the small Dashboard card over ~320ms, and `fitNoScrollSection("seatsBody")` was called one frame after that animation *started* (`requestAnimationFrame`, not after it finished) — so it measured `#seatsBody`'s position while its ancestor was still mid-`transform:scale(...)`, producing a squashed number that then got written to the inline style. Harmless while the CSS `!important` above always overrode that inline value anyway; once that override came off (this same PR, above), the bad mid-animation measurement started sticking instead of the correct one. Now re-measures again once the animation's own 380ms cleanup runs, on top of the existing immediate measurement (still needed for reduced-motion, which skips the animation entirely and has nothing to wait for).
    - **Fixed: the shelf you clicked didn't always come to the front.** Every shelf handle toggled purely on `.open` — closed → open, open → closed — with no notion of *which* same-edge shelf was actually on screen. So pressing Leaderboard's handle while it was open but buried behind Recent scans read as "already open" and closed it, instead of raising it to the front the way the shelves' own design (and this PR's earlier CHANGELOG entry above) says pressing a same-edge shelf while another is in front should. A new `shelfIsFront()` check (does `side`'s position in `shelfStack` beat its only-ever same-edge rival's) now decides: closed or open-but-behind → open/raise; open-and-already-front → close. Found and fixed alongside it: closing a shelf never cleared the z-index `shelfRaise()` had given it, so a shelf raised once and later closed could keep outranking a shelf opened after it — `shelfLower()` now clears that inline z-index and drops the shelf out of `shelfStack` on close, so the stylesheet's own "a closed shelf's handle always stays reachable above whatever's open" rule (z-index 150) is free to apply rather than being silently overridden by a stale number. Verified in the browser: opening Recent scans then Leaderboard puts Leaderboard on top; pressing Recent scans' (buried) handle raises it without closing anything; pressing the now-front shelf's handle again closes it, as before.
  - **Side shelves are now a Settings toggle, `Run → Settings → Side Shelves`, off by default.** A temporary revert, not a redesign — Ben wasn't sure about the feature yet and wanted it pulled back live without losing the code or anyone's arrangement. `shelvesFeatureOn()` is the one gate: `panelPlace()` reads a shelf-parked section as plain `"col"` whenever it's off, so a section already parked in a shelf simply draws back on the page — the stored `data.scanLayout.place` value is never touched, and flipping the setting back on reveals it exactly as it was left. The four shelf drawers and their edge handles are hidden outright (`body.no-shelves`, the same class the per-tab hide already used). The scan bar's Activities pill, Leaderboard and History buttons fall back to a small anchored dropdown apiece — the same standing content a shelf shows when nothing's parked in it (`populateActivityDropdown`/`renderScanHistoryList`/`renderMiniBoardRows`), just drawn into a `.hsb-dropdown` next to the button instead of a shelf body, matching how that pill and the History button behaved before this PR. Customize layout hides the now-inert ◧/◨/▤ "park in a shelf" buttons and the shelf-dependent "🪑 Seating chart only" preset, and its hint text drops the shelf sentence, while it's off. **New field, purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` bump:** `data.shelvesEnabled` (boolean, defaults `false` on new installs *and* existing saves — this ships already off). Browser-verified both directions: off by default hides every shelf and routes the three bar controls to their dropdown fallbacks (activity pick still arms and closes correctly, Leaderboard/History dropdowns stay live across a scan); switching the setting on with nothing else touched brings back the real shelves, still holding whatever was parked in them; no console errors either way.

- **Seating chart print: every prize chart is now titled, and several can stack on one sheet.** Two gaps in the prize seat modes, both closed the same way the activity charts already had them closed. A prize chart carried no label at all, so a stack of printed sheets — or two sheets side by side — could only be told apart by scanning one; and there was no way to get more than one prize onto a page, so selling three things meant three separate print runs.
  - **A title badge on every prize chart**, naming the prize and what it costs ("🎁 Pencil · −3"), in a colour derived from the prize itself so a glance tells two sheets apart. It appears for a single prize chart too, not only a stack — the band model now carries the prize even when there is only one of it, which is exactly how the activity path already behaved and is what makes one chart and six behave identically.
  - **"Several on one page" for prizes**, beside the prize picker and working exactly like the activity version: tick the prizes you want, each gets its own band down the sheet, ▲/▼ set the order, and the whole sheet shrinks itself to fit one page. Every band is the same seating chart — a boy sits in the same desk in all of them — and only the code in his card changes. Capped at six bands for the same reason the activity version is: each band redraws a QR per student, and a dozen on a class of thirty is thousands of codes and a visibly frozen page. Ticking the box carries the single picker's prize across as the first band, so the sheet you were looking at becomes band one rather than going blank.
  - **Each band encodes its own prize.** The seat card reads the band's prize rather than the stored picker value — reading the stored one would have printed six identical bands under six different titles, which is the exact failure this feature would be worthless with. Verified by pixel comparison per band (below), not by inspection.
  - **The picker and the checkbox never both claim to be in charge:** ticking "Several on one page" hides the single Prize picker, the same swap the activity row does, because two controls both saying which prize prints is how a rebbi ends up staring at a sheet that ignores the one he just set.
  - **New, additive, no migration:** `data.seatPrintOpts.multiPrize` and `data.seatPrintOpts.prizeIds`, both backfilled in `load2fix()` with matching defaults and inert until the box is ticked. `test-migration.html` in sync, 349/349 assertions pass, no `DATA_VERSION` bump.
  - **Browser-verified, no console errors:** a single prize chart showed "🎁 Pencil · −3"; ticking the box hid the picker, showed the list of three prizes and carried Pencil across as band one; ticking the rest produced three bands titled Pencil −3 / Snack −8 / Sefer −25, auto-shrunk to 60% to fit one page; ▲ on the third reordered both the stored list and the printed bands to Pencil / Sefer / Snack. **Every band's QR was checked by re-encoding its expected `PP:<student>:<thatBand'sPrize>` with the app's own encoder and comparing canvases — all three matched, and band 1's canvas was confirmed to differ from band 2's**, so the bands are genuinely distinct rather than coincidentally identical. Switching back to a non-prize Avatar mode hid the picker, the checkbox and the list together.

- **Student prize cards — a boy's own card for one prize, bought in a single scan.** The spending counterpart of the personal `PA:` activity codes, and it closes the same gap they did: selling a prize meant arming it first and then scanning the boy, which is two steps and a screen the rebbi has to be looking at. A new `PP:<student>:<prize>` code does it in one scan, with no arming step at all — so a sheet of these can be handed to the boys themselves and each scans the prize he chose.
  - **A boy who can't afford it is refused by name**, with both numbers ("has 2 pts — Snack costs 8 pts"), and nothing is written. The armed path and the personal-card path now share one `sellPrizeTo()`, extracted out of `awardPrize()`, so the two can never drift into refusing (or charging) differently for the same prize — the same split `award()` and the personal `PA:` codes already have. Both leave the identical pair of records behind, so Undo and Refund keep working exactly as they do for an armed sale.
  - **On the Print: Prizes page, as a second sheet.** The page now has a **Sheet** switcher: *Prize cards — one per prize* (the class sheet) or *Student cards — one per student*. The student sheet is grouped by boy, with filters for group, student and prize, so "one Pencil card for everybody" and "every prize for one boy" are both one dropdown away. Each card carries the prize, its cost in red, **and the student's name** — the page is read two ways, and a card that has been cut off the sheet has to still say whose it is. One sheet is shown at a time, so whatever is on screen is exactly what prints; there is no second print button to aim wrongly.
  - **On the seating chart print, as two new Avatar modes** — *Prize code* and *Prize code + photo*, sitting beside the existing *Activity code* pair and behaving identically: a **Prize:** picker appears (naming each prize with its cost), every seat's code becomes that one prize pre-charged to that boy, and you scan straight down the grid with no arming step. `data.seatPrintOpts.prizeId` is new, additive, and empty by default; the picker fills it from the store the first time a prize mode is chosen.
  - **The live seating chart is deliberately unaffected.** It reads the same `seatPrintCodeKind()` vocabulary but only ever draws a code for the plain `qr` kind, so a prize mode shows nothing on screen — exactly what *Activity code* already does, and the right answer either way: a per-student prize code is something you print, not something you tap.
  - **The floating scan panel refuses `PP:` codes out loud**, as it already does `PRZ:` and tracked activities, and for the same reason: that panel awards through `awardPointsSilent()`, which has no prize branch and would charge the boy while recording no purchase.
  - **No migration.** `data.seatPrintOpts.prizeId` is a new optional string with a `load2fix()` backfill and a matching default; `test-migration.html` is in sync and all 349 assertions pass. No `DATA_VERSION` bump.
  - **Browser-verified end to end, no console errors:** scanning `PP:<boy>:<Pencil>` took him 5 → 2 pts with one pickup row and the right toast; scanning `PP:<boy>:<Snack>` at 2 pts was refused by name with no write; the student sheet rendered 15 boys × 2 prizes = 30 cards, narrowed to 15 with one prize picked and to 1 with one boy picked, each card reading "🎁 Pencil / −3 / Shimon Adler"; the seat modes showed and hid the Prize picker correctly, persisted the choice, and rendered 15 codes (plus 15 photos in the + photo mode). **The seat QR payload was checked by pixel comparison rather than assumed** — re-encoding the expected `PP:S201:<prizeId>` with the app's own encoder produced a byte-identical canvas, and a deliberately wrong payload correctly did not match.

- **Print: Prizes — the Prize Store's own printable sheet, reached by a 🖨 on the Store tab.** Prize codes already printed on the activity menu, but that sheet and this one get used at different moments and cut up differently: the activity menu lives on a clipboard and is scanned all period, while prize cards get taped to the prize box or to the shelf the prize sits on. Getting a sheet of prize cards meant printing the whole activity menu and cutting the rest away. The Store tab now carries a **🖨 Print prize labels** button, in the same place and the same shape as the Activities tab's, opening a page that prints one card per prize — its emoji, its name, its cost, and its own `PRZ:` scan code.
  - **Nothing was moved or removed.** Prize codes still print on Print: Activities under their **Prize Store** section, still appear in Choose codes, and still print on the seating-chart footer exactly as before. This page is an addition, so no sheet a rebbi already prints changes, and there is nothing to re-learn.
  - **It reads `data.store.items` directly, not `menuLabelItems()`** — which is the whole point of it being the Store's own page: what prints here is what is in the store, full stop. Routing it through the code picker's `data.printExcluded` would have meant a prize going missing from this sheet because of a checkbox on the *Activities* tab, with nothing on this page to explain why.
  - **The same three controls every other label page has** — format (tiles or any Avery sheet), which prize, and copies each — plus **Fill a sheet**, which is the one this page exists for: a prize box wants twelve of one card far more often than one of each. Deliberately simpler than the activity menu in one respect: no sections and no Choose-codes step, because a prize list is already flat and already curated — it *is* the store. Copies is clamped to 400, the same clamp the other two label pages use and for the same reason (it multiplies straight into a QR-generating loop).
  - **`data.prizeLabelFormat` is new, additive, and defaults to `"tiles"`.** Kept independent of `menuLabelFormat` on purpose: the two sheets are cut up differently, so a rebbi printing his activity menu on Avery labels may well want prize cards as big cut-out tiles. Format is the only choice remembered here — it is a property of the paper in the tray — while "which prize" and "copies" are deliberately not persisted, matching the other label pages. `load2fix()` backfills it and `test-migration.html` is in sync; no `DATA_VERSION` bump.
  - **Wired like the other print pages, not as a nav tab:** owned by the **Reward** group (`printOwner`), titled "Prizes" in `TAB_LABELS`, hidden from the global scan bar, re-drawn on entry and when the class picker changes (its printed title names the class, so a rebbi with two shiurim can tell two printouts apart).
  - **Browser-verified end to end:** the 🖨 opens `#/c/local/rewards/printPrizes` with the Reward group lit; cards render with correct costs in the class's own currency; narrowing to one prize shows one card; copies of 3 shows three; Fill a sheet sets 12 and renders 12; switching to an Avery format hides the tile grid, renders 12 die-cut cells and persists the choice across a reload; an empty store shows "No prizes yet — add some on the Store tab" in whichever container the current format uses; and a code generated by this page, scanned back into the Record tab, arms the prize ("🎁 Pencil −10", kicker reads **Now selling**). Print: Activities was re-checked afterwards and still carries both prize codes. No console errors on any screen.
- **Backup & Restore: a "Check for updates" card that answers on demand, instead of only speaking up when the update-check banner has bad news.** The passive banner (near the end of app.html, the update-check IIFE) is fail-silent by design — offline, blocked, or a version.json that just hasn't been bumped all mean it says nothing, which reads identically to "you're up to date" even when it isn't. That's exactly what happened here: `APP_VERSION` and `version.json` sat matched at 0.9.0 for a month while real releases kept shipping to `main` underneath them, and nothing ever told a rebbi. The new card always answers — "you're on the latest version" or "version X is out," with a Reload button or (for a downloaded `file://` copy) a direct download link — reusing the same `version.json` fetch and comparison, exposed as `window.mmCheckForUpdate` across the two-IIFE boundary the same way `window.MENCHMARK_APP_VERSION` already is.
- **A CI gate (`check-version-sync.js`, wired into `validate.yml`) that fails the build if `app.html`'s `APP_VERSION`, `version.json`'s `version`, and CHANGELOG.md's newest dated release heading ever disagree.** This is exactly the drift that let the update-check banner go stale for a month — the three files agreed with *each other* the whole time, so nothing caught that none of them had moved. Pure verification, no auto-commit: it does not decide when to cut a release or push anything to `main` on its own, since that would mean granting an automated job write access this repo's branch ruleset otherwise lets nobody bypass (see CLAUDE.md's "Repo settings that enforce this").
- **`scripts/bump-version.js`, a maintainer-run helper that cuts a release in one command** instead of three manual edits across `app.html`, `version.json`, and `CHANGELOG.md` — bumping all three together, renaming `## [Unreleased]` to a dated version heading, and opening a fresh empty `[Unreleased]` above it. The three-file edit being manual toil is very likely *why* it lapsed for a month in the first place; this doesn't change who decides to cut a release or what the number is, only removes the "which files do I have to remember" friction. Used to cut this release: `node scripts/bump-version.js 0.10.0`.

### Changed

- **Version bumped to 0.10.0** — the update-check banner and the new "Check for updates" card were both meaningless while `APP_VERSION`/`version.json` sat stuck at 0.9.0 (2026-07-18) through a month of shipped features (the Custom-points dial, four Gradebook edit slices, the Setup Wizard's Prize Store step, scannable Prize Store purchases, several activities per seating-chart sheet, and more). No data model change, no `DATA_VERSION` bump — this is a display-only version string.

## [0.10.0] — 2026-08-19
### Added
- **Chavrusa Mode: manual and automatic group-building, plus a reusable Past Chavrusas history (Phase 8, first slice).** Spec'd in `docs/Menchmark_UI_Redesign_Summary.md` §6 and never built until now. A new "Chavrusa Mode" tab (Run group) lets a rebbi build learning pairs or small groups for the sitting: **Manual mode** taps students into a group one at a time (reusing `avatarEl()`, the same shared avatar helper the tap panel/Students tab/floating scanner already use — not the full seating-chart tap-panel component itself, which is single-tap-to-award and would have needed a large, risky retrofit to support multi-select); **Automatic mode** takes a group size and generates groups from whoever isn't grouped yet, with an "avoid previous pairings" toggle (checked by default, looking back **forever** through every saved past session, per Ben's direction) — a greedy randomized partition, not an exact constraint solver, so a class too small or constrained to fully avoid every repeat gets a dismissible fallback warning instead of a silent failure or a block. **A session ends every sitting**, also per Ben's direction: "Save & start new" saves the current groups into history and clears the active session, rather than anything timing out. **Past Chavrusas** is a collapsed shelf listing every saved session with **Load** (start a new sitting from a saved session's exact groups — deep-cloned with fresh ids, so editing the loaded copy can never mutate the saved history entry it came from) and **Delete**.
  - **New fields, both additive, no `DATA_VERSION` bump:** `data.chavrusaSession` (`{groups:[{id,studentIds}], pointTargetMode}` — always an object, never null, since it always represents "the current sitting" even when empty) and `data.chavrusaHistory` (an array of saved past sessions, capped at 200). `pointTargetMode` defaults to `"individual"` and is unused by this slice — Individual is the spec's own default and needs zero extra code ("no Dashboard changes"), so shipping only it first is a complete, real feature on its own. Entire Group and Group Entity point-target modes, the Dashboard visual integration (bracketed tiles, scan-bar target label), and the rule editor are separate, sequenced slices.
  - **"Avoid previous pairings" is derived fresh from `data.chavrusaHistory` on every Generate**, not kept as a separate standing index — one source of truth, no risk of the two drifting apart, and (per CLAUDE.md's Contest lesson) safe to derive from because `chavrusaHistory` is its own deliberate, uncapped record, not a wipeable/rotating log the way `data.log` is.
  - Browser-verified end to end: manual tap-to-select and Save as group, grouped students correctly grey out and become unclickable, Automatic's remainder handling (an odd class leaves one student in their own small group rather than being dropped), Save & start new correctly moves groups into history and clears the session, Load correctly restores a saved session's groups with fresh (non-shared) ids, no console errors throughout.

- **Custom points are now a dial: press or scan +Custom again and it counts up, −Custom counts it back down.** Giving a boy 3 points meant reaching for the amount box and typing a 3 — fine at a desk, useless mid-shiur with a scanner in one hand. The two Custom buttons used to *replace* the armed amount, so pressing "+Custom" five times armed +1 five times over and did nothing at all. Now they move one running number, **by one per press**: **+ + +** arms **Custom +3**, and a **−** after that arms **+2**. It works identically however you reach it — the Record tab's two buttons, the printed **+Custom / −Custom** QR cards (so an amount can be dialed with the scanner alone, never touching the screen), and the floating scan panel's own pair, which dials its own separate amount the way it always kept its own separate activity. Scanning and tapping a boy both award whatever is currently dialed, and the dialed amount **stays armed after a scan**, so a rebbi giving 3 points to six boys dials once and scans six times.
  - **Dialing down through zero keeps going into a deduction** — `+1` then `−` `−` lands on `−1` — so a deduction is reachable from the same dial rather than needing a different button. The "Now scoring" pill always shows the running total, and the two buttons highlight by which way the total is pointing.
  - **Custom 0 is a real resting state, and it refuses to score.** Dialing back to exactly 0 leaves Custom armed at 0 and says so; scanning or tapping a boy there is refused out loud instead of writing a 0-point log entry. That entry would have moved nobody's score, shown nothing on screen, and still eaten an "Undo last scan" press — the same 0-point hazard the arming paths have always guarded against, except that a dial makes 0 reachable on purpose rather than only by accident.
  - **There is only one number, and everything shows it.** The amount box, both button labels and the "Now scoring" pill move together on every press: from a box reading 2, three presses read **+2 → +3 → +4** on the button *and* in the box. One press is always **one point**, never the amount currently showing — a box reading 5 dials 5, 6, 7, not 5, 10, 15. The first press of a run arms the number already displayed, because a button reading "+5 pts" has to arm +5.
  - **Typing in the box is the override — it jumps straight to a number.** Want 20 rather than pressing fifteen more times? Type it, and dialing carries on from there. The amount modal on the header/seating-chart dropdown sets it the same way, and it is also the quickest way out of a dial run you'd rather not press back down. The printed personal Custom card (`PC:`) reads the same box, so it now follows the dial too.
  - **A dial run leaves one entry in the scan strip, not one per press.** Each press re-arms, and the strip records every arming change — so dialing to +10 buried the actual scan history under ten "Custom +1…+10" chips in a strip that only holds 20. Consecutive dial steps now rewrite the newest chip instead. They stop collapsing the moment a boy is scanned: the chip that was armed for that scan is left standing above it, so the strip never rewrites what a scan was already worth.
  - **No data model change, no new stored field, no `DATA_VERSION` bump, nothing to migrate.** The running total lives in the same in-memory `armed` record every activity has always used and is gone on reload, exactly like the armed activity itself. `migrateData()`/`load2fix()` are untouched and still in sync with `test-migration.html`.
  - **Browser-verified against seeded sample data, with real mouse clicks rather than scripted ones** — the blur behaviour only misfires under a genuine click. Confirmed end to end: from a box spun to 2, three presses give +2/+3/+4 with box, both buttons and pill all in step, and a − then gives +3; a tap awards the dialed amount and leaves it armed; the printed `CTRL:CUSTOM+` card then moves it by one from the scan bar; walking down runs +4 → +3 → +2 → +1 → 0 → −1 → −2 with the subtract button lighting once negative; 0 refuses to score and writes no log entry; typing 7 jumps straight to +7; scanning a boy *into the amount box* awards the dialed amount and leaves the dial standing; and the floating panel dials its own number the same way (box 2 → 2/3/4, awarding +4).
- **Gradebook: Bathroom Pass columns are now editable — today's cell only (#227, slice 4 of 4, the last one).** Attendance, Homework and Tracker all got a `dateKey`-style correction that can reach any past day; Pass can't, on purpose. `data.passCount` has no per-day history at all — it's a single rolling counter for the current, still-open week, reset destructively every "new week" — so there is no past day for `setPass()` to correctly write a correction into. Scoped instead to exactly what the Passes tab's own row already offers: **"Mark used" / "Give back"**, calling the same `setPass(sid, on)` the tab's own buttons call, editable only when the clicked cell's day is today (`gbCanEdit(item, dateKey)` gained an optional second argument for this one case). A past week's cell stays permanently read-only — decided directly (Ben, 2026-08-19) rather than building the bigger, riskier change of giving `data.passCount` real per-day history to correct a counter that's designed to disagree with its own mirror after every reset. No data-model change, no `DATA_VERSION` bump. **This closes #227** — all four legacy Gradebook writers (Attendance #248, Homework PR #285, Tracker, Pass) now have a write path; removing the four old standalone tabs is next, and is its own separate piece of work (moving the Sheet push, seating-chart badges, and every other direct reader off the legacy stores first). Browser-verified: today's exhausted cell shows only "Give back" (matching cap 1), clicking it correctly clears `data.passCount`/`data.passes.used` and the mirror together; a past week's cell (seeded 3 days back) renders with no `.gb-edit` class at all — confirmed genuinely un-clickable, not just visually similar.
- **Setup wizard: a 5th step for the Prize Store, alongside students and activities.** Step 4 asks what *earns* points; there was nothing asking what they can be *spent* on, so every new rebbi finished setup with an empty Store and no prize codes on his printed sheet — the Prize Store existed but had to be discovered later, from scratch. The new **Step 5 — “What can they spend on?”** is deliberately the same screen as the activities step in every respect a rebbi would notice: three starter prizes shown already filled in (Pencil, Snack, Pick from the prize box), each an editable name + cost row with a ✕, an “+ Add a prize” button, and a warning line if he clears them all. A rebbi who changes nothing still finishes with a working store *and* printable prize codes, which is the same promise the activities screen already made.
  - **Starter prices are seeded at two scales, because by step 5 the wizard already knows which currency the class counts in.** In points they are 10 / 25 / 50. In Shulchani Mode they are 32 / 96 / 192 — that is 1 Maah, 3 Maah and 1 Dinar, real round coin amounts rather than converted point values, so the printed prices read as coins to a rebbi running that mode. 10 *prutot* would have been barely more than an Isar and put every prize within reach of a single scan. The unit word in the screen's own copy switches to match (“points” / “prutot”), the same way the activities screen already switches between “points” and “values”.
  - **Cleared rows stay cleared.** The seed is keyed on the array being *absent*, never on it being empty — the identical rule `renderActivityRows()` already follows — so a rebbi who deliberately removes every prize does not find them grown back when he reloads a half-finished setup or steps back into the screen. Verified by reload.
  - **Cost is clamped at 0 in two places, on purpose.** The wizard clamps as it is typed, so the number on screen is the number that gets saved; `applySetupWizard()` clamps again on the way in, because the wizard's blob is plain `localStorage` and is not to be trusted just because our own screen wrote it. A negative cost would have *paid* a boy for taking a prize.
  - **The bridge merges by name, never replaces.** `applySetupWizard()` writes the wizard's prizes into `data.store.items`, skipping any name already there, so running the wizard twice cannot produce two “Pencil” rows at two different prices with no way to tell which printed code sells which. Blank rows the rebbi never filled in are dropped rather than saved as nameless prizes. Ids carry the loop index (`it<time><i><rand>`) because `Date.now()` is identical for every item written in one pass and the store's own id shape could otherwise collide within a single wizard run. Nothing here seeds the printed **Prize Store** code section — `load2fix()` runs again at the end of the bridge and `ensureStoreCodeSection()` picks it up, so a wizard-created class gets its prize codes on the sheet with no extra wiring.
  - **No data model change and no migration.** `data.store.items` already existed and already had every guard it needs; the wizard blob gains a `prizes` array that is simply absent for anyone who set up before this shipped. `migrateData()`/`load2fix()` are untouched and still in sync with `test-migration.html` (349/349 assertions pass).
  - **Browser-verified end to end, twice.** Once against a store that already had items (proving the merge adds without duplicating or replacing) and once from a genuinely empty `localStorage`: wizard → Step 5 renders at 5/5 → edit a name, clamp a negative cost, remove a row, add a blank one → Finish → first boot writes exactly the filled-in prizes with unique ids, seeds the Prize Store print section, clears the wizard blob, and the prizes show on the Store tab in correct coin formatting (96 prutot renders “3 Maah”) with their QR codes on Print: Activities. The done-screen summary now counts prizes too. No console errors on any screen.

- **Several activity charts on one class sheet — +1 at the top, −1 in the middle, Freeze at the bottom.** Printing the seating chart with **Avatar: Activity code** gives every boy a code that already carries one activity, so a whole scoring action is a single scan with no arming step — but it was one sheet per activity, which for the three or four codes a rebbi actually reaches for all day meant three or four pages on the clipboard with the wrong one always on top. A new **Several on one page** tick (only shown in Activity mode) replaces the single activity dropdown with a list: tick the activities you want, and each gets its own chart stacked down the one sheet, with its own colored badge and a dashed line between them. **It is the same seating chart every time**, so a boy sits in the same desk in all of them and your hand learns one position — only the code inside his card changes. **The order is yours and it is remembered:** the list numbers them top-to-bottom and ▲/▼ move a chart up or down the page. Six charts is the cap — past that a sheet has nothing readable left on it, and every extra chart is another QR code per boy to draw.
  - **The sheet shrinks itself to fit one page, like the A–Z list already does and unlike a lone seating chart.** Asking for three charts on one page *is* asking for them to be made to fit, so a stack is allowed to resize itself where a chart you laid out by hand deliberately never is. It tightens the names and spacing first and only then steps the codes down, because the code is the one thing on the page that has to work with a scanner at arm's length — and it still refuses to go below the existing "small enough to stop scanning reliably" floor. If even that isn't enough it prints honestly at your own size and warns, and a chart that doesn't fit moves to the next page whole rather than being cut in half. Verified in the browser on a 15-boy class in a 4×4 room: two charts fit one portrait page at full code size, three fit with the tightened spacing at 60% codes (about 0.37in square, still comfortably scannable).
  - **Nothing about the ordinary sheet changes.** With one activity — or in any of the other Avatar modes — the page is built exactly as it always was, down to the same elements, so a sheet a rebbi prints every Monday paginates on Monday exactly as it did last week. Works in the A–Z list layout and in Rebbi's view too (one "Front of room" per sheet rather than one per chart, since it is the same room three times over).
  - **New fields, both additive, no `DATA_VERSION` bump:** `data.seatPrintOpts.multiActivity` (the tick) and `data.seatPrintOpts.activityIds` (which charts, **in print order** — a list, not a set). Both default to the single-chart behaviour every existing save already has. An id whose activity has since been deleted, renamed, or hidden from the class in front of you is skipped when the sheet is drawn rather than erased from the setting, so switching to another class and back doesn't quietly forget your selection. Migration harness re-run: 349/349 checks pass.
- **Gradebook: Tracker columns are now editable (#227, slice 3 of 4).** Attendance (#248) and Homework (PR #285) got this first; Tracker was the harder case because `data.trackerLog` has no per-day bucket the way `data.attendance`/`data.hw` do — which day an entry belongs to has always been derived purely from its `ts`. A click-to-edit cell now shows a target-count input (a number, not the enum picker Attendance/Homework use, since "how many" has no fixed option list); saving diffs the typed number against that day's actual entries and adds/removes the difference through the exact same `trackerLogAdd()`/`trackerUndoOne()` the Tracker tab's own +1 and an armed scan already use — never the mirror directly, so the tab and the Gradebook can never disagree. **The mechanism:** a correction entry now carries an optional `about` field (the day it corrects) alongside its real `ts` (when the correction was actually made) — the same ts/date split `setAttendance()`/`setHw()` already use for their own mirror writes, ported here since a log entry has no day-keyed bucket to select into. `trackerMatchesDay()` prefers `about` when present and falls back to the ts-derived day otherwise, so every existing entry (which has no `about`) keeps behaving exactly as before. Purely additive — no `DATA_VERSION` bump, no `load2fix()` backfill needed. Browser-verified end to end: editing today's count grows/shrinks the mirror and trackerLog correctly and marks the right History rows undone; editing a past day only touches that day's entries and leaves every other day's count untouched; the picker's starting number always matches what the grid cell already shows. Pass (slice 4, the last one) is scoped differently — `data.passCount` has no per-day history at all, only a current-week rolling counter, so its Gradebook editing shipped separately as "this week only," not a `dateKey`-style port (see the Bathroom Pass entry above).
- **Prizes from the Prize Store are now scannable and armable, exactly like an activity — scan the prize, then scan or tap each boy, and the cost comes off his own balance.** Buying a prize used to be a Store-tab-only errand: leave whatever you were doing, open Reward → Store, find the boy in a dropdown, tap the prize. That is the wrong shape for the moment it actually happens, which is a boy standing at the rebbi's elbow asking to spend his points while the seating chart is on screen. Every prize now behaves like a point activity does:
  - **A printed QR code per prize (`PRZ:<id>`), on the same sheet as everything else.** Prize codes join the activity codes on **Print: Activities** (the 🖨 on the Activities tab) under a new **Prize Store** section, so one clipboard still covers the whole room — scan the prize card to arm it, then scan students. The card's sub-line quotes the real cost through the same formatter the rest of the app uses, so a Shulchani class reads it in coins and a points class in points. The section is pushed into the rebbi's **own** section list the first time he actually has a prize to print, exactly the way the Shulchani coin section already is — he can rename it, reorder it, drag codes out of it, or delete it, and it is seeded once so deleting it keeps it deleted.
  - **A Prize Store group in the seating chart's activity dropdown.** The same dropdown that arms “On time +1” now lists every prize below the activities, each showing its cost as the deduction it is (−3, in red). Arm one and the pill reads “🎁 Pencil −3”; the kicker above it reads **Now selling** rather than “Now scoring”, so there is never a question about what the next tap will do.
  - **Buying is one code path, whichever surface starts it.** The Store tab's own buy button and a scanned/tapped purchase both go through one new `storePurchase()`, extracted verbatim out of `storeBuy()` — the same reason `applyScore()` was once extracted out of `award()`. There is one definition of what buying a prize *does*, and two surfaces that report it differently, so the two can never drift into disagreeing about what a purchase is.
  - **A purchase that can't be afforded is refused out loud, by name** (“has 5 pts — Sefer costs 50 pts”), never silently. So is a code for a prize that was deleted after it was armed. Neither refusal carries a banner band, so a rebbi who has switched the negative banners off still sees both — a refusal nobody sees reads as a purchase that worked.
  - **A frozen boy may still buy.** Freeze has only ever blocked *giving* (`armed.delta>0`), and spending points already earned is not a reward — blocking it would turn a freeze into a confiscation of a boy's balance rather than a pause on his earning.
  - **“Give to all” refuses a prize outright.** That button (and its printed `CTRL:GIVEALL` card, which is reachable by one mis-aimed scan) runs the *points* path: a prize let through would have charged every boy in the class its cost and recorded no purchase for any of them — every balance down, nobody owed anything.
- **Undo now reverses both halves of a store purchase, and a refund can no longer be applied twice.** A purchase has always written two records — the `data.store.log` row saying which prize the boy is still owed, and the `data.log` spend entry saying what it cost him — but nothing connected them. Undoing a purchase handed the points back and left the prize sitting on the “not picked up yet” list, so the rebbi's pickup list insisted a boy had bought something he had just been refunded for; and refunding from the Store tab left the spend entry standing, so ↩ Undo would happily reverse it a second time and hand the cost back twice for one prize. Both are closed by a `pid` stamped on the pair at purchase time: undo removes the matching pickup row, and a refund marks the spend entry undone so whichever reversal the rebbi reaches for, the other can no longer double it. Matched on `pid` only — never on name or timestamp — so it is exact.
  - **Purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` bump.** New optional `pid` on each `data.store.log` row and `purchaseId` on its spend entry, plus a one-shot `storeCodeSectionSeeded` flag for the print section. A purchase made before this shipped carries no `pid` and has no linked entry to find; both reversal paths test it for truth before matching, so an old purchase behaves exactly as it always has and no pickup row can be lost. `load2fix()` normalises the field's shape, `test-migration.html`'s copies are in sync, and all 349 harness assertions pass including every corrupt-data scenario.
  - **Deliberately not built: selling from the floating scan panel.** That panel awards through `awardPointsSilent()`, which has no prize branch — a prize armed there would have written a plain point deduction and no purchase record, charging the boy and owing him nothing. It refuses a `PRZ:` code out loud instead, the same treatment and for the same reason it already refuses a tracked activity. Selling is a deliberate, one-boy action; that panel is the fast-scoring surface.
  - **One pre-existing gap left alone and worth knowing:** undoing a *refund* (not a purchase) still only reverses its points — it re-charges the boy without putting the prize back on the pickup list. That is a property of how `kind:"refund"` entries have always worked, not something this change introduced, and fixing it means teaching the refund entry to carry the row it removed. Flagged rather than folded in.

- **Sign in on a new computer and your class comes back on its own — the "I got a new laptop" path, which until now quietly did nothing.** Signing in already created a real account and, for a school account, claimed the class up to the server; what it never did was bring the class back *down*. A rebbi who signed in on a fresh machine ended up correctly signed in and still staring at an empty roster, with the only real way back being a `.json` file he had to still have, or a Google Sheet link he had to still have. Now, when you sign in on a device with no class on it and there's a backup sitting in your Drive, the app offers to put it back — full class, points, activities, log, settings, exactly as the last device left it — and restores it through the *same* code path as restoring a backup file by hand, so a class restored by signing in is indistinguishable from one restored the old way (coin-mode classes still get asked the same Shulchani question, too).
  - **The offer is no longer school-accounts-only.** It began life as a one-way "upload your existing class" step for school accounts, and skipped everyone else — which was backwards, since a rebbi with no school is exactly the rebbi whose class lives in Drive and nowhere else. The two now differ only at the moment you accept: a school account uploads *and* restores; a rebbi on his own **only restores, and nothing is sent to the server** — his class stays his, which is the whole point of the two-tier split.
  - **Nothing is ever overwritten.** The offer only appears on a device with no roster on it at all, and it is an offer — it asks before it writes, and declining leaves everything untouched. The restore itself independently refuses to run if there are any students on the device, so there is no route to it that can cost a rebbi a class he already had. Both checks test the roster directly: an earlier revision leaned on the existing "is there a class worth uploading?" signal, which reports *no class* when the log is empty or the last scan is over 30 days old — so a rebbi who set his class up in advance, or came back from a break, could have had a live class replaced by an older Drive backup. Caught in testing before release; the fix is that a destructive restore now asks the only question that actually matters, which is whether there is a roster here.
  - **An empty device can no longer overwrite a good backup — and this one was real.** Menchmark keeps one backup file in Drive and rewrites it in place, and two separate paths wrote to it without ever asking whether there was anything worth writing: the automatic once-per-visit backup, and the write that fired the instant Drive was connected. On a rebbi's *new* computer, where the class is by definition not there yet, both wrote an empty class straight over the backup he had signed in to get back. Found by testing this feature against a real Google account, where it silently emptied the backup file twice. Neither path will now write a class with no boys in it. "Back up now" is untouched — that is a rebbi saying exactly what he wants, and it still does it.
  - **New fields: none. No `DATA_VERSION` bump.** Restored data goes through `migrateData()` + `load2fix()` exactly as a restored file does, so an older backup is brought up to the current version on the way in.
- **Signing out on someone else's computer can take your class with you.** Borrowing another rebbi's computer for one shiur used to mean leaving your boys, their scores and your whole log sitting on it — signing out deliberately never touched the class, which is right on your own machine and plainly wrong on a colleague's. Sign out now asks, but only when there is actually something on the device to leave behind: **Keep it here** (your own computer, nothing changes — exactly as before) or **Back up and remove it**. Removing backs the class up to your Drive *first* and **only removes it if that backup provably succeeded** — if Drive can't be reached, nothing is removed and it says so, because losing a class would be the worst possible outcome of a button whose whole purpose is tidiness. If Drive was never connected there is nowhere safe to put it, so it declines and points at Backup & Restore instead. The computer is left exactly as a never-used one looks — no class name, no boys, no scores, no school — and your class comes back the next time you sign in on your own machine.
- **Signing in on a new computer explains itself now.** A rebbi who signs in to get his class back was being asked to "connect Google Drive to back up your class" — on a computer where he has no class yet, which describes the opposite of what he came to do. On an empty computer it now says it is *looking for* his class, and that the permission is what opens the backup file where the class actually lives. The found-class step is named as the thing he lost rather than as a file operation — "Found your class — Shiur Aleph, 18 boys, backed up 2 hours ago", with **Bring it back**.
- **Setup's "Restore / Import" screen offers signing in as a third way back, alongside the backup file and the Google Sheet.** This is the screen a rebbi lands on when he opens Menchmark on a new computer and wants his class back, and it previously offered only the two paths that require him to be holding something — a file, or a link. Signing in requires neither. The two existing paths are untouched and unmoved for anyone who has never signed in, and skipping this leaves setup exactly as it was: it is a third option on a screen full of options, not a step or a gate.
- **The Activities tab is now scoped to the selected class, and Freeze can no longer be deleted.** Previously every activity showed regardless of which class was selected — a "master" list a rebbi juggling two periods had to wade through to find the activities that actually applied to whichever class was in front of him, even though the per-activity "Groups:" checklist already restricted where each one could be armed. `renderActivities()` now filters through `actVisibleInCurrentClass()` — the same check the Record-tab buttons and scan bar dropdown already use — so a specific class shows only its own activities; "All classes" is unaffected (that check reads `activeStudents()`, which is the whole roster there) and now also shows a small badge on each row naming which class(es) it's restricted to (or "All classes" if unrestricted), since with everything mixed together that's no longer obvious from context alone. The class selector's `change` handler now also re-renders the Activities tab when it's open, and the per-class "Groups:" checkbox handler now does too — neither needed to before, since nothing on this tab used to depend on the selected class. **Freeze is now undeletable, like Homework/Attendance/Bathroom Pass already were** (`coreTrackedActivity()`) — it was previously left off that list because, unlike those three, it owns no tab or store of its own, but it's seeded exactly once behind a one-shot flag the same way they are, so deleting the last Freeze activity removed the feature for good with no way back. It gets the same "Built in — uncheck Show to hide it" treatment now, via its own check (`freezeActivity()`) rather than joining `coreTrackedActivity()`, since the reasoning is different (no stranded tab, just no reseed). No data model change either way — `a.groups` and the freeze-seeding flag already existed; only what's shown/deletable changed. Browser-verified against a seeded two-class backup: restricting an activity to one class removes it from the other class's list and from that class's Record-tab buttons, the "All classes" badge reflects the restriction, and the default Freeze activity's Delete button is gone in favor of the same lock note the other three built-ins use.
- **Header account dropdown: an "Admin Tools" link for admin/superadmin accounts.** Reaching `admin.html` (step 5's standalone superadmin tools page — activity overview, view-as, email export) previously meant knowing to type `/admin` by hand; there was no link to it anywhere inside the app itself. `renderHeaderAccountDropdown()` now shows an "Admin Tools" button, opening `/admin` in a new tab, whenever `data.account.role` is `"admin"` or `"superadmin"` — the same role check `app.html`'s own `#/admin/classes/{classId}/book` route already used (`loadAdminClassBook()`). Discovery-only: the real gate is unchanged and stays entirely server-side, per call, inside `admin.html` itself (`docs/Firebase_Step5_Superadmin_Tools_Design_Proposal.md`, "Access boundary") — this link can't grant access, it only saves an admin/superadmin from needing to remember the URL exists. New tab because `admin.html` is its own standalone page with its own Firebase sign-in, not a route inside this one. No data model change, no new field.
- **Students: a "Delete selected…" batch button, which also doubles as "delete a class."** A class here has never been a stored thing of its own — it's just the `group` label on each student (see `groups()`), so there was no dedicated "delete a class" control to add. Instead, the Students tab's existing batch bar (already home to "Assign to class…" and "Give points…", both of which operate on whatever's checked) gains a matching "Delete selected…" button: filter to a class with the existing class dropdown, tick "Select all shown," and delete. Reuses the exact same removal the per-row Delete button already does (drops the student from `data.students`, deletes their `data.scores` entry) — batched, with one confirm naming the count, the class(es) represented, and the total points about to be removed. Once every student in a class is gone, the class itself disappears from every class-picker in the app, since none of them read anything but the `group` values still in use. Browser-verified against seeded data: filtering to a class, selecting all, deleting, and confirming the class no longer appears in the class filter or the class selector.

- **Board: a 4th floating button (📋, stacked above the picker wheel) that opens a fullscreen, auto-fitting big-text display for homework or a reminder — something the whole room reads off a projector, not just the rebbi's screen.** Type directly into it and the font grows to fill the box as large as it can, live, shrinking back down as more is typed rather than ever overflowing or wrapping mid-word. Up to 4 boxes split one screen; fill a 4th and the **+** button opens a new swipeable screen instead, shown as dots along the bottom (touch swipe, arrow keys, or tap a dot — each dot carries its own delete ✕). Each box can be color-coded (blue/green/red, or a neutral that's automatically white on the chalkboard skin and black on the whiteboard skin — never black text on a dark board), reordered by dragging its grip handle, and bookmarked into a "Saved" drawer (slides out from the right edge) for reusing a recurring message — Siddur test reminders, a standing homework format — without retyping it. A skin toggle (bottom-left) switches between a green chalkboard and a dry-erase whiteboard look. Every box shows both a ✕ and a top-center **Clear**: Clear wipes just that box's text in place, color untouched; ✕ deletes the box outright (dropping the whole page too if that was its last one) — except when it's the very last box on the very last page, where there's nothing left to fall back to, so ✕ there does the same thing "Close board" does and leaves the board entirely, rather than clearing content a rebbi may still want (Clear is what wipes it now).
  - **New fields, all additive, no `DATA_VERSION` bump:** `data.boardPages` (array of pages, each a list of `{id,color,text}` boxes — always at least one page with at least one box), `data.boardShelf` (the reusable saved messages, independent of any page), `data.boardSkin` (`"dark"`/`"light"`).
  - **No external dependency.** The prototype this was built from pulled two Google Fonts; the shipped version uses only `var(--sans)`, the system stack `body` already loads — rule 3's no-external-runtime-deps holds, and the offline `file://` copy is unaffected.
  - **Browser-verified end to end** against a seeded local class: typing and auto-fit shrink/grow, the black/white skin toggle, adding a 2nd box (equal-height split, confirmed after fixing a `1fr`/`minmax(0,1fr)` grid-track gotcha where a `contenteditable` child's intrinsic size could out-rank its row's fair share), color swatches, the bookmark save/unsave toggle, and a full page reload confirming every field — text, color, box layout, bookmark state, and skin — survived through `save()`/`load()` untouched. No console errors on load or interaction.
