- **The 4 floating tool buttons (Points, Timer, Picker, Board) now lock into one collapsed + in the bottom-right corner instead of sitting on screen as 4 separate always-visible, individually-draggable circles.** Tap the **+** and all four fan out above it; tap one and it opens while the tray folds back down — the same close-on-select behavior any speed-dial menu has. Adopted specifically for Board: a fullscreen feature needs its launcher to stay put and unchanged regardless of what's open, not transform into a close control or compete with per-box content for the same corner of the screen — Board now has its own independent "Close board" pill, top-center, in line with the color-picker row. Settings' "Header Shortcuts" card is updated to match — one checkbox, one description, no more three-buttons-not-four staleness.
  - **Drag-to-reposition is gone.** `makeFabDraggable()`/`applyFabPositions()` are removed along with every call site; a simple `wireFabTool()` just wires a click. `data.fabPos` (the old per-teacher dropped position) is **not** removed — an existing field stays in the data model per rule 1 even once nothing reads or writes it — but it's now inert.
  - **Browser-verified:** tray expands/collapses, each of the 4 tools opens and auto-collapses the tray, the Settings checkbox correctly hides/shows the launcher (and force-collapses an open tray), no console errors.
- **Every rebbi, either tier, must now connect Google Drive before Settings → Account shows anything else, and sign-in jumps straight to Settings so they actually see it.** Real live sign-in testing this session (2026-08-18) confirmed tier-1's own Firestore write-sync path from a rebbi's own scans still doesn't exist (flagged when step 7 shipped) — so right now, for every account, Google Drive is the only real backstop against data loss, not a nice-to-have. `renderAccountSection()` gained a check (`!data.driveBackupEnabled && driveSupported()`) ahead of its normal signed-in view — it shows a "Connect Google Drive" screen instead, no skip. This is a Settings-tab gate, not an app-wide block: a rebbi who never opens Settings isn't stopped from scanning — a deliberately smaller scope than a true full-app overlay, chosen over the bigger build. `signinCompleteFromRedirect()` now also calls `router.go({tab:"settings"})` the moment an OAuth redirect lands (success or error), so the whole sign-in sequence — code entry, same-device adoption, this new Drive gate — actually lands somewhere a rebbi will see it, instead of leaving them on whatever tab was open before they signed in. `driveCompleteFromRedirect()` now re-renders the account section on both the success and error path (previously only touched the separate Settings Drive card), so a failed connect attempt from this gate lands back on the gate with the error visible, not silently on a card they may never have looked at. `signinAcceptDriveRestore()` also now stamps `driveBackupEnabled` on a successful Drive restore, since listing/fetching the file already proved Drive access — avoids immediately re-prompting someone who just used Drive to restore their class. Browser-verified via a temporary debug bridge (removed before commit): the router jump lands on `#/c/local/manage/settings`, the gate correctly replaces the normal signed-in view when `driveBackupEnabled` is false, and correctly falls through once it's true.
- **Backup & Restore: every card is now click-to-expand.** Follow-up to the redesign below — even grouped, a page of a dozen-plus always-expanded cards (each with several sentences of copy) still read as "too much." Every `.bk-card` is now a `<details>` element (the same collapsible pattern the two shelves already used) instead of a plain `<div>`: collapsed by default, showing just the title (and any status pill, e.g. "No internet needed") until clicked, matching the Emergency-recovery/Google-Sheets shelves' own look exactly. Applies uniformly, Danger zone included, for consistency — the destructive buttons still need their own click either way, this just adds one more before you get there. Pure markup change (`<div>`→`<details>`, `<h3>`→`<summary>`) plus a few lines of CSS for the summary styling; every element id and all JS wiring is untouched, since `.style.display` toggling on status/preview elements inside a card works the same whether the card is expanded or not. Browser-verified: the whole page now fits without scrolling in the common (signed-out) state, clicking a summary expands it and its buttons work, no console errors.

- **A persistent header account menu, plus "Connect to school" — backfilling a school code onto an account that skipped it.** Until now the only sign-in UI lived inside Settings → Account, so a rebbi had to know to go looking for it, and there was no way at all to add a school code after the fact once an account already existed with `schoolId:null` (`redeemCode` only ever processed a code on brand-new accounts). A new always-visible header button ("👤 Sign in" / "👤 {name}") opens a small dropdown — email, **Connect to school** (only shown while `schoolId` is still `null`), Sign out — independent of whatever tab is open, kept in sync by calling `renderHeaderAccountMenu()` from the same places `renderAccountSection()` already runs, plus once explicitly at boot (most page loads never open Settings, so nothing else would have refreshed it). **Server-side, `redeemCode` gained a real backfill path**: called again with a code on an existing `schoolId:null` account, it validates and redeems the code exactly like the fresh-signup path (same transaction, same `usedBy`/`maxUses`/`revoked` checks) but updates the existing account instead of creating a second one — and refuses outright (`already-exists`) if the account already has a `schoolId`, since connecting to a school is a one-way door here, never a silent reassignment; a non-`"school"`-type code is rejected too (`invalid-argument`), so this can't be used to silently no-op a beta code a second time. Three new emulator tests cover the backfill success case, the already-connected refusal, and the wrong-code-type refusal. **The trickiest client-side piece:** `signinState`'s Firebase ID token is deliberately never persisted (see its own comment), so a returning visit — which is exactly when someone would use this — has no live token to call `redeemCode` with at all. "Connect to school" checks for one first; if there isn't one, it stashes a `sessionStorage` flag and re-runs the same OAuth redirect the original sign-in uses (a real visible redirect, not a silent `prompt=none` one — this is a deliberate "connect my account" action, not a background refresh) to get a fresh token, then `signinContinueAfterAccount()` checks that flag first thing on the way back and prompts for the code immediately instead of falling into its normal offer/idle flow. The flag is cleared on every exit path (success, code-entry failure, the redirect itself erroring) so a stale flag can never fire on some later, unrelated sign-in. Browser-verified via a temporary debug bridge with a mocked `fetch`/`prompt`/`alert` (removed before commit): the dropdown renders and toggles correctly signed-out and signed-in, both the has-a-live-token and needs-a-fresh-token code paths send the right code to `redeemCode` and clear the pending flag correctly, and errors surface without corrupting local state.

- **Backup & Sheets → Backup & Restore: the settled 2026 redesign, finally built, plus Google Sheets marked legacy.** `docs/Menchmark_UI_Redesign_Summary.md` had a locked, approved spec for this exact page ("simplified to Backup & Restore" — grouped sections instead of a flat pile of a dozen-plus cards) that was never implemented; the live page had also grown several cards since that doc was written (Drive backup, folder backup, step 3b's restore-into-account) that only ever got bolted onto the same flat grid. This redesign follows that spec and folds the newer cards into its buckets: **Automatic** (Drive, folder backup, Install as app — set up once, runs by itself), **Manual backup & restore** (download/restore a backup file, restore into your signed-in account, the standalone offline copy), **Other exports** (standings CSV, clearly labeled "not a backup"), two collapsed shelves using the `<details>` pattern already established 3× elsewhere in this file — **Emergency recovery** (Log CSV restore, a lossy last resort) and, new, **Google Sheets sync** — and **Danger zone** isolated at the very bottom, unchanged in content from before. **Google Sheets is now explicitly marked legacy**, per Ben's direction: a "Legacy" pill on its collapsed shelf and a deprecation note inside pointing at Download backup file / Export standings (CSV) as the replacement — still fully functional for anyone already relying on it, nothing behind it removed or rewired, purely a framing change. Every element id is unchanged from the flat layout this replaces (`sheetUrl`, `sheetStatus`, `scriptCode`, `manifestCode`, `driveBackupCard`, `folderBackupCard`, `backupRestoreCard`, etc.) — this is a pure regrouping, no JS wiring touched. Browser-verified: every section renders in the right place, the Google Sheets shelf expands to show the deprecation note plus its own nested "First-time setup" shelf correctly, Danger zone still isolated at the bottom. **The rename is now complete everywhere, not just the page `<h2>`:** `TAB_LABELS.backup` (the subtab nav pill) and every other in-app "Backup & Sheets" reference — toast/status messages, the embedded user-guide's Help text, `setup.html`'s restore-failure messages — now read "Backup & Restore" too, so the nav pill and the page title agree again. Left as a plain page-title change, this would have silently reopened `docs/UI_Theory_Tab_Audit.md` finding #22 (nav label vs. page title disagreeing), which a prior PR (#301) had specifically fixed.

- **`quick-start.html` now points beta rebbeim at Google Sign-In as the recommended backup/sync path, with Google Sheets sync kept as a still-supported fallback.** Now that the Firebase rebuild's sign-in flow (`Run → Settings → Account`) is shipped, the guide's "Protect your data" section gained a new tip recommending it — one click, no Apps Script, no link to paste — and reworded the existing Google Sheets sync paragraph from "optional" to "still available," so nobody reads it as removed. The opening privacy line and the footer tagline were also updated: data no longer unconditionally "stays on your device," since signing in is now a real, live option. Sheets instructions were kept rather than deleted — this is a downgrade in prominence, not a removal.

- **Attendance: a visible "not yet marked" state, matching Homework's "not yet" badge.** In the default (absence-first) mode, an untouched student has always *counted* as present — that's the mode's whole point, and it hasn't changed — but until now he also *looked* exactly like a boy a rebbi had actively confirmed present, with no way to tell the two apart on screen. The Attendance tab's per-student row now shows a dimmed name and a "not yet marked" badge for anyone with no real timestamp yet (the same `ts:null` vs `ts:number` distinction `attDayTaken()` already used internally), and the tracked-item tile badge (shown when Attendance is armed on the Record tab / scan panel) now reads "unmarked" instead of "present" for the same students, exactly like Homework's badge already did. Look-only: nothing about what counts as present, what gets sent to Sheets, or what the Gradebook reads has changed — `attStatusOn()` (which still returns "present" for an untouched boy in absence-first mode) still drives every one of those. Presence-first mode's own existing "not yet taken" treatment is unaffected. No data model change.
- **Undo now reverses attendance, homework, the Bathroom Pass, and Tracker scans — not just points.** Previously `undoEntry()` had an explicit gate that refused to touch a tracked row at all ("the buttons are not drawn for these rows"), so "Undo last scan" and every ↩ button silently skipped past a mis-scanned attendance mark, homework check, pass use, or Tracker tap to whichever point scan came before it — the only fix was finding the right tab and correcting it by hand. Both paths now reverse a tracked scan at the store that wrote it: `setAttendance()`/`setHw()` restore the previous status when there was one, or delete the record outright when it was the first mark of the day (guarded so a write that's since moved on is never touched); `setPass()`/`trackerUndoOne()` reuse their tabs' own existing give-back/undo.
  - **The harder half:** most attendance/homework scans (a routine Present, a routine full-credit check) never write a History row at all — that's deliberate, so a full class marked present doesn't bury History under 25 rows nobody reads (see `trackedValueIsNotable()`). That design stays untouched; what's new is a one-slot-deep, unpersisted `lastTrackedScan` pointer, set right after any tracked scan, that "Undo last scan" checks against the newest reversible log row by timestamp — whichever is actually more recent is what gets reversed, so it still finds a routine mark that never reached History, and repeated clicks still step back through real history for point scans and logged tracked rows alike.
  - **Row-level ↩** now also works on any tracked History row that carries enough to reverse — a pass use, a Tracker tap, an attendance/homework exception — reusing the same reversal. A row written before this shipped, or the old pre-tracked-concept "Bathroom pass" rows, carries neither and stays exactly as un-undoable as it always was; Edit stays points-only (a tracked row has no point value to change).
  - **New fields, all additive, no `DATA_VERSION` bump:** attendance/homework day-records gained an `id`; tracked History rows gained `titem` (which store wrote it), and `src`/`tprev` are now populated for attendance and homework the same way the Tracker item already used `src`. Nothing existing is reshaped or renamed — an old save simply has rows that predate the new fields and reversal degrades gracefully to "not reversible," same as before this shipped.
  - **Browser-verified end to end** against a seeded local class: attendance's first-mark-clears-the-record case, its restore-to-previous-status case (both via "Undo last scan" and via the row's own ↩), homework's first-mark case, a Bathroom Pass use, and a Tracker count — all confirmed correct against `localStorage` after each undo. Confirmed no regression on ordinary point-scan undo (score, correction row, and the existing "-0" tracked-row gate's *reasoning* — not its blanket refusal — both hold).

- **Firebase rebuild, step 3b: the existing-cohort upload path — same-device adoption's conflict case, self-serve backup-upload restore, and Drive restore.** Implements the remaining scope of `docs/Firebase_Step3b_Existing_Cohort_Upload_Design_Proposal.md` (approved 2026-08-13) — the same-device adoption offer itself shipped earlier in the sign-in UI (PR #290), but three pieces its own checklist still listed as unbuilt were genuinely missing: the two-device conflict screen, the Settings self-serve restore mount, and Drive-list-and-pick. This is the gate the doc calls out explicitly — **step 8 (migrating the real beta cohort) cannot start until 3b is fully closed**, because signing in before it exists would either strand a rebbi's existing class or, worse, upload it with no overwrite protection.

  **All three flows are payload-sourcing in front of one function, per the design's central claim — no new Cloud Function surface.** Every path below ends in the same `provisionRebbi({mode:"backup", self:true, ...})` call, the same `importReceipts` verification, and the same overwrite-refusal gate; what changes is only where the JSON comes from and how much ceremony the offer earns.

  **Two-device conflict screen.** When `signinClaimLocalClass()`'s same-device-adoption write hits the server's existing overwrite-refusal gate (the account already has a class from another device), it used to just show a raw error. Now it recognizes the specific `already-exists` code (`callCloudFunction()` gained `err.code`, parsed from the callable protocol's `status` field) and shows two non-destructive choices instead: **"Use my account's class here"** (writes nothing — local data stays exactly where it is) or **"Add this device's data as a separate class"** (`asNewClass:true`, always a fresh classId, never a collision). Merging was deliberately never considered — two independently-scored logs have no correct automatic resolution, and a wrong one is invisible to a non-technical rebbi. Fixed a real (if minor) pre-existing bug while touching this code: a generic (non-conflict) claim failure used to leave `signinState.step` on `"idle"`, which silently hid the offer screen and its error/retry — it now correctly stays on `needsAdoptionChoice`.

  **Self-serve backup-upload restore, mounted in Settings → Backup & Sheets.** A new "Restore a class from a backup file" card, tier-1-gated (hidden until a signed-in school account exists, same pattern as the Drive card next to it) — for a new device, a recovery, or the `file://` cohort that same-device adoption can never detect since there's no local class to find. Chooses a file, normalizes it through the same `window.__exportNormalized()` bridge step 3's converter uses, previews the count, and on an `already-exists` conflict offers the same two safe choices as the sign-in screen (**Overwrite**, behind a `confirm()` since it's genuinely destructive to the account's existing class, or **Add as a separate class**) rather than failing with a bare error. Carries a compact copy of `tools/converter-core.js`'s `renderReceiptReport()` rather than a `<script src>` — app.html stays one file for every tier, and CLAUDE.md rule 3's Firebase-SDK exception doesn't cover this.

  **Drive-list-and-pick restore.** Reuses step 7's Drive engine (`driveApi()`, `driveWithRecovery()`, `driveFindOrCreateFolder()`) rather than inventing a second HTTP layer — lists the same `"Menchmark Backup"` folder tier-2 devices already write to, and offers to restore the newest file into Firestore now that the rebbi has a real tier-1 account. Checked once per session, only for a tier-1 account with no claimed class yet, and only *after* same-device adoption resolved with no local match at all — never as a fallback from a declined same-device offer, per the design's explicit ordering. Silent on any failure (no backup, no permission, offline): a rebbi with nothing in Drive is the overwhelmingly common case here, not an error worth surfacing on top of an otherwise-successful sign-in.

  **New field, additive:** `data.firebaseMigrated` — the `importReceipts` runId behind whichever claim succeeded, display-only (a light one-line confirmation plus a native `<details>` "See details" disclosure into the full receipt, per the design's "lighter confirmation... not the full diff grid" for same-device claims specifically), never read to gate anything. `load2fix()` backfill, no `DATA_VERSION` bump, `test-migration.html` kept in sync.

  **Browser-verified end to end**, all three flows, via a temporary debug bridge + a mocked `fetch()` standing in for the Cloud Function (this machine has no live Firebase project to sign into for real) — same-device adoption's happy path (receipt renders, both fields stamp correctly), the conflict screen's both choices, the manual-upload preview → confirm → conflict → overwrite/separate round trip, and the Drive-restore offer's copy. The debug bridge was removed before this commit; `grep -c "__DEBUG_STEP3B"` confirms zero remaining.

- **Dashboard: the activity buttons are now grouped into Give / Take / Record, and the armed pill tints and reads with what's actually armed.** The flat, uncapped button list is now three sections by the same tone the printed activity codes already use — points-earning activities under **Give**, deductions under **Take**, everything tracked (attendance, homework, tracker items, freeze) under **Record**. Each section caps at a sensible size (Give 8, Take 4, Record 6) and overflows into a **"＋N more"** button that opens a small picker; the armed activity is always shown as a real, highlighted button even if it would otherwise be in the overflow, so the band never looks like nothing is armed. The "NOW SCORING" pill and its kicker now read three ways instead of two: unchanged brass/"Now scoring" for points, red/unchanged for a deduction or the warning flash, and a new ice-blue tint with **"Now marking"** for attendance specifically or **"Now recording"** for any other tracked item — reusing the exact same attendance special-case the tile-modes change above already introduced, so the two stay consistent about what counts as "status" vs. "count." **One real behavior change, called out on purpose:** arming an activity now re-renders the whole button list (it previously only updated the pill and the `.selected` highlight) — necessary so the "always show the armed item" rule holds when arming happens from inside the overflow picker, and safe because, unlike the seat grid, this list carries no QR codes or scroll position a rebuild could disrupt. `giveAllBtn`/`freezeAllBtn` (Give to all / Pause all) are untouched — same elements, same behavior, verified live. No data model change — group caps are code constants, not a setting.
- **Firebase rebuild, step 7: the fragile-storage warning — plus the real Google Drive backup it needed, ported from a spike that had never shipped.** Implements `docs/Firebase_Step7_Fragile_Storage_Warning_Design_Proposal.md`, approved 2026-08-14, the last design-only step in the rebuild's build order. Building it surfaced that step 7's tier-2 branch assumed Google Drive was already wired into `app.html` ("the same 0b token-surface write that `driveBackupEnabled` devices already run automatically") — it wasn't. `spikes/drive-oauth-spike.html` (PR #243, 2026-08-09/10) proved the mechanism on its own standalone test page and was never ported into the real app. This PR is that port.

  **Google Drive backup, real and wired into Settings.** A new "Back up to Google Drive automatically" card (mirrors the existing folder-backup card's shape) — Connect once, and Menchmark writes a "Menchmark Backup" folder + `menchmark-backup.json` into the rebbi's own Drive, once per session automatically and on demand via "Back up now." Same non-negotiables the spike established: the OAuth access token lives in memory only, **never** persisted to `data` or `localStorage`; `login_hint` is sent on every request (the spike lost an hour to `interaction_required` failures before finding this was mandatory when a browser holds more than one Google account) — and unlike the spike, `app.html` already knows the rebbi's Google email from sign-in, so this needed no separate "who am I" round trip; no refresh token exists in a browser-only OAuth flow (Google requires a client secret and won't accept PKCE instead), so renewal rides the Google account's own session via a silent `prompt=none` request in a hidden iframe — proven working invisibly by the spike, and now actually shipped. `data.driveBackupEnabled` is the only new field (additive, `load2fix()` backfill, no `DATA_VERSION` bump) — the token/folder/file ids all stay in-memory, matching "did the rebbi opt in," nothing about the connection itself.

  **A third hash-fragment consumer, disambiguated by `state`, not by which params are present.** Drive's OAuth redirect (`response_type=token`) returns its response in `location.hash`, the same place step 4's router and the sign-in flow's own OAuth capture already live. `buildDriveAuthUrl()` prefixes `state` with `"drive."`; a new `parseDriveOAuthFragment()` (dependency-free, `.github/scripts/check-drive-oauth-fragment.js`, 9 new assertions) checks that prefix rather than param presence — the case that actually needs it is a **bare Drive consent-denial redirect** (`#error=...&state=drive.xyz`, no `access_token` at all), which is otherwise indistinguishable from a sign-in error redirect by param names alone. Runs first, before the sign-in flow's own `parseOAuthFragment()`, and clears the hash on a match the same way, so the sign-in parser correctly sees nothing left when a Drive response was actually what arrived.

  **Three real bugs found in browser testing, not caught by any static check — all in code that "looked right" on read-through:**
  1. **A silent `ReferenceError`, swallowed by an existing try/catch.** `_storagePersisted` (the cached `navigator.storage.persisted()` result `maybeShowBackupNudge()` reads) was declared with `var` inside `reportStorage()`'s own scope — invisible to `maybeShowBackupNudge()`, which lives in the same-looking-but-actually-separate `<script>` tag as the rest of the app. Referencing an undeclared variable across that boundary threw immediately, caught by `maybeShowBackupNudge()`'s own wrapping try/catch, so the entire unpersisted-storage warning silently never fired — no error surfaced anywhere.
  2. **An implicit global masquerading as a fix.** Moving the `var` declaration to the shared outer scope didn't work either: `reportStorage()` lives in a genuinely **separate top-level `<script>` tag/IIFE** from the main app (a pre-existing, deliberate split — "PWA: register the service worker... fully additive and self-contained") that isn't in strict mode. Its own bare `_storagePersisted = persisted` assignment silently created an *implicit global* on `window` instead of erroring — a third, different variable from either of the first two attempts. The actual fix: `window._storagePersisted`, written and read explicitly on both sides, the same bridge pattern this file already uses for `window._refreshTimerPanel`/`window.__exportNormalized`.
  3. **A synchronous throw escaping a promise chain's `.catch()`.** `driveWriteBackup()`'s "force the recovery path when there's no token yet" trick called `driveWithRecovery(function(){ throw ... })` — but `driveWithRecovery(fn)` calls `fn()` and chains `.catch()` onto its *return value*; a function that throws synchronously never returns one, so the throw escaped the whole chain as an uncaught exception on every single fresh page load (the token is never persisted, so this fired every time). Fixed by returning a rejected promise (`Promise.reject(...)`) instead of throwing.

  **Decision logic, matching the design doc exactly (once `_storagePersisted` is actually reachable):** `persisted === true` (or the API is missing, or not yet resolved) falls through entirely to the existing 7-day/`daysSinceBackup()` staleness logic, completely unchanged. `persisted === false` shows **now**, ignoring the 7-day floor, in a new red/urgent CSS variant (`#backupNudge.urgent`) — "This browser can wipe your data at any time, with no warning." When Drive is connected and online, "Save to Drive" appears as the primary action (forces the automatic write on demand, then `markBackedUp()`), with "Back up now" (the existing CSV/JSON download, always works offline) stepping down to secondary; offline or not-connected, only "Back up now" shows.

  **One deliberate deviation from the design doc's literal tier-1 text, flagged rather than silently followed.** The design's tier-1 branch suppresses this warning entirely ("nothing is at risk") on the strength of a live Firestore write-sync path from a rebbi's own scans — that path doesn't exist anywhere in the codebase, and building it is a materially larger, separate undertaking (real-time writes on every scan, not an occasional read) that this pass deliberately does not take on. Telling a signed-in rebbi his data is safe when the mechanism that would make that true isn't built would be exactly the false reassurance this project's own risk-calibration rules exist to prevent, so **every account sees the same unpersisted-storage warning, tier-1 included** — no special-cased reassurance branch was built. Re-enable it once real write-sync exists to back the claim.

  **Test coverage:** `.github/scripts/check-drive-oauth-fragment.js` (9 assertions, new, wired into CI) — same dependency-free Node-extraction pattern as `check-oauth-fragment.js`/`check-router-hash.js`, specifically covering the bare-error-redirect disambiguation case. Existing `check-oauth-fragment.js` (10/10) reconfirmed unaffected by the new capture running ahead of it. `migrateData()`/`load2fix()` sync confirmed against `test-migration.html` for the one new `driveBackupEnabled` field.

  **Managed-Chromebook verification still owed, unchanged from the design doc — this build does not satisfy it.** Three separate claims need Ben's own hardware: `navigator.storage.persisted()` actually reports `false` on the beta cohort's real devices (the load-bearing assumption the whole step rests on); the warning renders the right variant at the right trigger point across all three account states; and the recommended actions (`downloadBackupFile()` under managed-Chromebook download restrictions, the Drive OAuth popup under managed-profile extension/popup policies) actually work on that hardware. **Do not ship to beta rebbeim until that session runs and passes.**

- **Seating chart / class list: tiles now show one thing at a time instead of stacking a score and a status badge.** Arm a points activity and a tile is exactly what it's always been — name and points. Arm Attendance, Homework, the Bathroom Pass, or any Tracker item and the tile's tracked-item badge (already existed) now becomes the primary thing on the card, with points stepping aside to a small brass secondary figure rather than sitting beside the badge at full size — controlled by the existing **Points** toggle in ⚙ Display, whose meaning shifts slightly: it now means "also show points" rather than "always show points at full size." Attendance additionally gets a real status *chip* (rounded pill, colored) instead of plain text, and a third color — **Late is now amber**, distinct from Absent's red, where both used to render identically. Every other tracked item (Homework, Pass, Tracker counts) keeps its existing plain-text badge look; only attendance's short enumerated states read well as a pill. No data model change — reuses `data.seatShowPoints` as-is, no `DATA_VERSION` bump, no `load2fix()` backfill needed. Applies identically to the List view, the seating chart, and the full-screen pop-out, since all three already share one card builder (`fillSeatLiveCard()`). Also fixed a real bug found while browser-verifying this: arming a different activity uses a cheap in-place repaint (`refreshTrackedBadges()`, so a full rebuild doesn't nuke QR codes or scroll position mid-scan) that only ever touched the badge — the adjacent points figure was left showing its stale full-size style until the next full render. `refreshTrackedBadges()` now keeps the two in sync on every arm change, not just on reload.

- **Chazaroom: named, saved question sets.** The Questions tab gained a **Saved sets** card — save the current list under a name (a parsha, a unit, a test) so several can be prepared ahead of time and swapped in without retyping. Saving under a name that already exists overwrites that set in place rather than creating a duplicate; loading a set replaces the current list, confirming first if it would discard unsaved questions. Stored as full copies (a JSON round-trip on save, not a live reference), so editing the current list afterward can never silently mutate a saved set out from under itself. Persists in the existing save blob (`savedSets`, additive — old saves without it default to `[]`, no migration needed). Verified live end to end (bypassing `confirm()`, which freezes browser automation the same way a print dialog does): save, overwrite-by-name (count updates in place, no duplicate entry), two sets coexisting independently, load, and delete all behave correctly.

- **Chazaroom: the AI-prompt maker's CSV format had a stray, unexplained `"20"`.** Not just a typo — `rowsToQuestions()` only ever reads the *last* field of each row for the correct answer (`r[r.length-1]`), which is exactly why the extra field was silently harmless rather than breaking imports, but the format spec and both examples showed a bare `"20"` with zero explanation, indistinguishable from a mistake. Checked the surrounding documentation before touching it: this field is intentional — a Kahoot quiz-export CSV carries a per-question time-limit column, deliberately supported and ignored on import ("so a Kahoot export drops straight in," already stated in the Import questions card right above). The actual fix was narrower than "remove the field": labeled it properly in the format spec (`"<time limit in seconds>"` instead of a bare number) and told the AI plainly what it's for and that it's unused, rather than silently deleting Kahoot-export compatibility to chase the symptom.

- **Chazaroom: the camera now asks for 4K, and the room is scanned continuously while students are actually answering — not just once at the end.** The camera constraint and the detection canvas's own downscale cap both moved from 1080p to 4K (`ideal`, not `exact` — a camera that can't do 4K just gets its own closest match); the detection cap especially mattered, since capping the canvas at 1080p regardless of what the camera streamed was quietly throwing away the extra resolution before either detector ever saw it, making the 4K request pointless on its own. More real pixels across a printed wand/card is what actually helps a smaller or farther marker decode at all.
  **Separately, a real fairness gap**: `loadQuestion()` centers the camera "for the countdown" and it then sits **completely still** for the whole answer window — only sweeping once, briefly, at the very end (`scanPass`, at lock). A student outside that one fixed view had no real opportunity to register an answer until that single end-of-window pass, while anyone already centered got the whole countdown to be read. The lobby's own continuous, ping-ponging sweep (`sweepStart`/`sweepAdvance`) already existed but was gated to run only in `mode==="calib"` — `beginAnswerPhase()` now starts that same sweep, and `sweepAdvance`'s gate now also covers `mode==="game" && qphase==="answer"`, so the camera sweeps the room the whole time students are thinking, not just at the last second. `scanPass()` already halts any continuous sweep at its own start (pre-existing code, `sweep.on=false`) and takes over for one final thorough pass — that hand-off needed no changes and was confirmed working cleanly. **PTZ-only**: a fixed camera with no gimbal has no way to widen its coverage in software — the 4K capture is the only lever that helps there, by keeping off-centre seats legible at all even without panning.
  Verified against the real Insta360 Link 2 Pro hardware (not just the JS state): `sweep.on` flips true the instant answering begins, and the gimbal's actual reported pan value was traced moving through its computed stops (-20° → 60° → -60° → 20°) throughout a live answer window, then correctly settling as `scanPass()` took over exactly when the countdown naturally elapsed.

- **Chazaroom: camera thumbnail no longer covers the countdown, and the answer text reads from across the room.** The camera thumbnail was fixed at the board's top-right — exactly where the timer ring already lives — so it silently sat on top of the countdown. It's now docked just *under* the timer instead of anchored to the bottom-right corner: the bottom is where the pill bar and the action-button row both live, and their combined height isn't fixed (more joined students means more pill rows), so a bottom-anchored offset was one bigger class away from covering the buttons instead. The top-right corner's height never moves regardless of what's below it, which is what actually guarantees no overlap rather than just tuning a number that happened to work once. Trimmed the thumbnail down to just the video feed during play (the aim pad, pause row and stat counters belong to the lobby) so it's small enough to fit there cleanly. Verified with exact bounding-box math, not eyeballing a screenshot: zero overlap with the timer or the button row, checked again at a stress-tested 14-student class (two pill rows). Also bumped font sizes for back-of-room legibility — pill names 1.05rem → 1.6rem, tile answer text/letters ~40% larger (base and the class's own projector-window copy, which has its own separate style block and would otherwise have stayed small).

- **Chazaroom: "boy"/"boys"/"talmid"/"talmidim" replaced with "student"/"students" throughout** — every UI string, hint, confirm dialog, code comment and one internal function name (`nextBoy` → `nextStudent`). Pronouns ("he"/"his") were left alone; only the noun changed. Verified live (roster/card/wand setup text) and via a full source sweep — the sole remaining "boy"-shaped substring is an incidental match inside the base64-encoded WASM blob, not real text.

- **Chazaroom: full-screen board with a live pill bar — who's answered, who just changed, whose wand is tilted.** The game screen no longer splits its width with a side panel: the board owns the whole screen, the camera docks as a small corner thumbnail (still showing the scan overlay), and the stats card steps out of the way during play. Under the answer tiles sits a **pill bar** — one pill per joined student (never an absent one). A pill turns **green with a brief glow** when his answer lands, the **outline stays green** after the glow fades, and a **changed answer re-glows** (a same-letter re-scan deliberately doesn't — only news flashes). If his cube wand is angled enough that **two faces read at once**, his pill flashes **yellow** for a couple of seconds — "straighten your wand" — driven by a new tilt signal from the wand detector (two distinct faces of the same wand in one frame; a warning only, never an answer, and deliberately not gated behind the multi-frame confirm since a warning needs to feel immediate). Also fixed while wiring this: **a scan can no longer register as an answer before the choices are even on screen** — during the get-ready beat a student's card/wand still marks him present, but his answer only counts once the tiles are up, in per-student and anonymous mode alike. All states verified live by driving the real game logic: get-ready scans register nothing, answer→green+glow, same-letter re-scan after the glow window stays green without re-flashing, changed letter re-flashes, tilt→yellow, and the bar steps aside for the scoreboard/podium screens.

- **Firebase rebuild: the sign-in UI — `app.html` can now actually sign a rebbi in.** Implements `docs/Firebase_SignIn_UI_Design_Proposal.md`, approved 2026-08-16, closing the gap flagged during steps 3 and 4 (no sign-in UI existed anywhere, so their backend halves had nowhere to be called from). This is real end-user UI, browser-verified throughout — not just unit-tested plumbing.

  **The SDK-free-until-tier-1 mechanism (§2):** every sign-in starts with a plain Google OAuth redirect (`response_type=id_token`, no library, same code path the Drive spike already proved) — zero `<script>` tags load. The Google ID token is exchanged for a Firebase identity via a plain `fetch` to Firebase Auth's REST API, `redeemCode` is called over plain HTTP with a Bearer token (the documented callable-function envelope), and only once the resulting account's `schoolId` comes back set does the vendored SDK actually get injected — tier-2 (or anyone who signs in and turns out to be tier-2) never loads it, no matter how far into the flow they got.

  **A real bug found and fixed while implementing, not anticipated by the design doc:** Google's OAuth redirect returns its response in the URL *fragment* — the exact same place step 4's router lives (`#/c/{classId}/...}`). Left alone, the router's own boot-time hash read would either choke on the OAuth response or silently overwrite it with a default route before the sign-in code ever saw it. Fixed by capturing and clearing the OAuth fragment as the very first thing that runs in the entire IIFE, before `load()`, before the router, before anything else — verified end-to-end with a real fresh page load (not a same-document soft navigation, which masked the bug on the first attempt at testing it): the fragment is correctly captured, the hash is correctly replaced with the router's own canonical URL, and the error/success state renders correctly in Settings.

  **Two more real bugs caught by the test suites, not eyeballing:** `URLSearchParams` doesn't exist in a bare Node `vm` sandbox, so the first run of the new OAuth-fragment-parser test silently "passed" 5 false negatives as `null` before the sandbox was fixed to inject it. And a first draft of the same-device-adoption offer's copy reused `trackedAgoText()` (a badge-shaped formatter, `"last: 3d"`) inside a sentence, producing "last scan last: 3d" — replaced with a dedicated `signinRelativeDayText()` ("3 days ago").

  **Screen sequence** (§3, matching step 2's already-locked reads/writes exactly): Sign in with Google → (first time only) an optional code-entry screen → step 3b's same-device-adoption offer ("This device has a class — add it to your account?") when local data qualifies (present, non-trivial, recent within 30 days) → otherwise a first-run class-name + roster screen, reusing the same paste-a-roster convention `tools/admin-convert.html` already established. **`provisionRebbi` gained the `mode:"roster"` + `self:true` combination** the design doc flagged as a small necessary extension rather than assuming — a shared `rosterRowsToNormalized()` helper now backs both the admin-driven and self-serve roster paths, covered by 2 new emulator tests.

  **Three entry points, every one skippable (§1) — "never a wall" for the real never-online cohort:** a new step in `setup.html`'s wizard (a plain link to `app.html?signin=1`; `setup.html` has zero sign-in code of its own and doesn't need any — `app.html` notices the flag on boot and starts the real flow), a permanent Settings → Account card (privacy summary link sits *above* the button, per `docs/Account_Privacy_Note.md`'s required placement — that note itself isn't published yet, so this is an honest inline stand-in), and a one-time nudge banner reusing the exact `data.lastBackupAt`/`backupNudgeSince` pattern already shipped for backup staleness (`data.signinNudgeSeen`, permanent once dismissed or signed in, never a second nag system).

  **Three new emulator-verified test suites**, none of them mocks: `.github/scripts/check-oauth-fragment.js` (10 assertions, same dependency-free-extraction pattern as step 4's router-hash test — no second copy to drift), and a new `functions/httpCallable.test.js` (5 assertions) that verifies the *exact* wire protocol `app.html` actually speaks — raw HTTP with a Bearer token, not `onCall()`'s `.run()` test harness used everywhere else in this repo, which fabricates `request.auth` directly and would never have caught a header-format mistake. It also verifies the Google-credential exchange and the Firestore REST read via the Auth emulator's documented fake-IDP support, since those two REST shapes had no coverage at all before this.

  **What this unblocks:** step 3's self-serve restore mount now has a screen to live on (self-serve `mode:"backup"` was already built and tested); step 4's tier-guard PR (deliberately left unbuilt) now has a real signed-in session to test against; step 3b's same-device-adoption offer now has a UI to surface in.

- **Firebase rebuild, step 5: superadmin tools — a new standalone `admin.html`.** Implements `docs/Firebase_Step5_Superadmin_Tools_Design_Proposal.md`, approved 2026-08-14. A separate flat page (Design A+C over B per the doc — no router integration, no shared bundle with the rebbi-facing majority), loading the vendored tier-1 SDK independently and gated server-side on every single call, never by the page itself.

  **Activity overview:** an explicit "Load activity" button (not a live `onSnapshot` — a privileged cross-school read has no business being a standing listener) reads the new `activitySummary` collection once, then sorts/filters entirely client-side, default sort **last-active ascending** so whoever's gone quietest surfaces first. A rebbi with zero classes some days after being invited gets a **⚑ flag** in the name cell. The load itself is logged, same as everything else privileged on this page.

  **`activitySummary` — populated by real events, never a client write:** two Cloud Functions triggers plus one existing callable extended. `redeemCode` (step 2, unchanged in shape) already runs unconditionally on every sign-in — its early-return "account already exists" branch now also calls a new `stampSignIn()`, which stamps `lastActive`/`lastSignIn` on `activitySummary/{uid}` **and** `lastActive` directly on `accounts/{uid}` itself (so `exportEmails` below never has to join two collections for one CSV column), seeding `invitedAt` exactly once, at account creation. Two new Firestore v2 triggers cover the rest: `onClassContentWrite` (a wildcard over `classes/{classId}/**` — any owned-data write signals a live human, scan volume itself is noise) resolves the owning rebbi from the parent class doc and stamps `lastActive`/`lastWrite`; `onClassCreated` bumps `classCount`. **Only "create," never "delete"** — `firestore.rules` already sets `allow delete: if false` on `classes/{classId}` (archive, not delete), so a decrement trigger would be permanently dead code.

  **View-as, wired end to end:** click a row → a confirmation naming the rebbi and school (never a bare click — this wields a token mint) → `viewAs(targetUid)` (step 2, unchanged) → a **new browser tab**, never same-tab or an iframe, so `admin.html`'s privileged context never shares a DOM with a scoped rebbi session. **The handoff is `sessionStorage`, not a query string:** step 4's router only ever parses `location.hash` on boot, so `admin.html` writes `{token, viewAsExp, name, school, targetUid}` to a `menchmark_viewAsHandoff` key immediately before `window.open()` — same-origin `sessionStorage` is copied into a tab opened this way, which is the entire mechanism, no router change needed on either side. The opened tab's `app.html` gained `viewAsBootFromHandoff()`: reads-and-clears that key (captured-then-cleared, same discipline as the OAuth fragment capture from the sign-in UI work), then signs into a **second, separate `initializeApp(config, "viewAs")` instance** — deliberately never the tab's own default-instance auth, so a view-as token can never collide with whatever sign-in state this tab would otherwise set up. A fixed, document-level banner ("Viewing as {rebbi} — {school}") now shows across every tab switch for the session's duration; "Exit view-as" signs out only the `viewAs` instance and closes the tab, returning focus to the still-open `admin.html` tab rather than navigating anywhere.

  **Originally flagged as a known limitation in this same PR, now closed by step 6's read pipeline (see that entry below):** a successful view-as now lands the tab straight on the target's real Class Book — `viewAsBootFromHandoff()` calls the same `routerGoAdminClassBook(targetUid + "_1")` step 6 built, so the banner sits over a real, server-filtered read of the target's actual Firestore data rather than nothing. **What's still deliberately out of scope, and why:** the rebbi-facing tabs themselves (Scan, Standings, Rewards…) still assume local `data` throughout a 22,500-line file — making all of them tolerant of a remote, read-only, foreign data source is a different and much larger undertaking than "add a read pipeline," and isn't what either step 5 or step 6 needed to deliver. View-as shows the Class Book for real; it does not (yet) show the full app the way the rebbi sees it.

  **Email export:** one button, one server-side Cloud Function (`exportEmails`), one flat scan of `accounts` — `name,email,school,role,lastActive` as CSV, no filters, no preview, no scheduling, matching the design doc's "export the list, don't build a mailer" exactly. `lastActive` comes straight off `accounts/{uid}` (via `stampSignIn()` above), never a join against `activitySummary`.

  **A new `auditLog` collection, deliberately separate from step 2's existing `viewAsLog`** rather than folding the two together — this doesn't touch already-shipped step 2 code, and `admin.html` can read both for a combined view if one is ever wanted. Covers the three events the design doc calls out: `activityLoad`, `emailExport`, and — the one that matters most — `unauthorizedAccessAttempt`, logged by a new `checkAccess()` callable that `admin.html` calls **unconditionally**, right after auth resolves and before the client-side role check ever decides whether to render anything. That ordering is the whole point: a curious non-superadmin loading this page gets logged even though the UI never shows them a thing.

  **Test coverage, all new:** `firestore.rules.test.js` gains 9 assertions filling a real pre-existing gap — `activitySummary`/`viewAsLog` had rules but no tests at all before this pass, `auditLog` is new. `functions/superadmin.js` (a new module, pure-ish helpers taking `db` explicitly, same pattern as `classWriter.js`) gets its own `superadmin.test.js` (9 assertions against the Firestore emulator directly — no Cloud Function wrapper needed since these aren't `onCall`). `functions/adminAccess.test.js` (10 assertions) exercises the three new `onCall` functions plus `redeemCode`'s new activity stamp via the same `.run({data,auth})` convenience harness `provisionRebbi.test.js` already established. **Not run locally this pass** — this machine has no Java runtime, which the Firestore/Auth emulators require; CI's `ubuntu-latest` ships a JDK and is what actually executes all three suites (this matches how every prior emulator-based suite in this rebuild has been verified when local Java wasn't available).

- **Firebase rebuild, step 6: admin's Class Book — plus the read pipeline neither step 5 nor step 6 had.** Implements `docs/Firebase_Step6_Admin_Gradebook_View_Design_Proposal.md`, approved 2026-08-14. Building this surfaced a structural gap bigger than step 6 itself: **nothing anywhere in the rebuild could read a class's real Firestore content into `app.html` at all** — step 5's view-as opened a correctly-authenticated tab with nothing real to show, and no design doc had ever assigned "build the actual read side" to a step. This PR builds it, once, and both consumers use it.

  **The `book` field and its rules, mechanical and additive:** `trackedItems.book: 'class'|'teacher'`, set once at write time by `functions/classWriter.js`'s new `classifyBook()` — the exact same three name-match predicates `app.html`'s already-shipped `trackedActIdsStamped()` uses (`"Attendance"`, `"Bathroom Pass"`, `"Homework Checked"` → `'class'`; everything else → `'teacher'`), not new inference logic, per the design doc's explicit instruction to reuse rather than reinvent. `firestore.rules` gains `itemBook()`/`itemVisible()` and rewrites `trackedItems`/`trackedEntries`' read rules to `isOwner() || isSuperadmin() || (isAdminOf(schoolId) && itemVisible())`, implemented exactly per the design doc's literal rule block — which, like `canWriteClassContent()` already does, deliberately omits `isViewingAs()`; flagging that rather than silently adding it back in, since a superadmin's own `isSuperadmin()` escape hatch already covers support access and extending view-as into these two subcollections was never in the approved spec.

  **`fetchClassFromFirestore(classId, idToken)` — the read pipeline, in `app.html`, reused by both consumers.** Mirrors `classWriter.js`'s write shape in reverse: reads the class doc plus `students`/`trackedItems`/`trackedEntries` (a new paginated `firestoreListCollection()` helper, same typed-field unwrap `firestoreFieldsToObject()` already established for `firestoreGetAccount()`), reshapes into the exact `students`/`trackedItems`/`trackedData` shapes the rebbi's own Gradebook already trusts. Read-only — never calls `save()`, never touches local `data`.

  **The Admin Class Book UI reuses the real Gradebook rendering code, not a second implementation.** `gbBuildTable()`/`gbBuildCodeMaps()`/`gbDatesFor()`/`gbBuildInitials()`/`gbBuildLegend()` were already fully parameterized (never read global `data`) — confirmed by grepping each function body before touching anything, not assumed. Only `gbCollect()` read `data.trackedData` directly; it gained one additive, backward-compatible parameter (`trackedDataOverride`, defaulting to the exact same global lookup — the one existing call site is untouched) so the new admin route can feed it a fetched, remote class's data instead. The `editable` argument `gbBuildTable()` already had for its own print-page rendering is exactly "read-only mode" — the admin route calls it with that argument omitted, so this is genuinely the same table an item's real column visibility badge (`🔓 shared`, non-dismissible, on every `book:'teacher'` column the rules let through) added on top, matching the design's "never hover-only or icon-only" requirement.

  **Routing:** a new `#/admin/classes/{classId}/book` hash, outside step 4's `#/c/{classId}/{group}/{tab}` grammar entirely — the design doc's own 2026-08-14 consistency-pass note flagged that step 4's router never specified an `#/admin/` namespace or a role guard. Checked first in `routerApplyRoute()` and boot's own deep-link block via a small dedicated regex, rather than teaching `routeParse()` a second, unrelated hash shape. `routerGoAdminClassBook()` is its own `pushState` (a real nav action, its own history entry), and the route hides the rebbi's own group/subtab nav and the global scan bar while active (`body.admin-class-book-active`) — undone by `activateTab()`, the normal path back into any real tab — matching "not a tab in `TAB_GROUPS`, the rebbi's own nav never shows it" literally, not just functionally.

  **Deliberately minimal entry point, flagged rather than silently shipped as complete:** the design doc's own UI spec calls for "an admin-only Schools list → class row → View Gradebook" — a real cross-class browser. That's genuinely a second, separate UI surface (a Firestore structured query by `schoolId`, a list view, its own scoping), not part of "the read pipeline." This pass ships a plain classId-entry field instead — real, functional, fully gated and tested end to end, just not browsable yet. A follow-on, not an oversight.

  **Browser-verified:** the rebbi's own Gradebook (real seeded data, `#/c/local/manage/gradebook`) renders identically after `gbCollect()`'s parameter addition — confirms the existing call site truly is untouched. The new route renders standalone (nav correctly hidden, confirmed via computed styles after ruling out a false negative from browser HTTP caching serving a stale script — cache-busted to confirm), refuses non-admin/superadmin accounts with a plain message, and — for a seeded admin account — correctly attempts the real Firestore read and fails closed with a clean "no class found" message against this machine's real `menchmark-backend` project (no live class exists at that id to fetch yet, but the network call itself, the auth-instance selection, and the error handling are all real, not mocked). Zero console errors throughout. Full render-with-real-data (the table itself, the shared-badge, the whole-book banner) needs a real classId in the live project to verify past this point — same "needs Ben's deployed backend" limitation every step since sign-in has carried.

  **Test coverage, all new:** `firestore.rules.test.js` gains 8 assertions distinguishing `book:'class'` vs. `'teacher'` vs. the whole-book `sharedWithAdmin` override, on both `trackedItems` and `trackedEntries`, plus a write-still-owner-only check. `functions/provisionRebbi.test.js` gains one integration assertion proving `classifyBook()`'s effect through a real `provisionRebbi` call (not a unit test of the classifier alone) — Attendance/Bathroom Pass/Homework Checked land `'class'`, a custom item lands `'teacher'`. **Not run locally** — same no-Java-runtime constraint as step 5; CI's `ubuntu-latest` job runs them for real.

- **Firebase rebuild, step 3: the converter tool's engine, plus the vendored SDK.** Implements `docs/Firebase_Step3_Converter_Tool_Design_Proposal.md`. `provisionRebbi` (from step 2) now dispatches on `mode`: `"admin-invite"` (unchanged), `"roster"` (paste-a-class, no backup involved), and `"backup"` (a full normalized `data` blob — either admin-driven for someone else, or self-serve via `self:true`, which skips the admin-role check entirely and targets only the caller's own account). `functions/classWriter.js` turns a normalized blob into class/student/trackedItem/trackedEntry/activity/log writes, chunked under Firestore's 500-op batch limit, then re-reads everything it wrote and stores a `classes/{id}/importReceipts/{runId}` — count parity, a 5-student spot check, and a `nameSplitFlags` count for names that couldn't be split into first/last. Refuses to silently overwrite an existing non-empty class unless `force:true`; self-serve also accepts `asNewClass:true` to restore alongside an existing class instead. **Honest scope note, not an oversight:** this covers the collections the design doc actually specifies plus activities/log/scores (shapes confirmed directly against `app.html`) — `prizeLedger` (raffle/auction/store history), seating, and Settings are deliberately NOT yet wired in, exactly matching the design doc's own "out of scope to enumerate here" for that list. A new `functions/provisionRebbi.test.js` runs the real exported Cloud Function (via `onCall()`'s `.run()`, no mocks) against the Firestore+Auth emulators — 8 assertions covering the overwrite gate, idempotent rerun (zero new docs), `asNewClass`, the admin/self-serve permission split, and all three modes — folded into the same CI job as step 2's rules tests. The Firebase SDK is vendored for real now (`vendor/firebase/*-compat.js`, pinned to 10.14.1, downloaded once from Google's own CDN and committed — never CDN-loaded at runtime): deliberately **not** added to `sw.js`'s install-time `PRECACHE` list, since that array is fetched unconditionally for every install and rule 3's amendment requires the tier-2 majority to re-download nothing extra — the existing cache-first fetch handler picks it up the first time a tier-1 browser actually requests it instead. `app.html` gains one small, surgical addition: `window.__exportNormalized()` plus a same-origin `postMessage` listener, so a backup blob is *always* normalized through app.html's own `migrateData()`/`load2fix()` (save-and-restore the live tab's real `data` in a `finally`, so calling it never leaves a tab pointed at someone else's uploaded backup) — the converter can never drift from the shape app.html itself trusts. `tools/converter-core.js` (shared logic) and `tools/admin-convert.html` (the superadmin UI: roster-only, backup-upload with a preview-before-write step, and sequential bulk invite with a per-row status table) round out the tool. **CLAUDE.md rule 3 amended a second time (2026-08-16, Ben's explicit sign-off via chat):** the vendored SDK may now also load in `tools/admin-convert.html` and step 5's `admin.html` — both new, superadmin-only, standalone files the original 2026-08-12 amendment didn't already cover — never a third file without asking again. **Deliberately deferred, and why:** the self-serve restore mount inside `app.html`'s own tier-1 Settings screen is NOT built this pass — `app.html` currently has zero sign-in/tier detection of any kind (no Firebase Auth, no way to know a `uid`), because that's step 4's job, not yet built. The backend half (`provisionRebbi({mode:"backup", self:true})`) is built and tested and waiting for it; building an ad-hoc sign-in flow now just to hang a button on would conflict with step 4's own locked routing design. **One doc inconsistency worth a second look:** the design doc describes the writes as "commit-whole-or-write-nothing... structurally impossible" to partially fail — true for any class small enough to fit Firestore's 500-op batch cap, but a real class with a few thousand `trackedEntries` (the log cap alone is 5000 per this file) does not fit one batch, so `commitChunked()` sequences multiple batches instead. The verification harness's post-write count-parity check is what actually catches a crash-mid-run here, not raw single-batch atomicity — flagged in code rather than left to quietly not match the stronger claim.

- **Firebase rebuild, step 2: auth + security rules, backend only — no app.html changes.** Implements `docs/Firebase_Step2_Auth_Rules_Design_Proposal.md` against the locked step-1 data model. `firestore.rules` enforces the full tier-gating matrix (owner/superadmin/admin-with-sharedWithAdmin/view-as, each with the exact write restrictions the design doc specifies — admin can only ever flip `archived`/`sharedWithAdmin`, never touch roster or gradebook content; `accounts` and `codes.usedBy` are Cloud-Function-only, never client-writable). Three callables in `functions/`: `redeemCode` (first-sign-in account bootstrap for both Path A and Path B, code or no-code), `provisionRebbi` (admin self-provisioning — the school is always derived server-side from the caller's own account, never trusted from the request), and `viewAs` (mints a short-lived, read-only, logged custom-claim token for "view as this rebbi," per the design's no-impersonation approach). A new `firestore.rules.test.js` is the Firestore-emulator analogue of `test-migration.html` — it seeds fixtures and asserts the full (actor, target, operation) → allow/deny matrix from the design doc's verification harness (23 assertions, all passing), wired into CI as a new `firestore-rules` job in `.github/workflows/validate.yml`. A root `package.json`/`package-lock.json` hold the test-only devDependencies (`@firebase/rules-unit-testing`, `firebase-tools`) — `app.html` itself gains no dependency and stays exactly as it is; see the file's own description field. **Two things this PR does NOT do, both deliberately out of scope for step 2:** it doesn't touch `app.html` at all (sign-in UI is step 4's job per the design doc's own "what this unblocks" section) and it doesn't deploy anything to the live `menchmark-backend` Firebase project — `firebase deploy` needs an authenticated `firebase login`, which is Ben's action.

- **Firebase rebuild, step 4: the app now has a real, working back button, deep links, and every button that jumps tabs leaves a trail.** Implements all 5 slices of `docs/Firebase_Step4_Routing_Design_Proposal.md`'s incremental migration path — this step is done.

  **PR1 (inert router module):** `routeParse()`/`routeBuild()` (hash shape `#/c/{classId}/{group}/{tab}[/{subtab}]`, using the persisted internal tab/group keys verbatim, never the R-labels) plus `router.go()`/`router.applyRoute()`, added with zero call sites — no behavior change of any kind. A real bug turned up while writing the test: an early version of `routeParse()` filtered out ALL empty hash segments before reading them positionally, so a hash with a missing classId (`#/c//scan/scan`) silently shifted every later segment left by one instead of being rejected — fixed to only strip the leading/trailing-slash artifacts, never interior empties, and caught by `.github/scripts/check-router-hash.js`'s 17-assertion round-trip suite (now in CI). That test extracts and runs the **exact function block from app.html** under Node rather than keeping a second copy — `routeParse`/`routeBuild` were written dependency-free specifically so this works, unlike `test-migration.html`'s `migrateData()`/`load2fix()` duplicates, which need a second copy because they depend on the full app context.

  **PR2 (back-button wiring, single implicit class):** the two group/subtab click handlers now call `router.go({classId: LOCAL_CLASS_ID, tab})` instead of `activateTab()` directly — `LOCAL_CLASS_ID` is a hardcoded placeholder standing in for real multi-class resolution, which is PR4's job. `router.go()` now renders immediately after pushing history state (pushState fires no event on its own), and a new `popstate` listener calls the same `router.applyRoute()` for the browser's own Back/Forward buttons — one render path for both. **Every other indirect `activateTab()` caller** (print buttons, the contest badge, the seating-chart pop-out close, etc.) is deliberately untouched — PR5 sweeps those opportunistically, matching the design doc's own "one extra line per call site" minimal-disruption choice. Browser-verified end to end: clicked Record → Recognize → Reward, pressed Back twice (correctly undid to Recognize, then Record), pressed Forward (correctly redid to Recognize), clicked a subtab (Trends) and confirmed its hash — zero console errors throughout.

  **PR3 (deep-link cold start):** boot now reads `location.hash` instead of always cold-booting to Dashboard — a bookmarked or shared link like `#/c/local/rewards/store` lands directly on that tab. A missing hash gets the canonical URL written in place via `history.replaceState` (never `pushState` — a fresh boot shouldn't leave a bogus "no hash" entry behind it for Back to return to). The existing `?tab=printSeats` query-param deep link (the setup wizard's own "go straight to the print sheet" handoff) is preserved untouched and now correctly informs the canonical hash it writes (`#/c/local/manage/printSeats`, not a hardcoded `scan/scan`) rather than the two mechanisms silently fighting over the URL. **No guard/hydrate phase yet** — that's PR4, below. Browser-verified: fresh boot with no hash (canonical hash appears, `history.length` confirms no extra entry was pushed), a direct deep link to Reward → Store (lands correctly), a full reload mid-session on Recognize → Leader Board (preserves the tab), and the `?tab=printSeats` path (still works, canonical hash reflects the real landing tab) — zero console errors across all four.

  **PR4 (tier-aware route guard) is now built too**, ahead of the sign-in UI it was originally waiting on. It doesn't need a real signed-in session to be *correct*, only to ever exercise its tier-1 branch — so it ships live, with a genuine fail-closed check that already protects against a bad or hand-edited classId, rather than staying dormant code with nothing calling it. `CLASS_INDEX` is the "small local class index" the design doc describes as cached from `accounts/{uid}` on login; since that login doesn't exist in this branch (step 2's sign-in work is a separate, unmerged PR), it's seeded with only the one class this app has ever had, permanently tier-2 (`schoolId:null`) — an index, not a hardcode, so nothing here needs to change once accounts/classes populate it for real. `routeGuardClass()` implements the design doc's 6 rules exactly (unknown classId fails closed; `schoolId:null` is tier-2 and always allowed; `schoolId` set is tier-1 and requires the signed-in uid to be the owner or an admin-share) and is written dependency-free like PR1's `routeParse`/`routeBuild`, so `.github/scripts/check-route-guard.js` extracts and runs the exact block from `app.html` under Node (9 assertions, now in CI) rather than keeping a second copy. `currentAuthUid()` reads `data.account` if present — a no-op today, forward-compatible with step 2's shape once that PR merges. A failed guard renders a new `view-routeError` section ("Can't open that class" / "Back to Dashboard") instead of a blank screen or a silent redirect, and rewrites the hash via `replaceState` so a bad deep link never leaves a bogus history entry Back could return to — both the runtime guard (`router.applyRoute()`, every popstate/`router.go()`) and boot's own deep-link check now go through one shared `routerGuardAndRender()` so the two can't drift. Browser-verified: normal boot and Record→Recognize→Back all unaffected, and a hand-typed `#/c/xyz-unknown/scan/scan` correctly shows the error screen, whose button correctly returns to `#/c/local/scan/scan` — zero console errors throughout.

  **PR5 (opportunistic audit of remaining indirect `activateTab()` callers):** every user-click that jumps tabs now goes through `router.go()` — the contest badge/three Standings-banner contest buttons and all four print buttons (`printSeatsBtn`, `goPrintPointsBtn`, `printStudentsBtn`, `printActivitiesBtn`). **Two sites deliberately still call `activateTab()` directly, each flagged in place with a `PR5 audit` comment** rather than converted: `syncNavAfterVisibilityChange()`'s redirect (fires when a tab disappears out from under the rebbi via a Settings toggle — corrective, not something he clicked, so it shouldn't create a history entry Back could "undo" into a hidden tab) and `closeSeatsPopout()`'s return to Dashboard (the seating-chart pop-out never actually changes `activeTabName` while open, so this is UI chrome collapsing back, not a real tab switch — routing it would push a same-hash history entry that undoes nothing visible). Both match the design doc's own "non-nav-shaped internal state" carve-out. Browser-verified: clicked `printSeatsBtn` (correct hash, Back undid it) and `startContestBtn` (correct hash, Contest tab rendered, Back undid it) — zero console errors.

- **Chazaroom: the game now runs itself end to end, and three real UX bugs are fixed.** Previously every phase past the answer countdown needed a rebbi click — reveal, scoreboard, next question all sat waiting for "Next question" to be pressed. Now **Auto-reveal at 0 defaults on** and, after a reveal or a scoreboard, a 5-second pause (`scheduleAutoAdvance`) walks the game on to the next step by itself — verified end to end with a live trace: a 3-question game ran from the lobby straight through to the podium with zero clicks after the first two simulated scans, landing cleanly on "Game over" with no looping or skipped questions. Every manual button (Lock/Reveal/Next) still works exactly as before and simply preempts whatever the timer would have done — the auto-advance is scoped to the exact phase and question it was scheduled for, so a rebbi who advances by hand can never have a stale timer fire later and silently skip a question.
  - **Only students who actually joined the lobby count as "in" the game.** `calibSeen` (set only while scanning in the lobby) already existed for the lobby's own display, but nothing downstream read it — a student absent today who therefore never joined still counted toward "of the whole roster answered" and showed up as "missing" everywhere. A new `joinedRoster()` helper now backs every in-question denominator and list (the answered count, the lift-modal's list, the reveal's "didn't answer" line). Scoring and the final leaderboard/podium deliberately still read the full roster — a scoreboard listing everyone, even at 0 points, is normal; it's specifically the *"who's expected to answer this question"* assumption that was wrong.
  - **A live, named "who's answered" list is now visible throughout the question**, not just in a popup after time's up. The response panel (right column) gained a roster-chip grid mirroring the exact chips already used in the lobby and the (rebbi-hidden) lift-modal — green once that student's card or wand lands, built from `joinedRoster()` so an absent student is never shown as expected to answer.
  - **The "Lock answers" button stopped doing anything once the answer window had already closed** (whether it closed via the countdown or via Lock itself) — a second click just restarted the same 4-second capture wait with no visible change, which read exactly as "the button doesn't work." Lock now disables the moment the window actually closes, and re-enables if the rebbi backs out via ✕/Escape (`dismissLift`) — reopening the window is exactly when locking it again becomes a valid action once more.
  - **The "Lift up your cards!" popup felt like it was still blocking the board.** Simplified to a calmer "⏱ Time's up — lift your cards!" banner (the bobbing-arrow animation is gone) on the rebbi's own screen; the class's own projector window is untouched, since a full-screen, unmissable prompt is exactly right for the students actually being asked to lift a card.

- **Chazaroom: a persistent countdown ring, always on screen, never blank — the first piece of a Claude Design redesign handoff being integrated in phases.** Previously the timer only existed inside the question board, so it vanished during the lobby and between-question beats, which is exactly when "what happens next and how long do I have" matters most for a rebbi glancing up from across the room. The ring now lives at the top-right of the whole play area (lobby included) and is the single place any phase's countdown renders: it shows `∞ / starts when you say` in the lobby, the get-ready and answer countdowns, `revealing in`/`scoreboard in`/`next question in` during the self-driving pauses between beats, `⏸ / paused` when the settings drawer is open, and hides itself only for the podium screen (which has nothing left to count down to). Every phase's own timer function (`startReady`, `startTimer`, `pauseTimer`/`resumeTimer`, `scheduleAutoAdvance`, `onCountdownEnd`, `showScoreboard`, `showPodium`, the manual Lock button) now only changes the underlying state (`tLeft`, `timerPaused`, a new `ringDeadline` timestamp); a single `updateRing()`, polling every 250ms, is the sole thing that ever writes to the ring's DOM — removing every direct `$("timer").textContent=...` write scattered across those functions, several of which were found to be stale leftovers from before this pass that would have silently fought the new renderer.
  Two real bugs surfaced and were fixed while wiring this up, not just cosmetic tuning: (1) the ring's `[hidden]` attribute was being silently overridden by its own `display:flex` rule — an ID selector always outranks the browser's `[hidden]{display:none}` — so the ring stayed visibly rendered on the podium screen even after JS correctly hid it; fixed the same way three other elements in this file already handle it (`#ringWrap[hidden]{display:none}`). (2) the "answers open in"/"starts when you say"-style labels wrap to two lines at this width, and the camera thumbnail that docks just below the ring was positioned assuming a single line, so a long label visibly overlapped the top of the camera box; fixed by reserving a fixed two-line height for the label and re-measuring the camera's dock offset against that worst case (112px → 140px), plus shortening one label ("manual — lock when ready" → "manual pacing") that was long enough to threaten a third line. Verified live end to end via a temporary debug hook (not shipped): lobby, get-ready, answer countdown, pause/resume, the capture-window countdown, the podium hand-off, and the manual Lock button all drive the ring to the correct text/label/visibility with zero console errors, and the label/camera overlap was confirmed fixed by screenshot before and after.
  **This is phase 1 of several** — the intro screen, a settings overlay reachable without leaving the board, and the new speed+streak scoring model from the same design handoff are still to come.

- **Chazaroom: the camera thumbnail no longer intermittently covers the question, and the "time's up" beat is now on the board instead of a popup — both from the redesign handoff, continuing phase 1.**
  The camera was docked at a *fixed* pixel offset from the top — sized for a typical question — so a longer question or taller "get ready" line pushed the tiles/pills further down while the camera stayed put, letting it drift onto content it was meant to clear ("blocks some questions", not all — exactly the intermittent symptom a fixed offset produces). `positionCamThumb()` now measures the pill row's actual on-screen position every render tick (or the button row's, in anonymous play, which has no pill row) and keeps the camera in line with it — the same *anchor to the stable side* fix already applied to the camera-vs-timer overlap earlier, now applied correctly to the camera-vs-question case it didn't originally cover. The pill bar itself now reserves 180px on its right so a wrapped second row can't run underneath the thumbnail either — a real overlap confirmed by screenshot with a 12-student stress test, fixed, and re-verified clean.
  **The "⏱ Time's up — lift your cards!" box is gone from the rebbi's own screen**, replaced with the redesign's `03-times-up.html` treatment: tiles dim (never hide — the class still reads them), a gold status line takes over ("⏱ Time's up — reading the room / Hold your wands up high and still…"), the camera thumbnail's border turns gold while it's actively reading, the answered line names who's still missing ("7 of 9 in — Berel S. still missing"), and a **↻ Pan the room** button appears in the action row only during this beat. The popup element itself (`#liftModal`) still exists and still runs exactly as before — it's now CSS-hidden on the rebbi's screen only, because it's still the data source the class's own projector window clones for its big full-screen "lift your cards!" takeover, which stays exactly that loud there on purpose (the redesign is explicit that this prompt is for the students, not the rebbi). Verified live via a temporary debug hook: tiles dim, status line and gold border appear together the instant capturing starts, the missing-students text is correct, and everything reverts cleanly once the window closes.
  **A real bug found while wiring the status line, not just cosmetic:** the line's own closing HTML comment was accidentally written as `*/` (a JS/CSS habit) instead of `-->`, which never closes an HTML comment — it silently swallowed the new status-line element plus `#tiles` and `#pillBar` into commented-out content until the *next* `-->` in the file closed it early. The whole question board briefly went missing from the DOM as a result; caught immediately via a `document.getElementById` check before it ever reached a screenshot, and it's exactly the class of bug `validate`'s CSS comment-balance check exists for, just in HTML instead of CSS — worth remembering that the same mistake is possible in markup, not only stylesheets.

- **Chazaroom: questions, the class list, and mid-game pacing are all directly editable now — no more delete-and-retype for a typo, and no more leaving the board to change how fast the game moves.**
  **Questions** — the Questions tab's existing "Add a question" form now doubles as an editor: an **Edit** button on any question in the list loads it back into the same six fields, re-labels the form ("Edit question 3" / "Save changes"), and saving replaces that question in place rather than adding a duplicate. **Cancel** (or just adding a fresh question afterward) returns to normal add mode. Verified live: editing a question updates it in place with no count change, cancel discards the in-progress edit cleanly.
  **Class list** — the read-only chip grid is now an editable table (`08-class-wands.html` from the design handoff): each student's **name is a plain inline text field** (click, retype, blur to save — no more re-pasting the whole roster for one correction), and each student's **wand/card number is a dropdown**. Changing a number to one already in use **swaps** the two students rather than doubling one wand up and orphaning another — exactly the handoff's rule ("the student who had it swaps to yours") — and each row's tagline reads "auto" or "changed — swapped with &lt;name&gt;" so a rebbi can see at a glance what's been reassigned. **Scores follow the student through a swap, not the number** — the two students' point totals swap along with their ids, so nobody's score can jump onto someone else's wand.
