A per-row **✕** removes just that one student (confirmed first) without touching anyone else, and a new **↩ Reset to list order** button un-swaps everything back to position-based numbering (also carrying scores correctly). "Remove class list" was renamed **"Make everyone anonymous"** to say in the button itself what it already did. Verified live end to end: rename, a real number swap (ids and scores both moved correctly, confirmed by reading the saved data directly), individual delete, and reset-to-order all behave correctly with zero console errors.
  **Mid-game settings** — a new **⚙** button (lobby and board both) opens a settings overlay matching the handoff's `07-settings.html`: **Answer time** and **Between beats** steppers (the latter — how long the reveal/scoreboard beats pause before auto-advancing — was previously a hardcoded 5 seconds, now a real adjustable, persisted setting), a **Runs itself** toggle (off = every beat past reveal/scoreboard waits for the gold button instead of a timer — the ring shows "manual pacing" to match), **Sweep the room** / **Second screen** toggles (reusing the exact existing state each already had, just reachable without leaving the board), a **Who's in the game** checklist (unchecking a student drops him from this game's pill bar/answer-counting without touching his roster entry, score, or name — checking him back in restores him immediately), and **Careful** actions (Restart game, Zero all points). Opening it pauses the game clock exactly like the setup drawer already does (`pauseTimer()`/`resumeTimer()`) — **which needed a real fix**, not just reuse: the between-beats auto-advance was a bare `setTimeout` with no per-tick pause check (unlike the answer/get-ready countdowns, which already checked `timerPaused` every second), so pausing during a reveal or scoreboard beat previously did nothing at all to that timer. `pauseTimer()` now cancels it outright and `resumeTimer()` reschedules it for whatever time was actually left, re-derived from `ringDeadline` so it can never drift from what the ring itself displays. Verified live: the overlay opens/closes correctly from both the lobby and the board, the ring shows "⏸ paused" immediately, unchecking a student live-drops him from the pill bar (10 → 9 pills, confirmed), and the between-beats seconds setting persists across reload.
  **Confirmed as a side effect of the fix above:** opening *any* drawer tab (Questions/Class/Answer cards) mid-game now genuinely holds the between-beats clock too, not just the new settings overlay — both go through the same `pauseTimer()`/`resumeTimer()`. Verified live: opened the Questions drawer during a "scoreboard in 2" countdown, waited 4 seconds (twice the original window) with the drawer open, and the game never advanced; closing it picked the countdown back up and it completed normally seconds later.

- **Chazaroom: the countdown ring was missing from the class's own projector window ("second screen").** A real regression from building the persistent ring: `#ringWrap` was deliberately pulled out of `#gameBoard`/`#calibPanel` so it could stay visible across every phase including the lobby, but the projector-window sync (`__chazPresent`) only ever cloned those two elements' `innerHTML` into the popup — it had no idea the ring existed anywhere else, so the class's own screen silently lost its countdown the moment the ring became a sibling instead of board content. Fixed the same way `#liftModal` already gets its own separate clone into `#mirrorLift`: a new `#mirrorRing` container in the popup, populated from `$("ringWrap").innerHTML` (empty when the source is hidden, matching every other mirrored element's convention) each sync tick, styled to the popup's own enlarged scale and fixed to its top-right corner (no `#playArea` to position against there). Verified via a synthetic detached document exercising the real `__chazPresent()` function directly: the ring's countdown/label clone correctly, and the container's own `hidden` state tracks the source ring's.

- **Chazaroom: the board's own size no longer changes from beat to beat — full-height, no page scroll, ever, regardless of class size or content.** Previously `#gameBoard`/`#calibPanel` were ordinary content-flow boxes that simply grew or shrank with whatever a phase happened to show (a long question, a big pill row, the scoreboard's five rows, the podium graphic), so the page's own height visibly changed beat to beat and could scroll. The whole `header → .wrap → #viewPlay → #playArea → #calibPanel`/`#gameBoard` chain is now one `flex-direction:column` stack sized off `100vh` (`:has(#playArea:not([hidden]))`-scoped, so it applies to the lobby too, not just `body.playing`'s game board — the ring's own earlier scoping bug), each board's fixed chrome (top bar, foot buttons, **tiles — the actual answer choices, which must never clip**) locked at `flex:0 0 auto`, and everything genuinely variable between them absorbing the difference: a `.tile` floor that can shrink from 120px down to 70px under real pressure, and — since even that has a limit for a large class — the pill row specifically gets its own internal scrollbar rather than clipping mid-row, so a huge class scrolls its own name strip while the question, tiles and buttons stay fully visible and fixed regardless.
  **Two real bugs found while building this, both the same class as the ring's own earlier one:** (1) `#playArea`'s actual parent is `<section id="viewPlay">`, not `.wrap` directly — without `#viewPlay` in the flex chain too, `flex:1 1 auto` on `#playArea` had nothing to shrink against and it silently rendered at full natural height regardless, overflowing its own parent (confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()`: 862px inside a 757px box) while `.wrap{overflow:hidden}` quietly clipped the difference instead of scrolling — which is what actually looked like "the board doesn't fit" before this was traced. (2) `#calibPanel`/`#gameBoard` getting `display:flex` unconditionally (needed so the visible one fills the fixed box) beat the browser's own `[hidden]{display:none}` on specificity — the exact bug the ring hit earlier — so both were rendering, permanently, regardless of `setMode()`; fixed with the same `:not([hidden])` pattern. **A third instance of that same bug surfaced by this work, pre-existing and unrelated to any of this session's own additions:** `.pillbar{display:flex}` has always had the exact same specificity as `[hidden]{display:none}`, so `renderPillBar()`'s own `pb.hidden=true` was never actually hiding it — invisible before (nothing gave the empty/stale box a reason to be noticed), but very visible once `#pillBar` got a real scrollbar, leaving a stale, scrollable roster strip sitting on the scoreboard and podium screens. Fixed the same way, `.pillbar[hidden]{display:none}`, matching the precedent (`.tiles[hidden],.podium[hidden]`) already in this file.
  Verified live at a deliberately extreme stress case (25 students, a 3-line question) that would have clipped or scrolled under the first attempt at this fix: zero page scroll at every beat (lobby, answering, capturing, reveal, scoreboard with a real second-round rank change, podium), all four answer tiles always fully visible, board-foot's buttons always fully visible, and the pill row's own scrollbar reachable when genuinely needed — confirmed via both `getBoundingClientRect()` measurements and screenshots at each beat, with zero console errors throughout.

- **Chazaroom: the scoreboard was noticeably smaller than everything else around it, and now shows who moved.** `ol.blb`'s rows sat at a flat 1.15rem in a 560px column — unchanged since before this session's font-legibility pass bumped pills and tiles considerably, so it read as small relative to the rest of the board. Sizing is now `clamp()`-based (matching the tile/pill scale) in a column that uses most of the board's width. **Each row now also carries a ▲/▼ movement indicator** (e.g. "▲2") showing how many places that student moved since the *previous* scoreboard, computed against the full roster's ranking (not just the top 5 shown, so someone jumping in from 6th+ still gets a correct delta) and reset whenever scores are — a fresh game shows no arrows on its first scoreboard, exactly as it should. Verified live: a scoreboard with random scores showed no arrows (nothing to compare against yet); changing two students' scores and showing it again correctly moved the leader to #1 with `▲1` and shifted everyone below by exactly one, in the right color on both the normal and gold first-place rows.

- **Chazaroom: the header logo now links back to the main site**, matching the "click the logo to go home" convention `quick-start.html` already uses (`<a href="/">`) — this was a standalone page with no way back to `menchmark.app` itself.

- **Chazaroom: ArUco cube wands are now part of the real game, not just the sandbox.** Each student gets one foam cube with four printed marker labels (A/B/C/D) on its faces and answers by turning his letter toward the camera — no more juggling four separate cards. The wand pipeline proven in `chazaroom-aruco-test.html` (js-aruco2, vendored offline the same byte-verified way as the QR engine, so the no-internet promise still holds) now runs **alongside** the QR detector on every camera pass: QR answer cards, QR control cards and wands all work in the same room with nothing to switch on. A detected wand speaks the exact same `HAD:`/`ANS:` language the QR cards already speak — wand #N is the Nth student on the class list — so the lobby join, seat-map learning, the privacy rule (the overlay names who was scanned, never which letter), the tally, scoring and the lift-cards list all work for wands without any of that logic changing. Because a 4×4 marker has no QR-style checksum behind it, wand reads get the sandbox's field-tested false-positive gate: only ids a printed wand can actually carry (within the class list, or the anonymous cap of 60), and the same marker seen on 3 consecutive passes before it counts, with the streak reset at every question and camera boundary. The Answer cards tab gained a **Cube wands** section that prints the label sheets directly — on Premium Label Supply PLS470 (2.5", 12/sheet) or PLS289 (2", 20/sheet), with the gap/spacing geometry taken from PLS's own templates. **Labels are numbered only, never a student's name** — "Student 1", "Student 2" and so on — so one physical wand set can be handed between classes and games without being tied to whoever held #7 today; his real name still appears live on the board (the lobby list, the in-question answer list, the privacy overlay) exactly when his wand is scanned, the same as it already does for QR cards, through the same shared detection seam with zero extra code. Each wand prints as **5 labels**: the four marker faces plus a **top-face label** — big wand number readable from above (the camera never sees the top, so it carries no marker), with edge letters pointing at the matching sides so the sticker is self-orienting. If the marker library ever fails to load, wands simply don't exist and the QR game is untouched. Printing (wands or cards) now also skips a false leading blank page: the live classroom screen (`#viewPlay`) had never been marked print-hidden, which the QR cards' loose page flow quietly absorbed but a forced page-break between wand sheets turned into an unmistakable wasted first page — `#viewPlay` is now hidden for print like the header and drawer chrome already were. **The label sheet's registration also needed a second pass**: it first tried a CSS named page (`@page` scoped to just the wand sheet) so QR/control cards could keep their old `.4in` print margin, but a real print showed the labels landing well below and to the right of where they should — the named page's `margin:0` wasn't actually overriding the document default, so the two margins stacked. That's an unproven, unreliably-supported browser feature with no way to print-test it in this environment, so it's gone: the whole document now prints under one plain zero-margin page, the same approach already field-tested working in `chazaroom-aruco-test.html`. The label sheet's own padding is the true, exact registration margin now. QR/control cards lose their old `.4in` outer buffer as a result and print closer to the physical edge — cosmetic only, since they're meant to be hand-cut, not registered against die-cuts. **Even at margin 0, a real print still landed low** — traced (via this machine's actual configured printer) to Chrome's print dialog itself: the CSS `@page` margin is only reliable for the "Save as PDF" destination, and for a real printer Chrome's own **Margins** dropdown governs instead, defaulting toward the print driver's own suggested margin (measured at a full 1in on the default printer here) rather than the page's requested 0. No code fixes a browser dialog setting, so the wand print note now says plainly to set **Margins → None** before printing — the hardware itself (measured at ~0.12in inset on all sides on the same printer) comfortably fits inside the label sheet's own 0.25in/0.375in margins once that's selected. **A real print with Margins set to None still landed ~0.2in low and slightly large** — some of that is ordinary mechanical paper-feed tolerance no software setting removes. Rather than chase that number exactly, PLS470's marker was shrunk (2.12in → 2in) and its interior cell padding grown (.06in → .09in) for forgiveness against the real die-cut edges, and its top margin pulled in (.25in → .15in, still above the measured ~0.117in hardware floor) to absorb the observed drift. These three numbers are a tuned safety margin, not the manufacturer's literal template values, and — like the marker-size iteration already recorded in `chazaroom-aruco-test.html`'s own commit history — may need one more round once printed again. **A real print then came out with thin horizontal lines through the markers specifically — not the surrounding text — and the camera couldn't read them.** js-aruco2's `generateSVG()` draws a marker as many separate same-color rects (a white background, a black border square, one white square per '1' bit) placed exactly edge-to-edge; a print rasterization pass can leave a faint anti-aliased gap at a shared edge between two same-color shapes, which laid out in a grid reads as lines — selectively hitting the fine marker pattern rather than the whole page is exactly what that looks like, as opposed to a printer hardware/ink problem, which would streak everything. `wandMarkerSVG()` now grows every rect by a small overlap (0.04 of the marker's 8-unit grid, symmetric on all sides) so adjacent same-color edges overlap instead of touching — confirmed against the real vendored detector two ways: the expanded geometry still decodes correctly across a spread of pixel-per-unit resolutions (including non-integer ones, where this kind of artifact is most likely), and — the direct test — injecting the exact same synthetic 1px seams into both versions broke detection completely without the fix (0/6 markers read) and left it fully intact with the fix applied (6/6). The same seam risk likely exists in `chazaroom-aruco-test.html`'s own marker rendering too; not touched here since it wasn't what was reported broken.

- **`quick-start.html` now covers the features that shipped since it was written.** The guide's core 6-step "zero to first scan" flow and its five-tabs-at-a-glance table were already accurate, but several real, shipped tabs were never explained anywhere on the page: Gradebook (un-hidden by #185), Tracker, Spinner, Brachos, the Freeze activity type, and Shulchani (coin) Mode. A new "A few more tools worth knowing about" section gives each a one-line pointer, the Daily Flow section gained a Homework and a Gradebook/report-card beat, and the Reward row in the tab table now mentions Coin Deposit. Deliberately left out: a link to the "Video A" onboarding video (`docs/NOW.md`'s open item) — no URL for it exists anywhere in the repo, so it isn't something this pass could add without guessing.

- **Double-scan detection: a soft "scanned a moment ago — again?" nudge.** Scan the same boy twice within 2 seconds — a stuck scanner, a card that didn't quite lift, a second rebbi scanning the same seat — and the confirmation toast now says so, folded right into the line it was already showing. Never a modal, never blocked: the second scan lands exactly as it always did, with its own points and its own log row: this only changes what the toast says, so the rebbi notices and can undo if it wasn't intentional. Deliberately doesn't care whether the two scans share an activity — a same-second duplicate is ambiguous by nature, and the nudge's job is to surface that, not to guess on the rebbi's behalf. An undo (or a correction) between the two scans clears it, since there's nothing left to nudge about. Adopted from the UI redesign review (`docs/Menchmark_UI_Redesign_Summary.md` §12, `docs/NOW.md` item 4); no new stored state — it reads the same `data.log` entry every scan already writes.

- **Gradebook: Homework cells are now editable too (#227, slice 2 of 4).** Same picker #248 built for Attendance — click a cell, pick a value, it writes through the legacy setter so the Homework tab and the Gradebook can never disagree. `setHw()` gained an optional `dateKey` parameter (mirroring `setAttendance()`'s own), so a correction from the grid can name a past day directly instead of only ever touching whatever day the Homework tab's own date picker happens to be showing. **The picker offers Checked / Not checked, not the full four-state cycle** — the Homework tab itself is still the old boolean store (unchecked/full credit are the only two states any writer can produce today), so "partial credit" and "no credit" stay display-only until #122 converts that tab to the real cycle. A blank cell is editable too, same as Attendance: "he never had his folder scanned" is as real a correction as "he did." Screen only — printed Gradebook pages are still built with editing off. **Homework and Attendance only, for now.** Passes and the Tracker column stay read-only in the grid — each of those stores still has to be taught to accept a correction for a past day before its column can be made editable.

- **The Help (?) modal's Scanner Setup tab now shows the actual Tera configuration codes, not just instructions to "scan the manual's barcode."** Tera gave written permission (2026-08-15) to reproduce their scanner's setup barcodes, so the barcode graphics themselves — cropped directly from Tera's own manual (`docs/Tera_UM1300_Manual.pdf`, now in this repo, sourced under `docs/tera-scanner-codes/`) rather than re-encoded from scratch — are now embedded inline next to the relevant explanation: Real-Time Mode/Storage Mode, Beeper Volume (High/Medium/Low/Off), Vibration On/Off, Battery Level, Power Timeout Timer (1/5/10/30 min, Never, Immediately), Clear Timestamp, USB-COM/2.4G Mode, and both flavors of factory reset (Wireless-only vs. full). A rebbi with a misbehaving scanner can now fix it from the app itself, scanning straight off the screen, instead of hunting through a printed manual. Support for markdown image syntax (`![alt](src)`) was added to the Help modal's `mdToHtml()` renderer to make this possible — it previously escaped all HTML, so an `<img>` tag pasted into the markdown source would have rendered as visible text rather than an image.

  **Also fixes a pre-existing bug this surfaced:** the Scanner Setup and About tabs rendered straight into `#helpBody`, which is `overflow:hidden` — only the User Guide tab wrapped its own content in a scrollable div, so anything past the visible height on the other two tabs was silently clipped with no way to scroll to it. Harmless when Scanner Setup was short text, but with the barcode images added its content now runs well past the fold, so the codes were invisible and unreachable. Both tabs now wrap their content in the same scrollable container the Guide tab already used.
- **A warning flash: a heads-up that reuses the minus flash and writes nothing — off by default, one Settings checkbox to turn on.** A new **⚠️ Warning** button sits next to Custom (add)/(subtract) on the Record tab — arm it, then tap or scan the boy it's for, same as any other activity. It shows the same red toast and red tile pulse a real demerit gets ("Not recorded — just a heads-up"), and stops there: no log entry, no save, no Sheet push, no score change, nothing added to any store. **Deliberately not the behavior ladder** — a recorded warning implies a count, a count implies rungs, and rungs are a bigger, separate feature (`docs/NOW.md` item 4). Because it isn't a real activity, it can't be armed by accident and left recording zero-point rows the way a stray Custom-0 arm can — `award()` recognizes it before any of its normal branches and returns immediately. **New Settings → Warning Flash card, unchecked by default** — a rebbi who's never used it shouldn't find a new button on his scan panel unasked; checking "Show the ⚠️ Warning button" is what puts it on the Record tab, and unchecking it hides it immediately, mid-session, no reload needed.

- **Undo now sits on the scan bar itself — one tap, everywhere.** Until now, the "reverse the last scan" button lived on the Dashboard (and on the History tab), which is precisely where you *aren't* while scanning: from the full-screen seating chart, or from any other tab, undoing a mis-scan meant opening the History dropdown and hunting for a small ↩ on the right row, mid-shiur. The scan bar — the strip that follows you to every scannable screen, including on top of the full-screen chart — now carries its own **↩ Undo** button, next to History. It reverses the most recent scan through the exact same path as the Dashboard button and the printed **Undo card** (which also still works, from wherever you're standing).

- **The app now remembers that you scan full-screen — and Settings lets you choose instead.** If you press **⛶ Full screen** on the seating chart and later close the browser from there, the next morning opens straight back into the full-screen chart — your scanning surface, ready to go — instead of starting you at the Dashboard every day. Pressing **✕ Close full screen** is what turns this off: the app takes that as "I'm done working this way" and goes back to opening on the Dashboard. Nothing to configure, and nothing changes for anyone who has never pressed ⛶. And if you'd rather not have the app guess, a new **Settings → How Record Opens** choice pins it: **Remember last** (the behavior above, the default), **Dashboard** (always start there), or **Full-screen chart** (always start in the room view). Changing it takes effect the next time the app opens — it never yanks the screen out from under you the moment you tap it.

- **Chazaroom: pausing the camera never restarts the quiz, and a "Live" badge follows you off the Play tab.** Previously, Stop Camera and Start Camera were a hard reset in disguise: Start's handler unconditionally wiped the game-so-far stats and dumped you back to the lobby, even if you'd only stopped to tweak the timer or fix a question — so the only way to "revert" a setting was to restart the whole game. Now `everStarted` tracks whether the camera has been started before this session; a later Start is a **resume** (new `resumeGame()`), picking back up at the same mode and question with scores intact, and only the very first Start of a session does the lobby/stats reset. A new **⏸ Pause** button sits right on the question board (`boardPauseBtn`) so pausing mid-question no longer requires detouring through the lobby first — the old detour was a trap, since the lobby's own "Start the game" button deliberately restarts at Q1 for its join/visibility check, which would have silently undone progress if reused as the resume path. A pill badge in the header (`#liveBadge`) shows "Live — Lobby" or "Live — Q*n* of *total*" whenever a game is running, on every tab, so hopping to Questions/Class/Answer cards to fix something never feels like it costs you the game — click it to jump straight back.

- **Chazaroom: a facelift for the header/nav**, borrowed from a UI mockup shown for comparison — a dark gradient command bar, a pill-shaped nav with a gold active tab, and a light crossfade when a section swaps in. Cosmetic only; no behavior changed beyond the pause/resume work above (same vanilla CSS, no new dependencies).

- **Chazaroom V3: the classroom is the primary screen.** The old five-tab structure treated Questions, Class and Answer cards as equal citizens with the live game — so setting anything up meant leaving the game screen entirely. Three changes, all with the same principle behind them: the rebbi should spend the whole shiur on the live screen, and everything else should come to *him*.

  **Setup moved into a slide-out drawer.** Questions, the class list and the printable cards now slide over the live screen from the right instead of replacing it — the running game stays visible (and running) behind a dimmed scrim, and ✕ / Esc / clicking outside drops straight back into it. Nothing inside those sections changed; only where they live. Printing the cards from inside the drawer still works.

  **The game screen is a two-column broadcast layout.** Left (~70%): the question board with a *large* camera feed directly beneath it — no more squinting at a 230px thumbnail to see whose scan just landed. Right (~30%): a live response panel — "*3 of 25 talmidim have answered — 22 to go*" with an A–D spread as horizontal bars. The spread honours the existing privacy rule (hidden until the reveal, so nobody copies the popular answer) with one deliberate exception: when the class has its own projector window, this screen is the rebbi's alone, and the spread runs live — seeing it early is the whole point of having his own screen. The mid-game leaderboard follows the same rule: off the class-facing screen (standings belong to the scoreboard beat), visible when presenting.

  **Three fixed classroom actions instead of one shape-shifting button:** *Show answers → / 🔒 Lock answers*, *Reveal answer*, *Next question* — always the same three, in the same place, with the gold one still saying what to press next. Lock is the "everyone commit" moment: it ends the answer window on the rebbi's schedule, triggering exactly the capture (lift popup + room scan) that the countdown reaching 0 triggers, so a class that answers fast never has to wait out the clock.

  Also fixed while in there: a `[hidden]` stat row could stay visible because the author `display:flex` beat the attribute (same quirk the tiles already carry a workaround for) — the anonymous-mode stats no longer show in roster mode.

  **The countdown holds while the setup drawer is open.** Both clocks — the get-ready beat and the answer countdown — freeze the moment a drawer opens and pick up at the same second when it closes, with the timer face showing ⏸ while held. Ducking out mid-question to fix a typo in the next question never costs the class answer time, and auto-reveal can never fire behind the drawer.

  **The "Lift up your cards!" popup no longer blocks the game.** It was a full-screen modal with a dark backdrop — which covered the camera feed and the response panel at precisely the moment the rebbi most needs them (during the capture, watching scans land). On the rebbi's screen it is now a compact floating banner over the board column: title, count, Pan-the-room / Reveal / auto-reveal, ✕ — with the tiles, the camera and the response panel all still visible and clickable around it. The class's own projector window keeps the full-screen takeover, because that's the screen the message is *for* — the boys need it big, the rebbi needs it out of the way. The who's-in chips are hidden on the rebbi's banner (the response panel already says who's in) but still render full-size on the projector.
- **A new rebbi's first app now shows the workflow, not the whole toolbox.** Finishing Quick Setup used to hand you the exact same app as a rebbi who's been running Menchmark for a year — Attendance, Homework, Tracker, Passes, Gradebook, Contest, Auction, Spinner and Trends all sitting in the nav on day one, next to the handful of tabs you actually touch to give your first points. From the UI design theory audit (`docs/UI_Theory_Tab_Audit.md`): those are real week-two-and-later tools, not day-one ones, and a brand-new rebbi can't tell which is which yet.

  **Now a fresh setup starts with those nine tabs hidden** — Attendance, Homework, Tracker, Passes, Gradebook, Contest, Auction, Spinner, and Trends — alongside the whole Review group, which already worked this way. What's left on day one is exactly the give-points-see-standings-redeem-in-store-run-a-raffle-print-your-sheet loop: Dashboard, History, Leader Board, Raffle, Store, Coin Deposit (if Shulchani), Students, Activities, Seating chart & print, Backup & Restore, and Settings.

  **Every hidden tab is one Settings checkbox away**, exactly like Review already was — this only changes what a *new* setup starts with. **Nothing changes for anyone already using Menchmark**: the write happens once, only inside the setup wizard's own completion step, and is skipped entirely when restoring from a backup or Sheet, so a rebbi's own saved nav choices are never touched or overruled.

- **`docs/Firebase_DataModel_Design_Proposal.md` — the Firebase rebuild's step 1 (data-model design session), approved 2026-08-13.** The rebuild's step 1 was unblocked once Phase 2d shipped and the rule-3 SDK-vendoring conflict was settled, but the class entity — the central open question, since "class" doesn't exist in today's data model at all — still needed designing. This doc covers the Firestore `classes` collection shape, how `group` maps to a plain `section` field on the student (not a second entity), the tier-1/tier-2 storage-seam adapter, and answers to the five remaining open questions: cutover detection (with a guard, added after review, against a cleared local shadow silently re-pushing empty data over a real class), converter tool scope + verification harness, step 4's routing scope, the Prize Ledger collection shape, and signup-code mechanics. **Approved and locked into `Firebase_Rebuild_Scope.md`'s open-questions list** (questions 2, 5, 6, 7, 9, 11) — step 1 of the rebuild is done, unblocking step 2 (auth + tiers + security rules).

- **`docs/Firebase_Step7_Fragile_Storage_Warning_Design_Proposal.md` — the Firebase rebuild's step 7 (fragile-storage warning design), approved 2026-08-14 — the last of the 8 build-order steps to be designed.** All 8 steps (0 through 7) now have an approved, locked design; step 8 (migrate the real beta testers) is the only step remaining, and it is pure execution against the 7 approved design docs, not a design step itself. A true retarget of the already-shipped backup-staleness nudge, per CLAUDE.md's explicit instruction — same `lastBackupAt`/`backupNudgeSince` fields, same 7-day logic, `markBackedUp()` just gains one new caller per tier (a throttled Firestore write-success signal for tier-1, the same Drive-write callsite step 3b already defined for tier-2). Unpersisted storage (`navigator.storage.persisted() === false`) fires immediately, bypassing the 7-day floor, but only escalates to visual urgency for tier-2 — a tier-1 rebbi with fresh Firestore sync sees nothing at all, since his data already has a durable copy elsewhere; only a tier-1 rebbi whose sync has *also* gone stale sees a warning. Copy and actions are tier-branched: tier-1 gets an informational dismiss-only message, tier-2 gets an urgent red variant with "Save to Drive" (primary, forces the existing automatic write on demand) and "Back up now" (works offline, when Drive can't). Flags a concrete three-part managed-Chromebook verification plan — Ben is the only person who can run it, and step 7 must not ship to beta rebbeim until it does, since an unverified `persisted()` assumption could mean either a warning that silently never fires or one whose fix-it buttons don't work on the device it targets. **Approved and locked into `Firebase_Rebuild_Scope.md`'s build order** — the retarget itself and the Chromebook verification session are still owed before step 8 can begin.

- **`docs/Firebase_Step6_Admin_Gradebook_View_Design_Proposal.md` — the Firebase rebuild's step 6 (admin's Class Book / Teacher's Book gradebook view design), approved 2026-08-14.** Closes an open follow-on flagged in step 2. Adds an item-level `trackedItems.book: 'class'|'teacher'` field — visibility resolves through the parent item, entries carry nothing — with attendance/homework/recognition items seeded `'class'` by default and everything else (any custom item a rebbi invents) seeded `'teacher'`, matching the scope doc's own "private notes stop being honest the moment they're watched by default" principle. The class-level `sharedWithAdmin` flag survives as a whole-book override rather than the only lever, so a rebbi has both a per-item "share this one thing" path and a separate, more prominent "share my whole Teacher's Book" path — never the same button, so one can't be mistaken for the other. Admin's Class Book view is one continuous table with no "N items hidden" counter (a visible private-item count would defeat the privacy promise); a shared Teacher's Book column always carries a non-dismissible "shared by [rebbi]" badge, never a hover-only or icon-only cue. Migration reuses the codebase's existing `trackedActIdsStamped`-style name-matching predicates to classify legacy items — no new inference logic, and no rebbi confirmation gate needed since nothing was "private from admin" before the admin role existed. Includes the concrete updated Firestore rule (`itemVisible()`), composing with — not replacing — step 2's `canRead()`/`isAdminOf()`/`isSuperadmin()`. **Approved and locked into `Firebase_Rebuild_Scope.md`'s build order** — step 6's design is done; the `book` field, UI, and sharing confirms are still to build.

- **`docs/Firebase_Step5_Superadmin_Tools_Design_Proposal.md` — the Firebase rebuild's step 5 (superadmin tools design), approved 2026-08-14.** Built on the approved step-2 "view as" mechanism and step-4 routing. A separate `admin.html` — not a route inside `app.html` — since this is the highest-privilege surface in the system (cross-school reads, token-minting, full-roster export) and keeping it out of the main file means the rebbi-facing majority never ships its code. An explicit "Load activity" button (not a live listener) reads an `activitySummary` doc per rebbi, populated by Cloud Function triggers on sign-in and any owned-data write (not scans specifically — sign-ins and writes signal a live human, scan volume is noise). View-as opens in a new browser tab, never same-tab or an iframe, so the privileged admin session and a scoped rebbi session never share a DOM. Email export is a single server-side Cloud Function returning a flat CSV (`name,email,school,role,lastActive`) with zero sending infrastructure, matching the locked "export the list, don't build a mailer" decision. Every privileged read — activity load, view-as mint, export, even a failed authorization attempt — writes to one consistent audit trail; the client-side role check is explicitly documented as defense-in-depth only, never the real gate. **Approved and locked into `Firebase_Rebuild_Scope.md`'s build order** — step 5's design is done; `admin.html`, its Cloud Functions, and the audit-log wiring are still to build.

- **`docs/Firebase_Step4_Routing_Design_Proposal.md` — the Firebase rebuild's step 4 (routing / back-button design), approved 2026-08-13.** Flagged in step 1 as the biggest hidden scope in the build order. Hash-based route shape (`#/c/{classId}/{group}/{tab}`, using the persisted internal tab keys verbatim, never the R-labels); `activateTab` stays a pure renderer while a single `router.go()` becomes the only thing that pushes history, so every class/group/tab switch is one undoable back-button entry; a two-phase `applyRoute()` (fail-closed tier guard before any fetch, then hydrate) satisfies step 1's deep-link-safety and tier-guard requirements; a three-primitive routing-only contract (`switchClass`/`currentClassId`/`listSwitchTargets`) for step 6's still-unbuilt class-switcher UI to build against; concrete re-anchoring of 2d's armed-item state and Phase 3's floating Points panel from a single global dataset to per-`classId` state; and a 5-PR incremental migration path (router module inert → back-button wiring on today's single class → deep-link cold start → dormant tier guard → opportunistic audit) so core navigation isn't rewritten in one big-bang change. **Approved and locked into `Firebase_Rebuild_Scope.md`'s build order** — step 4's design is done; the router module and its 5 PRs are still to build.

- **`docs/Firebase_Step3b_Existing_Cohort_Upload_Design_Proposal.md` — the Firebase rebuild's step 3b (existing-cohort upload path design), approved 2026-08-13.** The hard gate before any beta rebbi is asked to sign in. Same-device adoption, Drive restore, and self-serve backup upload all collapse into one payload-sourcing pattern in front of step 3's already-approved `provisionRebbi`/`restoreOwnClass` Cloud Function and verification harness — no new server-side surface beyond a `data.firebaseClaimedBy` stamp (gates re-offering) and a two-button, non-destructive conflict screen for the rare case where a second device's local class disagrees with what's already in the account (never auto-merged, never silently overwritten or discarded — the overwrite-refusal gate already makes that structurally impossible). Same-device detection reads three signals from the already-loaded `data` object (present, non-trivial — real students *and* real scan history, not just a roster — and recent within 30 days) so a demo/sample/abandoned local copy never triggers a false offer. For tier 2 (no Firestore class to write into), "claiming" a device is a single `accounts/{uid}` field write plus a Drive-backup enable — explicitly not the same as tier 1's "your data now lives in the cloud," and the screen has to say so. **Approved and locked into `Firebase_Rebuild_Scope.md`'s build order** — step 3b's design is done; the detection/offer UI, conflict screen, and Drive-list-and-pick client code are still to build.

- **`docs/Firebase_Step3_Converter_Tool_Design_Proposal.md` — the Firebase rebuild's step 3 (converter tool design), approved 2026-08-13.** Built on the approved step-1/step-2 designs. Covers a two-thin-surfaces/one-core architecture (`tools/admin-convert.html` for superadmin bulk/backup, a Settings mount in `app.html` for self-serve restore, both calling one `converter-core.js` module), an in-browser `migrateData()`/`load2fix()` normalization bridge so the converter can never drift from the shape app.html itself trusts, one extended `provisionRebbi` Cloud Function covering admin-invite/roster/backup modes (never a second account-creation path), a server-side verification harness (count parity, per-student spot check, idempotence-rerun, a stored `importReceipts` doc) that gates local-copy retirement behind an explicit human click, a concrete throwaway-account proof plan (read-model, referential, and ledger-math checks beyond count parity), and a four-stage rollout (synthetic → full-flow → real-shape/fake-identity → one real opt-in rebbi) with go/no-go criteria before the real beta cohort is trusted to it. **Approved and locked into `Firebase_Rebuild_Scope.md`'s build order** — step 3's design is done; `converter-core.js`, the Cloud Function, and the admin/Settings UI are still to build.

- **`docs/Firebase_Step2_Auth_Rules_Design_Proposal.md` — the Firebase rebuild's step 2 (auth + tiers + security rules design), approved 2026-08-13.** Built on the approved step-1 data model. Covers the identity-to-rules mapping (an `accounts/{uid}` doc lookup, not custom claims, so tier changes and adoption take effect on the next request rather than after a token refresh), full Firestore security rules for every step-1 collection, both sign-in flows end to end, a Firebase-Emulator-based rules test suite as step 2's `test-migration.html` analogue (proving a rebbi's rules physically cannot read another class, before step 4's UI exists to click through it), a scoped/logged "view as this rebbi" mechanism that never moves a real credential, and admin self-provisioning routed through a Cloud Function that derives the school server-side rather than trusting a client-supplied value. **Approved and locked into `Firebase_Rebuild_Scope.md`'s build order** — step 2's design is done; the Cloud Functions, `firestore.rules` file, and CI wiring are still to build.

- **A production plan for a Pixar-style animated promo video** (`docs/Menchmark_Promo_Flow_Production.md`). Nothing in the app changes — this is a document for making the video in Google Flow (Veo 3.1): a 13-shot story of a rebbi whose classroom is out of control, whose sticker charts and candy jars fail him, and who finds calm with Menchmark. It carries the full character bible, per-shot director's prompts, and the Flow workflow that keeps the characters looking like themselves from clip to clip (reference-image "ingredients" for identity, last-frame chaining for framing). Two standing rules are baked in: the video never shows a mocked-up copy of the real app (the honesty rule from the onboarding-video scripts), and all copy follows the positioning doc — rebbeim, not teachers.

  **Updated after a live test in Flow:** there's already a real project with a built "Rabbi Meir" character and four Act I clips — use that character rather than starting over. The doc now documents Flow's native Characters panel (`@`-tag reference) and its built-in Agent coworker, and records one confirmed lesson from an actual generation: a two-beat prompt silently rendered only the first beat, which is the doc's "one action per clip" rule showing up in practice, not just in theory.

  **Corrected three more issues the live test surfaced:** the test render put a girl in the classroom, which is wrong for a boys' yeshiva — every crowd prompt now says "boys" explicitly rather than "kids," since Veo defaults to a mixed classroom otherwise. The scanner prop was a generic invented shape — it's now specified as the real device rebbeim carry, a Tera 1300 pocket scanner, without depicting its printed configuration barcodes (CLAUDE.md rule 7 still applies to those, not to the device's shape). And the heart shot (shot 9) had the boy already looking up when scanned — he now stays down inside his sefer, absorbed in learning, and only looks up after the chime.

- **Shulchani Mode: a Coin Deposit tab, for turning physical coins into a safe digital balance and back.** Until now a student holding real Shulchani coin tokens — from a reward, or handed out separately from a scan — had no way to get them off his desk and into the app. If he lost them, they were just gone; the app never knew they existed.

  **Rewards → Coin Deposit**, visible only in Shulchani Mode. Pick a direction (Deposit or Withdraw), pick a student — or scan his code while the tab is open to jump straight to his balance, exactly as the Store already does — and tap the coins he's handing you or receiving — one tile per denomination, smallest on the left up to the Darkon on the right, each with a −/count/+ stepper beneath it (tapping the coin itself also adds one) — building "2 Sela, 1 Dinar" the same way you'd count the physical pile on the desk. No typing. **All** (withdraw only) fills in his entire balance as its coin breakdown, **None** clears the count. Confirm, and it lands on his normal spendable balance, the same one Standings and the Store already use.

  **This is loss protection, not a bank account.** There is no separate "banked" pool, no interest, no savings goal — deposited coins are simply added to the balance he already has, the same way a Store purchase already subtracts from it. That's also why it's named "Coin Deposit" and not "Bank".

  **One tradeoff, made on purpose:** a deposit or withdrawal writes a single lump-sum entry, not the per-activity detail behind it — you'll see "Physical coin deposit" once, not a list of what earned those coins. A quiet note says so before you confirm. The entry is still fully visible in History, tagged with 🪙 so it reads differently from a normal scan.

  **Withdrawing is the same tool in the other direction**, and reintroduces the physical loss risk deposit exists to remove — the screen says so, but it isn't a blocking warning; a rebbi handing coins back to a student knows what he's doing.

  **What it deliberately does not touch:** Trends and lifetime-earned are unaffected, exactly like Store and Auction spending already work — spending never erases earning. Standings updates immediately, same as any other spend.

- **Hold every freeze at once when the whole room stops.** A freeze is a countdown, and until now it kept counting through anything — davening starting, an assembly, you being called out of the room. Three boys frozen while the class is not even sitting there means the consequence is served while nothing is happening, and it is over before anyone comes back.

  **Now a ❄️ Pause all button appears the moment anybody is frozen**, with the number of boys on it — next to "Give to all" on the Record tab, **and on the seating chart's own toolbar, including full screen**, popping in and out exactly the way the attendance ✓ button does, so it is there on the screen you actually scan from. One press holds every one of their clocks exactly where it is; the button becomes **▶ Resume all**, and one press starts them all again from the same time they stopped at. When the last freeze finishes the button takes itself away.

  **It only touches the class you are looking at**, exactly like "Give to all" — a boy in another shiur keeps counting down. Choose "All classes" in the top-right corner and it covers everyone.

  **If some are already paused and some are running, it pauses the ones still running** and leaves the others exactly as they are. Nothing is restarted and no time is added or lost, whichever way you press it.

  **This is the same pause the boy's own freeze box already had**, just applied to everybody at once — so a freeze paused this way is an ordinary paused freeze. Tapping that boy still opens his box, and Resume, Adjust and End freeze all work on him as usual.

- **You can now email the day's attendance to the office, from your own email.** Until now the only way attendance left Menchmark was into a Google Sheet — which is exactly right for a school that uses one, and no use at all to a school whose secretary simply wants an email every morning. A rebbi with no sheet connected pressed **Send Attendance** and nothing happened.

  On **Run → Attendance** there is now **✉ Email this day…** next to Send Attendance. It writes the message for you — the class, the date, the counts, and who was absent or late with the time he came in — and shows it to you before anything opens, so you can read it, add a line ("Dovid is at a bris"), and change it however you like.

  **It is sent by you, not by Menchmark.** Pressing **Open in my email app** hands the message to whatever you already use for email — Gmail, Outlook, Mail on an iPad — with the address and subject filled in. Menchmark does not send it, and never sees the address on any server: it is saved in your own browser, on your own computer, so the next day is one click. The office receives a normal email **from you**, which they can reply to.

  **Emailing is now the main button on the Attendance tab**, and the Google Sheet one says what it is: **Send to Google Sheets**. Nothing about the Sheet changed — it works exactly as before — it just no longer presents itself as *the* way to send attendance. The email is the one that works for every rebbi with nothing set up at all, which is why it is the button your thumb lands on. If no Sheet is connected, the tab now says so and points at the email instead of leaving you with a button that does nothing.

  **Every name carries the time it was marked**, not just the boys who came in late — with one line at the top saying so, since the same column means "when he walked in" under Late and "when you marked him" under Absent. It is what makes the email a record rather than a summary: the office can see the absences were taken at 9:04 and the boy who turned up was marked at 9:41.

  **Who was absent comes first, then who was late** — the two things an office actually has to do something about, at the top where they will be read. **＋ full list below** adds the whole class underneath with each boy's status, for a school that wants the complete roll on file; the absent and late boys appear there too, in their place in the list, so the bottom half is a roll and not a leftover. **Absent & late only** — just the top part — is the default, and whichever you pick is remembered.

  From the seating chart it opens as a **small window floating over the chart** rather than a new tab — so the chart is still there behind it, and closing the window puts you back exactly where you were instead of switching away from the class in front of you.

  **And you can send it without leaving the seating chart.** Arm Attendance, tap the boys who are away, press **✓ Mark the rest Present** — and an **✉ Email attendance** button appears next to it. One tap opens the email, already written, for today. It only appears once every boy has been accounted for, because half a roll arriving at an office is worse than none, and it disappears again by itself if somebody is left unmarked. This is the same message the Attendance tab writes, sent the same way — it saves the trip out of full screen, mid-shiur, to send the roll you have just finished taking.

  **And when a boy walks in after the email has gone, the next one is an update.** This is the ordinary case, not an edge case: you send the roll at 9:12, and at 9:40 the boy you marked absent walks in. Mark him late as usual — the button now reads **Email an update**, and pressing it writes a message that opens with **"Changed since the 9:12 AM email (1): Dovid Berkowitz — Late 9:40 AM"**, above everything else, because that is the only part the office hasn't already read. Its subject line ends in **— update**, so a secretary holding both messages can tell at a glance which one is current instead of comparing them line by line. The whole roll is rewritten underneath, so the update stands on its own even if the first email never arrived.

  Menchmark can only tell you it **opened** an email at 9:12, never that you sent it — pressing Send is yours, and it says so in those words. It does not touch your Google Sheet's own record of the day, which keeps working exactly as before.

  **It will not claim you took attendance when you didn't.** If nobody has been marked for the day, the email says so in as many words. If you use "I mark who's here" and some boys are still unmarked, they are listed under **Not marked** — never quietly counted as present. That is the same rule the Google Sheet send follows, for the same reason: once it leaves the building it is somebody's official record.

  **It records nothing and changes nothing.** Emailing a day does not mark it as sent to your Sheet, because Menchmark has no way of knowing whether you actually pressed Send in your email app. The Sheet, if you use one, works exactly as before.

  One limit worth knowing: email apps can only accept a message of a certain length this way. A very large class on **Full roll** can exceed it — if so, Menchmark refuses to open a half-message and tells you to press **Copy text** and paste it into a new email yourself.

- **The app now tells you when you are running an old copy of it.** Until now there was no way to find out. Two rebbeim could be using Menchmark side by side, months apart in version, and nothing on either screen would say so — including to whoever was trying to help them.

  **Help → About now shows your version number.** That alone answers the question that used to be unanswerable: "which Menchmark am I actually running?" If you ever report a problem, that number is the single most useful thing you can send.

  **And if a newer one is out, a yellow bar appears across the top.** What it offers depends on how you use Menchmark, because the two ways behave completely differently:

  - **If you use it from the web or installed it as an app**, it can fix itself. The bar offers **Reload**, and you are on the new version a second later. (Most of the time you will not even see the bar — this way of using Menchmark already updates itself quietly whenever you open it.)
  - **If you downloaded the file and open it from your own computer**, it *cannot* fix itself, and pretending otherwise would be the wrong thing to do. There is no way for a file sitting on your machine to change itself. So the bar says exactly that, and gives you a **Download the new copy** link instead.

  **It stays quiet unless it is certain.** No internet, school wifi blocking it, or anything at all unexpected in the answer, and it says nothing whatsoever. A bar that cried wolf about being out of date would teach you to ignore the one that matters. You can also dismiss it with the ✕; it will leave you alone until the next time you open the app, because until you actually update, the copy is still old.

  **One thing it honestly cannot do, so nobody is surprised by it later:** it cannot reach a copy that was downloaded *before* this change. That file has no idea any of this exists, and it never will. This makes every future downloaded copy able to speak up for itself — it does not reach back to the ones already out there. Those still have to be told by a person.
- **Menchmark can now back itself up to a folder, by itself, with no internet.** This is for the rebbi whose classroom computer never goes online — and it is the first automatic backup he has ever been able to have.

  Until now, backing up without internet meant *remembering* to press **Download backup file**. The Google Sheet needs internet. So a rebbi working offline had one copy of his whole year sitting in one browser on one computer, and the only thing standing between him and losing it was his own memory.

  **Now: choose a folder once, and Menchmark saves into it on its own** — every time you open the app, and again when you switch away from it if anything has changed. Pick a folder on the computer, or on a **USB stick you leave plugged in**, which gets the backup off the machine entirely. No internet, no Google account, nothing to remember, no button to press.

  **The one you can count on is the one when you open it**, and it is worth knowing why: opening always writes, and what it writes is everything the last session did. The save when you switch away is a bonus that narrows the gap — a browser closing a tab does not always leave enough time to finish writing a file, so that one is not promised. Between them, the folder is never more than one session behind, and there is a **Back up now** button on the card whenever you want to be certain this minute.

  Find it under **Run → Backup & Restore**, next to "Back up everything".

  **Each day gets its own file** (`menchmark-backup-2026-08-10.json`), so the folder becomes a history you can go back through, rather than one file that keeps writing over the only copy you have. A week of them is about the size of one photo.

  **It tells you when it cannot.** If the USB stick is unplugged, or the folder was moved or deleted, or your school's Chromebook does not allow it, Menchmark says so on that same card and goes back to reminding you to save a backup by hand. **A backup that quietly stops is worse than one that was never set up**, so this one is never silent.

  **It also quiets the backup reminder** — a folder backup is a real, restorable backup holding exactly what the Download button saves, and it restores the same way, through **Restore from a backup**.

  **Chrome and Edge only** (which includes Chromebooks). On other browsers the card simply does not appear, rather than offering something that cannot work.

- **The Gradebook can now fix attendance, right in the grid.** Until now the Gradebook was something you could only read. If you spotted a boy marked absent on Monday who had actually been there, you had to leave the grid, go to the Attendance tab, move its date picker back to Monday, find him in the list, and change it there — then come back to check it looked right.

  Now you click the cell. A small menu opens showing **Present, Late, Absent**, with his name and the day at the top so there is no doubt which square you are changing, and you pick the right one. The grid updates underneath you.

  **A blank square works too**, and that is the correction this is really for — a boy nobody marked on a day the rest of the class was taken. Click his empty square for that day and mark him.

  **It changes attendance in exactly one place, so everything agrees.** The Attendance tab, the Gradebook, the History list and your Google Sheet are all fed by the same single write — and if that day had already been sent to your Sheet, the correction is sent on its own straight away, so you do not have to resend the whole day. There is no second copy of attendance to get out of step.

  **What it deliberately will not do:** it cannot turn a square back into "never marked". You can move a mark to the right status, but taking the roll back off a day is not something the grid will do — that needs its own careful thinking, and a button that quietly erased a record would be exactly the wrong thing here.

  **Printing is unchanged.** The printed Gradebook pages are built by the same code with editing switched off, so nothing on paper gains a cursor or a highlight.

  **Attendance only, for now.** Homework, Passes and the Tracker columns stay read-only in the grid — each of those stores has to be taught to accept a correction for a *past* day before its column can be made editable, and they are being done one at a time on purpose.

- **Settings: the Picker and the matching games now remember which way you like them.** Both of these already had a switch. Neither remembered it, so both started every session the same way regardless of how you actually work, and the first thing you did each morning was flip it back.

  **How the Picker opens.** The Picker chooses a boy at random two ways: the **Wheel**, which spins a wheel of names, and the **Seating chart**, which shows the class in their seats and flashes from desk to desk before landing — the "Flash & land" every boy can follow from where he is sitting. Settings now has which of the two it opens on. If you run the Picker off the chart so the class can watch it, it opens on the chart.

  **Matching games.** The two matching games in Review — the Pesukim/Mishnayos game and the Shorashim match — can be played by **dragging** a piece onto its line, or by **tapping** the piece and then tapping the line. Tapping is far easier on a Smart Board or a tablet, and easier for a boy who finds dragging fiddly. One setting sets how both games start.

  **These are defaults, not memory.** The switches on the Picker and on the games themselves still work exactly as before, and still change things for the rest of the session — they simply no longer decide what you get tomorrow morning. Change your mind in Settings and it applies right away as well as next time.

  **Nothing changes unless you change it.** Neither setting exists in any save made before this, so every app carries on opening on the Wheel and playing by dragging, exactly as it did — the settings are only there for whoever wants the other one.

- **Print: Activities now has the same choices the student labels have.** The activity menu could only ever print one thing: every code you use, once each. Now it carries the toolbar you already know from **Print → Students**, in the same order and with the same wording.

  - **Sections** — narrow the sheet to one of your own code sections. The sections are the ones you name and drag codes between under **Choose codes**, so "print just my deductions", or just the Coins strip, is now one sheet instead of the whole menu with the rest cut off by hand. The printed heading names the section as well as the class, so two sheets from one class are tellable apart at a glance.
  - **One code** — pick a single code instead of all of them.
  - **Copies each** — how many of each code to print.
  - **Fill a sheet (1 code)** — pick one code and this fills a whole sheet with it, exactly as "Fill a sheet (1 student)" does for a boy's label.

  The use this is really for: the codes that wear out. **Undo Last Scan**, and whichever activity you arm every single period, are the ones that end up taped to a desk, a folder and a clipboard — and getting three of them meant printing the entire menu three times and throwing most of it away.

  **Nothing about the normal sheet changed.** Leave the new boxes alone and you get exactly the menu you got before. Like the student labels, these choices are not remembered between visits — they are "what am I printing right now", not a setting, so you can never come back next month and quietly print a third of your codes without noticing.

- **"Mark the rest Present" is now on the class list too — including full screen.** Taking attendance off the seating chart is the natural way to do it: you arm **Attendance**, tap the two or three boys who are away, and everyone else is simply here. Saying so meant leaving the chart, dropping out of full screen and going to the Attendance tab to press one button. That button is now on the chart itself.

  It appears **only while Attendance is the armed activity** — the same moment each boy's card starts showing his present/absent badge. Arm something else and it goes away again, so there is no attendance button sitting on a points chart waiting to be pressed by mistake.

  **It does exactly what the Attendance tab's button does, because it is the same button underneath.** It fills in only the boys **nobody has marked yet** — anyone you already set to absent or late is left exactly as he is. If you mark who's *here* rather than who's away, it fills the leftovers as **Absent** and says so on the button, the same way the tab does.

  **It always means today.** The Attendance tab can be parked on last Tuesday; the seating chart has no day on screen, so this always writes today's date — the same rule a scanned attendance code already follows.

  It tells you what it did ("14 students"), and if the roll is already complete it says that instead of looking like a button that does nothing.

- **Voice notes are now switched on for everyone.** The 🎤 button next to "Undo last scan" lets you speak a note straight onto the scan you just made, instead of typing it. It arrived switched off so it could be used in a real classroom for a day first; that has happened, so it is on.

  **If you would rather not have it, turn it off in Settings and it stays off** — it will not switch itself back on afterwards.

  Two things worth knowing. **It is a per-device setting**, so turning it on or off on your tablet does not change your laptop. And **if you tried it during the first day and switched it off, it has come back on once** — the app cannot tell that apart from never having been offered it. Turning it off now will stick for good.

  Nothing new is stored. A spoken note is saved in the same place a typed note has always been saved, so it shows in History and reaches your Google Sheet exactly as before.
- **Give an activity to the whole class in one action.** Pick the activity you want — "Davening", "On time", whatever is armed — then press **👥 Give to all** next to it on the Record screen. Every boy in the class you have selected gets it at once, instead of you scanning twenty-five codes for something the whole class earned together.

  It is also **scannable**, because the whole workflow is built around a scanner: a **Give to All** code applies whatever activity is currently armed to the entire class. That code does **not** appear on your printed Activity Menu unless you tick it on yourself, under Choose codes. That is deliberate — every other code on that sheet affects one boy, and a mis-scan costs one point. This one affects everybody at once, so it should not turn up on a sheet by surprise.

  **It asks before it does anything**, showing the amount, the activity and how many boys it is about to reach.

  **If you have taken attendance, it offers to skip the boys marked absent** — it tells you how many and lets you decide, rather than quietly including or excluding them. Giving points to a boy who was not there is noticed by the class immediately; so is skipping a boy over a mark left from the morning. If nobody has taken attendance, it does not ask at all. Only boys you actually marked count — the ones filled in automatically are not treated as absent.

  **Every boy still gets his own line in History, exactly as if you had scanned him**, so the class's points, Trends and your Google Sheet all read the same as always. And because it is one action, it can be taken back in one action — see the undo note below.

  **Attendance, Homework, the Bathroom Pass and Tracker items are refused here on purpose**, with a note saying so. Those record a mark, not points, so "give to all" would have written a row of zeroes for every boy and looked as though it had marked the whole class. Marking a class present belongs on the Attendance tab, where it actually goes.

- **Scans that don't give points now show up in Scan history too — so you can write down why.** Freezing a boy, tapping +1 on the Tracker, giving out the bathroom pass, and marking a boy late or absent: until now none of those appeared under **Review → Scan history**, so there was nowhere to record what was behind them. Now they do, and every one takes a note — typed with 📝, or spoken with 🎤 if you have voice notes on.

  That was the whole point. "Absent" is a fact; "absent, third Tuesday in a row, spoke to his father" is the thing you actually want in front of you at parent-teacher night, and there was previously no place in the app to put it.

  **Attendance only puts the exceptions in the history — not the whole roll call.** The boys who were simply present don't each get a line; late and absent do, and so does a correction like changing a boy from absent back to present. So a morning gives you the two or three lines worth reading instead of twenty-five saying "present". Nothing is left out of your actual attendance record or the Gradebook — this is only about what the history page shows you.

  **Homework doesn't add lines yet, and that's deliberate.** On the Homework tab you tick the boys who *did* their homework — the boy who didn't is simply never ticked, so he'd leave no line at all. Filling the history with twenty "did it" lines while missing the one boy you'd want to note would be backwards. Once the Homework tab can record "handed in late" or "didn't do it" properly, those will appear here automatically.

  **A new filter beside the activity picker** lets you choose **Points + tracked** (the new default), **Points only** — which is exactly what the tab showed before this update — or **Tracked only**. Your choice is remembered. The activity picker beside it now also lists the tracked ones, so you can narrow to just "Attendance" or just "❄️ Freeze".

  **These rows have no ↩ Undo button, on purpose.** There are no points on them to take back, so an Undo there would have changed nothing at all while looking for all the world like it had un-marked the boy. To fix a mark, fix it where you made it — the Attendance tab, the Homework tab, Run → Passes — and the correction appears in the history as its own row. Tracker taps are the exception: undoing one on the Tracker tab crosses its row out here too, since that really is the same action.

  **Correcting an old day says so.** Fix Monday's attendance on Wednesday and the row reads "Wed 3:40pm (for 2026-08-03)", so a late correction never reads as something that happened today.

  **Nothing about your points history changed.** Tracked rows are counted against their own separate limit, so they can never push a points scan out of the history — a busy week costs you nothing you had before. They are also left out of "Most given" and out of a boy's points breakdown on Trends, both of which are about points and would only have been muddled by a row worth zero.

  **They stay out of your Google Sheet's Log tab too**, since attendance, homework and tracker taps already sync to their own tabs there and doubling them up would help nobody. The one exception is a row you write a note on — that one is sent across, so the note has somewhere to land.

- **The Gradebook is switched on.** One grid for everything you track: your boys down the side, Attendance / Homework / Bathroom Pass / anything you count across the top, with a total for each. Pick which items to show and over what dates, then print exactly what you see. It sits under **Run → Gradebook**.

  It was built a while ago and deliberately kept switched off, because it had nothing real to read — it could only show what a one-time conversion had put there, so it froze on that day and every day since looked blank. That is fixed: everything you record now reaches it, whether you scanned it or marked it by hand on one of the tabs.

  **Your history is already in it.** Every attendance day you have marked, and your whole Tracker history, were brought across the first time you opened the app after this update. You do not have to do anything, and nothing was moved or removed from the tabs you already use — they work exactly as before.

  **One thing worth knowing:** the **Bathroom Pass** column counts every pass ever used, with dates, while the Passes tab counts only the current week. After you press "Reset all passes", the tab starts again from zero and this column keeps the history. That is on purpose — a new week starting is not the same as last week never happening — and hovering the column heading says so.

  Homework fills in from the day you next mark it rather than reaching back, which matters to nobody in practice: the grid is arriving over the summer, when there is no homework behind it to reach back to.

  If you would rather not see the tab, you can switch it off in Settings, and it will stay off.

- **The timer can pop out into its own little window that stays on top of everything else.** Press **⧉** on the timer and it lifts off the page into a small window of its own — one you can drag onto a corner of the screen, or onto a second monitor, and which stays visible while you work in a different tab or a different program entirely. Stretch that window and the number grows with it, so it can be read from the back of the room.

  **The timer and the points scanner cannot both be popped out at once.** Your browser only allows one such window, so popping out one puts the other back on the page. That is the browser's rule, not ours, so rather than refuse we simply swap them over.

  It needs Chrome or Edge on a computer. On anything else the ⧉ button is simply not there, and the timer works exactly as it always has.

  **Everything on the timer keeps working while it is popped out**, including changing the snack length — see below.

- **You can now just type the time you want.** Tap the big number on the timer and type it: **12** for twelve minutes, **12:30**, **1:05:00**, or **90s**. Before this the only way to set a length was to nudge it with the +/− buttons, so twelve minutes took seven presses — and some lengths could not be reached at all, since the smallest step was ten seconds and 45 seconds simply was not a number the timer could be set to.

  **A row of one-tap lengths** — 1, 2, 3, 5, 10 and 15 minutes — sits under the number for the ones you reach for constantly.

  **The timer also remembers.** Whatever you type becomes the length it opens at next time, so if your shiur always starts with a twelve-minute chazarah, you type it once. It used to always open at five minutes with no way to change that. Nothing is lost if you never touch it: an existing timer keeps opening at five minutes exactly as before.

  **Setting the snack length works the same way.** Pressing ✎ next to the snack button now types straight into the timer instead of opening a separate pop-up box, and it accepts the same things — **7**, **7:30**, **450s**. It used to ask for minutes only, so seven and a half minutes had to be entered as "7.5". The old pop-up box also could not be used at all once the timer was popped out — it would have opened behind whatever program you had switched to, making the timer look frozen — so this is what lets the snack length be changed from the popped-out window like everything else.

- **The timer can now be a clock face, not just a number.** Three buttons across the top of the timer choose how it is drawn. **Nothing changes unless you press one** — it starts on Digits, which is exactly what the timer has always looked like.

  **Digits** — the number, as before.

  **Disc** — a coloured circle that shrinks as the time runs down. A boy can see how much is left from across the room without reading anything.

  **Clock** — a real clock face showing the actual time, with the time you have left **shaded across it**. If it is 1:10 and you set five minutes, the shaded block runs from where the minute hand is now to the 15 — so the answer on the board is not "5:00 left" but **"you have until 1:15."**

  That difference is the whole point of the Clock face. A number tells a boy how much time is left only if he already has a sense of how long five minutes is; a shaded clock face shows him *where the time ends*, and he can watch the hand travel into it. It is also the one that quietly teaches reading a clock, which not every boy in a Shiur Aleph can do yet.

  **The face has its numbers on it, and it tells you the time in words underneath** — "3:05 PM". With a timer running it also names the moment it ends: **"3:05 PM → until 3:09 PM"**. That line is the answer a boy actually needs, and it is there in words for the ones still learning to read a face.

  **When no timer is running, Clock is simply a clock on the wall of your screen** — which, popped out with ⧉, is exactly what it becomes.

  **Two smaller things it does on purpose.** The shaded block never runs past a full hour: beyond that it would lap the face and stop meaning anything, so the block fills and the number underneath tells you the real figure. And on the stopwatch (Count up) the Disc steps aside for the number, because a disc that shrinks needs a length to shrink from and a stopwatch has none — your choice is remembered, and the Disc comes straight back when you switch to Count down.

- **The timer turns red for the last thirty seconds, and you can pick the colour it uses before that.** Two small round buttons next to Digits/Disc/Clock choose **green** or **blue**. Whichever you pick, the timer switches to **red with thirty seconds to go**.

  That is the part worth having. A boy who cannot yet read the number still gets told to start wrapping up, and he gets told from across the room — no announcement needed, nothing interrupting the shiur.

  The colour carries across all three faces and the number itself, so the warning can never turn up on one and not another.
- **`chazaroom.html` — Chazaroom MVP, a standalone whole-class review game** (renamed from Hadroom). Not part of the Menchmark app and touches none of its data. The rebbi asks a multiple-choice question, every boy holds up a printed answer card, and the camera reads the whole room at once. Two modes, chosen automatically: paste a **class list** and it prints **per-student cards** (each coded to a boy's id + answer) and keeps **points with a leaderboard** — the camera records who answered what; leave the list empty and it falls back to **anonymous A/B/C/D** cards with a live letter tally. Questions are typed, **imported from a Kahoot-style CSV** (paste or file), or **drafted by any chatbot** via a copy-paste prompt (no proxy/account — the prompt even tells the AI to spread the correct answer across A/B/C/D instead of always landing on B). Prints its own cards (5" reads to ~22 ft, per the camera-test findings). The play screen is a real **Kahoot-style board** — big question, 2×2 coloured answer tiles with live counts, a countdown **timer that auto-reveals**, and a leaderboard — opening through a quick **calibration** check (boys hold up any card; each lights green so you can confirm the camera sees everyone before starting — and once a boy has been read he **stays green for the rest of that check** even after his card drops out of frame, so a gimbal sweep adds everyone up across the room instead of only counting whoever is in view at that instant; a brighter ring marks who's live right now). It can **sweep the room** with the Insta360 gimbal (via the same `ptz-bridge.ps1`), counting each boy once no matter how many pan stops catch his card — a toggle offered both in setup and on the in-game rebbi strip (the two stay in sync), so sweeping can be turned on or off mid-game without leaving the board; and the game master can run it **hands-free with QR control cards** (hold up Reveal / Next / **Pan** / Check / Start — Pan re-scans the room for stragglers). It is **anonymity-first**: the video overlay shows only *who* was scanned — the boy's name in a neutral box, never which letter he chose — and the per-answer tally stays hidden until the reveal so nobody can see or copy the popular answer. Each question runs a short **answer countdown** (10s); when it ends, a big **"Lift up your cards!"** popup appears with a live list of who has answered (filling in as the camera reads each boy), and only then does the camera sweep the room to read the raised cards — so boys commit first and the whole room is captured in one clean pass. Auto-reveal is **off by default** (the rebbi reveals when ready) — a toggle both in setup and right on the popup, which carries a **↻ Pan the room** button that re-scans (sweeping the gimbal again when the bridge is up, or re-opening the capture window on a fixed camera) so a boy who was missed still gets counted — additively, dropping no one — and can be dismissed (✕, Escape, or clicking outside) to go back to the board without revealing. The score panel carries two reset controls: **↺ Reset game** starts the quiz over from the first question (through the visibility check; points are kept), and **Reset scores** zeroes every boy's points (roster mode only) — both confirm first. Reuses the multi-QR detection proven in `hadroom-camera-test.html`. **It now works with no internet at all** — the QR library, the multi-QR detector and the detector's own WebAssembly engine are all inside the file, so a dropped wifi or a school network that blocks CDNs no longer means a game that simply cannot read a card (there is no native detector on a Windows laptop to fall back on, so this was the difference between working and dead, not fast and slow). The file is ~1.5 MB as a result, and asks the network for nothing but the optional camera bridge.
