When the PTZ bridge is up, the camera card also carries an **aim pad** — arrows to pan and tilt, +/− to zoom, ⌂ to recenter, hold a button to keep moving — so the rebbi can point the Insta360 at the class from the page itself, during the visibility check or mid-game, instead of walking over to nudge the camera by hand (aiming pauses a running sweep so the two never fight; the sweep setting itself stays on). The screens were also decluttered to keep the Kahoot feel front and centre: the sweep's technical numbers (stops/arc/dwell) fold away under a "Sweep settings" disclosure with working defaults, and the Questions tab folds the CSV-import and AI-prompt cards into one-line headings so "Add a question" and the question list are what you see. The game now walks **Kahoot's own beats**, with the rebbi pacing every step: the visibility check is reframed as the **Lobby** ("hold up your card to join!" — names pop green as boys join, same detection underneath); each question opens with a **Get ready** moment — the question alone on screen, no answers, for 4–8 seconds scaled to its length (Reveal skips it) — and **a completed scan always counts**: a card read during get-ready, or put down before the reveal, or caught at any sweep stop, stays counted until the answer is revealed (anonymous play keeps the highest simultaneous count per letter for the same reason — panning the camera can no longer erase cards it already read, though with no class list that count is a **floor** and now says so: it reads "at least 12 answered" once a sweep has fed it, because without per-boy cards the camera cannot tell whether the boy it sees at one end of the pan is the same one it saw at the other. The sweep panel says plainly that a class list fixes this, and only says it when there is no class list); after the reveal, **Next** shows a real **Scoreboard screen** — top 5 on the dark board, gold first place, a green +1 by everyone who just scored; and after the last question comes the **podium** — top three on gold/silver/bronze columns revealed 3rd → 2nd → 1st. Anonymous play (no class list) skips the personal screens and ends with the class total instead. **The screen can be split the way a slideshow app splits it — a "Second screen" option, off by default: tick it once (or press 🖥 Student view) and the board opens in its own window for the projector every time you press Start camera** — the boys' window carries only the board (question, tiles, "lift your cards", scoreboard, podium; every button hidden), while the laptop keeps the rebbi's own screen: full camera, aim pad, scores and all the controls, with a small copy of the board labelled *what the class sees*. Drag it to the projector and press F11. The choice is remembered, so a rebbi who always teaches on a projector never presses anything again, and one who never does is untouched — pressing the button ticks the box, unticking it closes the window. (It can only open on a button press, never on load: browsers block a pop-up that no one asked for, and if yours does, the app says so and keeps the board on this screen rather than leaving you with none.) Without it, the screens still fit: during play the board owns the page, the camera docks into its bottom-right corner as a thumbnail (so a scan landing is still always visible) and the standings step aside — they have the scoreboard beat now — which took the in-game page from three screens' worth of scrolling down to one, with the action button no longer below the fold. **One gold button always says what to press next** — Show answers now → Reveal answer → Scoreboard → Next question → Podium → ↺ Play again — so a rebbi who isn't comfortable with technology can run a whole game by pressing the only lit-up button, and the podium is never a dead end (Play again goes back through the lobby with points kept). With the PTZ bridge up, the camera also **goes looking for missed cards by itself**: every time a boy's card is decoded, the app notes the gimbal's pan angle plus the card's offset in frame — so the lobby scan quietly builds a seat map with no drawing or data entry — and when a sweep finishes with boys still uncounted, the gimbal drives to each missing boy's learned bearing, zooms in, and gives his card a moment to decode ("Looking for Dan…"), capped at a few seconds so the game never stalls. All local geometry, no AI, no images leave the room. And for a rebbi who doesn't want to type names, the Class tab gains **"No names? Just numbers"** — pick a count and it makes a class of #1…#N with per-student cards printed per number, so points and the leaderboard work with zero typing (the numbers land in the names box, so any can be renamed to a real name later).

- **`hadroom-camera-test.html` — a workbench page, not part of the app.** Nothing in Menchmark changed and nothing you use touches it. It is a throwaway diagnostic that asks one question before any of it gets built for real: can a webcam pointed at the room read several boys' QR cards *at once*, from where a rebbi actually stands, and how big would those cards have to be. It reads and writes none of your data, and is deliberately linked from nowhere. It can now also drive a camera's own zoom (the Insta360 Link's 4x), steer it automatically to whatever size the codes need to be, and swing the gimbal across the room and add up every code it saw on the way. The reliable way it aims the Insta360 Link is a tiny local helper in `insta360-ptz/` (`ptz-bridge.ps1`) that drives the camera's **standard Windows controls** directly — no Insta360 app, no pairing, no token — which the test page auto-detects and uses for its sweep. Delete it all once the question is answered.

### Changed

- **The activity codes under the seating chart are now grouped Give · Take · Record · Prize Store — the same headings the Dashboard's activity band above them already uses.** Both of those sit on the Dashboard at once, and until now they named the identical piles of codes in two different vocabularies: the band said **Give / Take / Record**, and the codes under the chart said **Positive / Negative / Navigation**. One rebbi, one screen, one set of activities, sorted into two sets of names — with the Prize Store section that PR #336 added, the seating chart now reads Give / Take / Record / Prize Store top to bottom, and every code sits under the same word in both places.
  - **Freeze moved from the deductions to Record, which is where the band has always drawn it.** Freeze scores nothing, and it had been filed with the minuses on the reasoning that it is unmistakably a consequence. That reasoning stopped holding the moment the heading over it read "Take" — a freeze takes nothing away from a boy. It now follows every other scoreless code into Record. If you liked it beside your deductions, drag it back under **⚙ Choose codes**; that is a per-code choice and it sticks.
  - **These are only the *default* names, and they are still yours.** Sections have been the rebbi's own list since v5 — rename, reorder, add, delete. So the rename is applied **once, and only to a section still carrying the name it shipped with.** If you had already typed your own name over Positive, Negative or Navigation, that name is left exactly as it is. And because it runs once ever rather than on every load, renaming a section *back* to "Positive" afterwards sticks too — the app will not overrule you on the next open.
  - **Nothing moved except the words.** Section keys, which codes are in which section, the order inside each one, and which codes you have switched on or off are all untouched — every code stays exactly where you put it and only the heading over it changes. The printed class sheet and the on-screen footer read from the same list, as they always have, so the sheet in your hand and the screen still agree. Purely additive: one new one-shot flag in your saved data, no `DATA_VERSION` bump, nothing converted, nothing that can be lost.

- **A brand-new setup now also starts with the Brachos tab tucked away, alongside the other week-two tabs.** Since the workflow-first default landed, a fresh setup has opened with Trends, Contest, Auction, Spinner, Passes, Attendance, Tracker, Homework and the Gradebook already hidden — the idea being that a rebbi's first week is scanning and standings, and everything else is something he turns on when he wants it. Brachos was simply missed off that list, so it was the one week-two tab still sitting in the Run row on day one. It now starts hidden too.
  - **This changes nothing for anyone already using Menchmark.** The seed only ever runs for a setup that comes through the wizard, and it is skipped entirely when you restore from a backup or your Sheet — your own saved tab choices are never overruled. If you already have Brachos showing, it stays showing.
  - **Nothing is removed, and it is one checkbox to bring back:** Settings → the tab visibility list → Run → Brachos. No data is touched, no field is added, no `DATA_VERSION` bump.
  - Also corrected a comment above the scan-bar code that had disagreed with the code itself for months: it claimed the scan bar stays visible on the Students and Activities screens, while the list right beneath it hid the bar there. The list was right and the behaviour is unchanged — only the comment was wrong. (Items 23 and 25 of `docs/UI_Theory_Tab_Audit.md`.)

- **The class name under the school name in the top-left corner is gone; the class name is set on the Settings tab now.** That line was showing `data.className` — the single name the app was set up for — while the class you are actually working with is the dropdown at the *top right*. The two were never connected: switching classes in the dropdown re-drew twenty different views and never once changed that line, so it sat there permanently showing one name (often the "Rebbi's Classes" placeholder left over from setup) in the spot a rebbi would most naturally read as "the class I am in." Removing it leaves the header showing only what it can actually keep true: your school name on the left, the class you have picked on the right.
  - **Nothing about the name itself changed — it is still stored, still used, and still yours to edit.** `data.className` is untouched in your saved data. It still heads your printed Gradebook, and still stands in on printed attendance and seating sheets when no single class is picked in the dropdown. Since that header line was also the only way to rename it after the setup wizard, **Settings now has a "Class name" box** — same value, saved the moment you click away from it, and it says in place that this is not the same thing as the class dropdown at the top right.
  - **No data model change, no new field, no `DATA_VERSION` bump.** Purely a matter of where one existing value is shown and edited. The in-app Help text and `docs/user-guide.md`, both of which told rebbeim to "tap the school/class name to rename it," now say where the class name actually lives.


- **Scan confirmations now appear as a floating banner, visible from anywhere.** The big "who / how many points / new total" confirmation used to live inside a box on the Dashboard — so when you scanned from the Leader Board, from Trends, or from the full-screen seating chart (where most scanning actually happens), the confirmation was playing to an empty room. It now floats briefly at the bottom-center of the screen, over whatever tab you are on. Same colors, same information, same 1.4 seconds — just somewhere you can actually see it. It also deliberately can't be clicked, so it never sits between you and a seat tile.

  **Settings → Scan Confirmation Banner lets you turn any of the three kinds off**, independently: points earned, points lost (including freeze), and tracked items (attendance, homework, pass, tracker). All three are on by default, matching today's behavior exactly. Turning one off only hides the banner for that kind — the confirmation beep still sounds, and the scan or mark is still recorded exactly as before; this is purely a display filter, nothing about what gets written changes. **Data:** three new booleans, `data.bannerPositive` / `data.bannerNegative` / `data.bannerTracked`, all backfilled to `true` in `load2fix()` — purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` change.

- **The Dashboard's dashed "Scan a QR code here" box is retired.** It was the third way of feeding in the same scan: a USB scanner already works from anywhere on any tab (no box needed at all), and the scan bar at the top of the screen has the typing box for entering a code by hand. Two identical "Scan a QR code here" prompts on one screen made the Dashboard read busier than it is, so the redundant one is gone. **No data is touched by this**: scanning behaves exactly as before, and if you had rearranged your Dashboard sections, your arrangement survives — the retired box simply drops out of it rather than resetting the rest.

- **Shorashim's "Show emoji instead of English" toggle now defaults off.** English words are what a new rebbi and a new student both understand without explanation; emoji was always meant as an optional flip, not the first thing you see. This is a session-only setting, not saved data — anyone who prefers emoji just switches it back on, same as before.

- **The Text tab's "Add pesukim or mishnayos" setup card now collapses once you already have text sets.** The AI-prompt builder, Sefaria fetch, and CSV import took up the whole top of the page every time, even for a rebbi who set up their sets months ago and just wants the list. It's now a single "Add pesukim or mishnayos" bar you tap open — "Your text sets" and the stitch tool sit above it, in view immediately. Nothing moved out of reach; every import tool works exactly as before once opened.

- **There is now one way to paste in a class list, and it's the safe one.** The Students tab had grown two paste boxes that did nearly the same thing. **Add many at once** — the one in the top card — checks your list properly: it accepts names copied straight out of a spreadsheet (tabs or commas), skips the header row, and tells you plainly what happened, e.g. "Added 12 · 3 already exist (skipped)". The second one, tucked inside the Google Sheet card as "Paste a list", did none of that: it had no protection against adding a boy twice, and if a name already existed it would **quietly change that boy's class** to whatever was in your pasted line. That second box is retired. Nothing is lost — **Add many at once** does everything it did and more, and the Google Sheet card now points you to it.

- **The Google Sheet roster card only appears once you've actually linked a sheet.** "Import roster from sheet" cannot do anything without a Google Sheet connected and its Apps Script deployed — so for the many rebbeim who never set one up, it was a prominent button whose only possible result was an error message telling them to go set up a sheet. It now stays out of the way until there is a sheet for it to read, and comes straight back the moment you save a Sheet link on the **Sync to Google Sheets** tab. **Nothing was removed:** if you use the roster import, it is exactly where it always was, and works exactly as before.
- **The seating chart's option buttons now wait behind one ⚙ Display button.** The chart's toolbar had grown to as many as nine controls sitting permanently between you and the seats — the Avatar picker, Points, Show activities, and (once activities were on) Grouped, the width control, and Choose codes. Every one of them is a set-it-once choice: you pick how the chart looks when you set the room up, and then you run the room. So they now live in a drawer that a single **⚙ Display** button opens and closes. Nothing moved *far* — every option is one click away, unchanged in what it does, and the chart still remembers all of them, so day-to-day scanning never needs the drawer at all. What stays out in the open is exactly what you use while teaching: the **List / Seating chart** switch, **⚙ Setup & arrange**, and the **Fill attendance** button when Attendance is armed. The full-screen chart gets the same calm: seats, the scan bar, and one small ⚙.

- **The List / Seating chart switch now shares a row with ⚙ Display and 🔄 Rebbi's view, instead of sitting on its own row above them.** On the full-screen chart this was three thin rows stacked before you ever reached a seat — the mode switch, then Display, then Rebbi's view — each mostly empty space. The switch is compact enough to share the row with Display (Rebbi's view still lands on the right of that same row, as before), so the panel is now two rows instead of three: **Setup & arrange** stays on the title row, and List / Seating chart · ⚙ Display · 🔄 Rebbi's view share the one below it. **The switch sits before Display, not after** — opening the Display drawer only ever pushes what's to *its* right (or wraps onto a new line), so the switch never jumps around as you open and close it. Nothing about what any of these buttons do changed, only where they sit — same for the Dashboard's non-full-screen panel, which shares this code.

- **Shulchani Mode now spells coins out by name wherever they fit.** The seating chart, the tap-to-give-points list, the live standings, the leaderboard and podium, the Store, and the printed point cards all showed balances as acronyms — "3 Di, 5 M, 1 Pr" — even when there was plenty of room for "3 Dinar, 5 Maah, 1 Prutah". Every one of those displays now uses the full coin names, and only falls back to the acronyms when a particular balance's breakdown is genuinely too long for that particular spot — so a boy holding "1 Darkon, 1 Sela" reads that in words right on his seat tile, while a messy seven-coin balance stays compact instead of overflowing. Hovering always shows the full spelled-out breakdown either way, exactly as before.

- **Planning only, nothing in the app changed:** six decisions about the upcoming Firebase rebuild were settled and written into the design record (`docs/Firebase_Rebuild_Scope.md`, `docs/Daily_Backup_Spec.md`, `docs/NOW.md`, and one narrow amendment to `CLAUDE.md`'s single-file rule). Nothing a rebbi uses is affected, and nothing about how Menchmark stores your data has moved. Recorded here because the single-file rule is a hard rule of this project: the Firebase SDK — and only the Firebase SDK — may now ship as a separate file alongside `app.html`, so that the app file itself stays one file for everyone and rebbeim who never sign in never download it. Also settled: retries of a save can never create a duplicate record, student photos stay where they are, and **the automatic folder backup moves earlier — it ships before anything else in the rebuild**, so there is never a stretch with no automatic backup behind you.
- **The Google Drive spike page no longer carries a live client ID.** It is entered once and remembered in the browser instead. Developer-facing only; that page is not part of the app.
- **The website now opens on the page that actually explains Menchmark, and the intro animation has its own address.** Until now `menchmark.app` played the scroll-driven brand-story intro, and the page that does the explaining — what Menchmark does, who it is for, what it costs — sat one click away at `menchmark.app/home`. That was backwards for anyone arriving from a search engine. The address that every link points at, and that every search result would show, held almost no words: no "yeshiva", no "rebbeim", no "middos", no "derech eretz", nothing about working offline. A rebbi searching for something like a middos tracker for his classroom had essentially nothing to find, because the front door of the site did not say what was behind it.

  **The two pages have swapped places.** `menchmark.app` now serves the landing page, and the intro plays at `menchmark.app/intro`, still reachable from "Watch the intro" at the bottom of the home page. **Neither page's content changed** — the animation is the same animation, the landing page is the same landing page, word for word. Only their addresses moved.

  **Every old address still works, permanently.** `/home`, `/home.html` and `/index.html` now redirect to the new home page. Any link already sent to a rebbi, any bookmark already saved, and anything a search engine has already recorded keeps working and lands in the right place. There was already a redirect from `/home.html` to `/home`, but it was a *temporary* one, which tells a search engine to hold on to the old address rather than fold it into the new — these are permanent, which is what actually consolidates the two.

  **The site also tells search engines what it is now.** It has a `robots.txt` and a `sitemap.xml` listing its real pages, the intro page has its own description and its own title (it previously shared the home page's title exactly, which reads as two copies of one page), and the home page carries a structured record stating plainly that Menchmark is a free educational app. **The app itself is deliberately left out of the sitemap** — what should come up in a search is the page that explains Menchmark to a rebbi considering it, not the tool he opens once he has decided.

- **Corrections you make on the Attendance, Homework, Passes and Tracker tabs are now kept alongside your scans, not just on the tab you made them on.** Until now, scanning a boy recorded his mark in two places, but *fixing* it on the tab afterwards only moved one of them. Nothing you could see was wrong, because the one screen that reads the second copy — the Gradebook — is still switched off. But it meant the two could disagree, and that disagreement was the last thing keeping the Gradebook off.

  **Now every way of marking a boy lands in both.** Press "Mark the rest Present", tap Present/Late/Absent on a row, check or uncheck homework, mark a pass used or give one back, press +1 or undo on the Tracker — each of those is now recorded the same way a scan is. Nothing about how you use those tabs changes; they simply stop being the only place the record lives.

  **Fixing a mark never erases the first one.** If you mark a boy absent and then correct him to present, both are kept, in order, and the correction is the one that counts. A mark you correct on Wednesday for Monday's date is filed under **Monday**, where it belongs, not under the day you happened to fix it.

  **Your existing records were brought across too, once, when you next opened the app.** Every attendance day you have marked, and your whole Tracker history, were copied into the second record. This cannot double-count: a record already there is recognised and left alone, so it is safe even if you restore an old backup. Homework was deliberately left out — it was already agreed that homework starts fresh rather than being carried over — and bathroom passes have no history to carry, so both simply start from today.

  **One deliberate difference to know about.** "Reset all passes (new week)" clears the counter on the Passes tab, as it always has, but it does **not** erase the record of who used a pass. So once the Gradebook is switched on, its passes column will show every pass used over time while the Passes tab shows only the current week. That is on purpose — a new week starting is not the same as last week never happening.

  **And one warning made honest.** "Reset" on the Tracker tab now also clears that activity's history from the Gradebook, because they are the same records. The confirmation box says so plainly instead of mentioning only the tab you are standing on.

### Added

- **You can now speak a note onto a scan instead of typing it.** Scan a student the way you always do, then press **🎤 Note** — or scan the new printed **🎤 Voice Note** card from wherever you happen to be standing — say what he did, and it is saved onto that scan. *"Helped Yitzy pick up his sefarim."* About five seconds, and you never touch a keyboard.

  **This is not a new record — it is a faster way into the one you already have.** Notes on scans have always existed: every entry on the History tab has had a "📝 Add note" button on it. But adding one meant a typing box, on a tablet, in the middle of a lesson — which is not something a rebbi is going to do while teaching. So the notes existed and almost nobody wrote them. A spoken note is saved in exactly the same place, in exactly the same way, and reaches the same Note column on your Google Sheet. A spoken note and a typed note are the same note.

  **Nothing appears on the screen while you speak, and that is deliberate.** The screen in a classroom is often facing the students — the seating chart, the Leader Board — so words appearing on it would broadcast the note to the room. Some notes are praise you would happily read out loud; "didn't do his homework" is not. The only thing shown is a small red dot in the corner while the microphone is on, which says nothing except that it is listening. You read and fix your notes afterwards on the History tab, at your desk, where they have always lived.

  **It stops on its own** when you stop talking, and after fifteen seconds regardless, so a microphone cannot be left running by accident.

  **You can also speak a note onto any older scan, from the History tab.** Every row there now has a 🎤 next to its 📝 button, so a scan from this morning — or last week — can be given a note without scanning anything again. Speaking onto a row that already has a note *adds* to it; the 📝 button is still how you rewrite or clear one. The 🎤 only appears on rows while the setting is on and your browser can do it.

  **Undo now works in two steps.** "↩ Undo last scan" — or the printed Undo card — removes the note you just spoke. Press it again and it reverses the scan underneath, exactly as it always did. So if you speak a note and then realise you scanned the wrong student, undo twice and both are gone. If that entry already had a note you typed by hand, undo puts your typed note *back* rather than deleting it. And if you have scanned anyone else in between, undo goes straight to that newer scan — the note is only ever the first thing undone when it genuinely is the most recent thing you did.

  **What it needs.** Chrome or Edge, and a connection. The speech recognition is your browser's own, built in, so there is nothing to install and no key to set up — the audio goes to your browser maker the same way any dictation on your device does, and never to Menchmark. On Firefox or Safari, or with no connection, the 🎤 button tells you so and you can still type the note in History as before. It listens through whichever microphone your device already treats as the default, so if you want it to hear a headset, set that headset as the default microphone in your device's sound settings.

  **It starts switched off — turn it on in Settings → 🎤 Voice notes.** While it is off both 🎤 buttons are hidden, the printed card does nothing, and the microphone is never opened at all; scan the card while it is off and the app tells you why rather than sitting there looking broken. No note you have already saved is touched either way, and typing a note on the History tab keeps working regardless.

  **Nothing is stored that was not stored before.** A spoken note goes into the same field, on the same log entry, that the History note button has always written to. The only new thing saved is that on/off tick box, which is added with a safe default the first time you open the app and cannot disturb anything already in your data. There is no data-version change and no migration, and a save from before this update loads exactly as it did.

- **While you're marking something, each boy's card now tells you where he stands on it.** Arm Attendance, Homework Checked, Bathroom Pass, or any activity you track, and a small line appears under every boy's name saying what he already has for that one thing — right on the Dashboard's class list and on the seating chart, on the screen you are already looking at.

  What it says depends on what you armed. **Attendance and Homework** show `present`, `absent`, `late`, `checked`, or **`unmarked`** — so "who haven't I done yet" is something you can see down the chart instead of working out. **Bathroom Pass** shows `available` or `used`, and if you've raised the cap above one pass, `1 of 3 used`. **Anything you count** — Chazan, Called On, Line Leader — shows how long it has been: `last: today`, `last: 3d`, or **`never`**. That last one answers the question a count is really for, which is *who hasn't had a turn in a while*, without leaving the room screen to go and look it up.

  **It only appears while something tracked is armed.** Arm a points activity, or nothing at all, and the line is gone — your chart looks exactly as it did. It updates the instant you scan a boy, so `unmarked` becomes `checked` under your finger, and it is shown whether or not you have points switched on: turning points off to get a cleaner chart shouldn't stop you seeing who is still unmarked.

  **It reads from the same place the tab does.** The Attendance tab, the Homework tab, the Passes tab and the Tracker tab each remain the record for their own thing, and this line reports exactly what they hold — so a boy you marked Present over on the Attendance tab reads `present` here too. Nothing new is stored and nothing is changed by looking; this only shows what your existing records already say.

- **Contest is back, and this time a contest's results are actually stored.** The Contest tab was hidden because of a fault that made it quietly useless: a contest's totals were never saved anywhere. Every number it showed — the standings, the "save to history" snapshot, the merge — was recalculated on the spot by adding up entries in the scan log. The scan log only holds the last 500 scans and is deleted outright by "Reset all scores", so a contest's results faded away as the class kept scanning, and vanished completely on a reset. A rebbi could run a contest for three weeks, press Save, and be left with a name and nothing behind it.

  **What is different now: each boy's contest total is a real saved number, added up as you scan and written straight into your saved data.** Nothing recalculates it from the scan log, ever. Wipe the whole scan log and every contest total is still exactly right — that was tested directly. The scan-by-scan breakdown still comes from the log and can still age out, and when it does the tab now says so in plain words instead of letting a half-empty list look complete.

  **How a contest works.** Press "Start a contest…" on the Contest tab and give it a name. From then on, the points you scan are **held on the contest** rather than added to each boy's score — his score sits still while the contest runs, and the tab, the Leader Board banner and a green **C** on the scan bar all say why. Scan exactly as you always do; nothing else about scanning changes. There is no end date to pick and no countdown: a contest runs until you finish it.

  **Finishing is two separate buttons, each with its own confirmation, and neither one destroys anything.** *"Add the points & finish"* adds every boy's contest total onto his real score. *"Finish without adding points"* closes the contest and leaves every score untouched. Either way the contest moves to **Finished contests** on the same tab, keeping its full per-boy results as a permanent read-only record — after adding points it is the only thing that explains why a boy's score jumped, and after letting them go it is the only record the contest ever happened. Each finished contest has its own "Remove from history" button, so pruning that list is always your deliberate choice, and its confirmation says exactly what removing it does and does not undo.

  This is deliberately narrower than the old feature. There are no start/end dates, no "contest only vs. both" modes, no pause toggle, and no separate save step. Those are what made the old lifecycle hard to reason about — including the end-contest dialog that promised a contest's history would survive while the OK button underneath it deleted the scan log that history was computed from. The old contest's own panels have not been deleted: if this device ever ran one, it still appears lower down the tab as read-only "Earlier contests" with its results intact, and if one was somehow left running it says so and offers a button to stop it. Nothing can start an old-style contest any more.

  **Nothing existing is touched, and no saved data can be lost.** Two brand-new fields are added (`miniContest`, `miniContestPast`) and nothing else is read, renamed, converted or removed — so there is no data-version change, and a save from before this update loads exactly as it did, simply gaining "no contest is running". Undoing or editing a scan during a contest correctly adjusts the *contest* total and leaves the boy's real score alone, since those points were never in it; once a contest's points have been added, undo behaves normally again.

  **The Leader Board can now show the contest standings.** While a contest is running, a **🏆 Contest** range appears next to Today and All-time, and it puts the contest's own standings up on the board — which is where a class actually watches a contest, rather than on a settings-ish tab nobody projects. The numbers come from each boy's stored total, so they are complete no matter how long the contest has run or how much scanning has happened since. The line above the list keeps saying the points are being held and are not in anyone's score yet, so nothing about the board implies the scores have already moved.
  **And each boy's held points show on his own card.** While a contest is running, a boy who has points held for him gets a second line under his score on the seating chart and on the Dashboard's class list — *🏆 3 pts held* — appearing the moment his first scan lands and updating on every one after it. This is the screen a rebbi is actually looking at while he scans, and until now it was the screen where a contest was invisible: his real score is frozen by design, so scan after scan appeared to do nothing at all. The score itself is untouched and still shown; the held line sits beside it, never instead of it, and a boy with nothing held gets no line, so the chart stays quiet until there is something to say.

  **The other ranges deliberately leave held points out.** Today, This week and the custom ranges show only points a boy actually has — so while a contest is running they read the same as All-time does, instead of quietly counting points that are still being held. Once you add a contest's points in, they appear in those ranges straight away, dated to the day each one was scanned. Nothing to turn on; it just stops the same board answering the same question two different ways.
- **Any tracked activity can now be armed and scanned from anywhere in the app — attendance, homework, the bathroom pass and every Tracker activity all record the same way.** Before this, only three of them could be armed at all: Homework Checked, Bathroom Pass and Attendance each had a hand-written branch in the scan code that recognised it *by its name* and sent it to its own store, and everything on the Tracker tab was deliberately kept out of every arming surface because it had no such branch and would have quietly scored points instead. So marking Chazan meant going to the Tracker tab and using its own scan box, while marking attendance could be done from anywhere — a difference no rebbi ever asked for and nothing on screen explained. Now there is one mechanic: arm the activity from the Record tab, the seating chart, the floating panel or by scanning its printed code, then scan boys. **Every tracked activity appears in the arming surfaces**, including the ones that were held back. Nothing about points changed — a tracked scan still awards none, and the armed pill still shows a tracked activity's name with no point value next to it.
  **This also closes the bug class behind the old homework problem.** Dashboard-armed homework scans once flashed a cheerful success and wrote nothing at all, because the part that flashed and the part that wrote were two separate mechanisms that could disagree about where a scan should land. They are now the same call: it records and reports, or it refuses and says why. It is no longer possible to express the failure.
  **Renaming an activity no longer breaks it.** Each tracked activity is stamped once with the id of the record it writes into, and everything reads that from then on. Renaming "Bathroom Pass" to "Hall Pass" used to turn it silently back into a plain activity worth zero points; it now keeps working. Existing saves get the stamp on first load — a new field alongside the activities that are already there, nothing renamed, converted or removed, and no data version change.
  **Nothing you already have moves.** Attendance, Homework, Passes and the Tracker keep their own records and their own tabs exactly as before, and a scan still writes to them first — the new shared record is written alongside, never instead. Every tab keeps showing what it showed. The one behavior a rebbi may notice besides the new buttons: a second bathroom-pass scan in the same week still refuses, and now records nothing at all rather than a refused attempt.
- **Attendance now works both ways round: mark who's away, or mark who's here.** A switch on the Attendance tab — *I mark: who's away / who's here* — and it changes one thing: what an **unmarked** boy means.
  **"Who's away"** is what the app has always done and stays the default for everyone: everybody counts as present, and you tap only the exceptions. Nothing changes for a rebbi who likes it that way.
  **"Who's here"** is the roll-call way. Nobody counts until you mark him, so a fresh day starts with every boy greyed and nothing claimed about any of them. The first tap or scan on a boy means **present** (in the other mode the first tap means absent — the cycle starts from opposite ends on purpose), and the fill-in button becomes **"Mark the rest Absent"**, which is the sweep that mode actually wants: you've marked everyone who showed up, so whoever is left is who didn't. The tab keeps count of how many boys you still haven't marked, and sending a day in this mode sends **only the boys you marked** — an unmarked boy isn't a present boy, so he gets no row rather than an invented one, and you're told how many are being left out before anything goes.
  **Switching is safe in either direction and rewrites nothing.** A day you already marked reads identically in both modes, so you can try the other way round mid-year and switch back if you don't like it. Nothing about how attendance is stored or synced changed.

- **The throwaway localhost servers development sessions leave running are now cleaned up automatically.** Looking at `app.html` in a real browser means starting a small local web server, and nothing ever stopped one again — the session ended, the server kept listening. Thirteen were found running at once on one machine, five of them from the previous day and no longer answering at all; only one belonged to a session that still existed. A hook now reaps them: **on session start** it kills servers whose session is gone, and **on session end** it kills the ones that session itself started. The two layers are not redundant — session-end never fires when a session is force-quit or crashes, which is exactly how all thirteen accumulated, so the session-start sweep is the one that actually holds. **It is built to under-kill rather than over-kill**, because the same `node` that runs a preview server also runs the editor's own background services, and killing one of those breaks a live session. A process is only ever touched if it is `node`, *and* is listening on a TCP port (the editor's services talk over stdio and never listen, so they cannot match), *and* its command line looks like a dev server, *and* it is on a dev port or references this repo — and even then, never while a live session sits anywhere up its parent chain. Anything ambiguous is left running. A newly orphaned server gets a 30-minute grace period so a preview still open in a browser tab isn't yanked out from under you, and **every kill is logged** to `.claude/reap-dev-servers.log` with the port, age and command line, so a surprise is always explainable afterwards. A server started by hand from a terminal has no session above it and so cannot be told apart from an abandoned one — its port can be pinned (`MENCHMARK_REAP_SKIP_PORTS`) and it is then never touched. Can be run by hand, including a `-DryRun` that reports what it would kill and touches nothing. Dev-only — no app behavior changes, nothing touched in the app or its data. See `.claude/hooks/reap-dev-servers.ps1`.

- **A Coin Guide for the class — Shulchani Mode finally explains itself to the boys whose scores it is written in.** Shulchani Mode swaps numbers for Talmudic denominations, which is the point of it; what it never came with was **anywhere a talmid could find out what they are worth**. A boy looks up at the Leader Board, sees **1 Dinar, 1 Maah, 1 Pundion, 1 Isar, 2 Prutah**, and has no way to tell whether that is more or less than the boy above him — the conversion table existed only inside the app's code and in whatever the rebbi had written on the board himself. **🪙 Coin Guide** now opens from the **Leader Board** and the **Prize Store**, and from **Settings → Shulchani Mode**. It has four parts: **the ladder** — every coin, biggest to smallest, colour-matched to how the app already prints it, with what it is worth in Prutot and what it is worth in the coin below it (1 Dinar = 6 Maah, 1 Isar = 8 Prutah); **how to read your balance**, including the one thing that genuinely confuses a boy the first time — that **earning can make your pile smaller**, because 7 Prutah plus one more Prutah is 8 Prutah, which is exactly 1 Isar; **worked examples** that show the arithmetic rather than the answer, stepping through *take the biggest coin that fits, subtract it, keep going* for five balances; and **a converter** — type any number, or tap one of the ready values, and watch it turn into coins. **It is built for the projector** — it opens over whatever tab you are on, so a boy asking "what's a Maah?" mid-scan is answered without leaving the screen and losing your place. **And it prints two ways, because the room and the boy need different things.** **🖨 Wall chart** is the one-page version for the board or the wall, in the same colours. **🪪 Pocket cards** prints **a sheet of ten identical business-card-sized cards** — the seven coins, their values and their rates, small enough to live in a sefer or taped inside a desk, so a talmid who wants to work out his own balance never has to ask. **A dropdown picks the sheet, the same way the student labels do** — **Avery 5911** business cards (3½″ × 2″), **Avery 5163 / 8163** (4″ × 2″), or **Cut out** on plain paper — **ten cards per sheet on all three**, with a note under it telling you what to load and what to set in the print dialog. **The two Avery options are looked up from the same `LABEL_SPECS` table the student labels use** rather than restated, so the guide and the labels can never disagree about where a card sits on the page; each carries that stock's real die-cut margins and gutter, and all three come to exactly 8½″ × 11″. **Cut guides appear only on plain paper** — ruling dashed lines onto real label stock would draw them straight across the cards. **Your choice is remembered.** The coloured dots are forced to print, since browsers drop background colours by default and the colour is the one thing tying the card to the board. **Every number in it is generated from the app's own coin table at the moment you open it**, deliberately: a conversion chart typed into the page would be a second copy of the same values, and the first time a denomination moved, the guide would carry on teaching arithmetic the app no longer does, with nothing to catch it. **The Leader Board and Store buttons only appear in Shulchani Mode**, since in points mode there is nothing to convert; **the Settings button is always there**, so a rebbi weighing up whether to turn the mode on can read exactly what his talmidim would be reading before he commits to it. **Data:** one new field, `data.coinCardFormat`, backfilled to `"avery5911"` in `load2fix()` — the same purely-additive shape `data.labelFormat` already has, remembering which sheet you print cards on. Nothing else is stored: the guide saves no student's balance and reads none, it is a page of arithmetic. Nothing is renamed, converted or read destructively, and **no `DATA_VERSION` change** — a save from before this update opens exactly as it always has, on business cards. The migration harness asserts all three cases: a save with no choice defaults to Avery 5911, a saved choice survives a reload, and a garbage value repairs to Avery 5911 rather than reaching the print code. All 264 built-in scenarios pass, including "Corrupted data".
- **Code size — a − / + on the class sheet that decides how big the codes and photos print, and a List that sizes itself.** A class of 25 with a photo *and* a code in every seat, and the activity codes underneath, did not fit one page: the codes are most of a card's height, that height was fixed, and the only ways out were to drop the photos, drop the activity codes, or print two pages — each of them giving up something the sheet is for. **Code size** now sits on the **Seating chart &amp; print** toolbar with a **−**, a percentage, and a **+**. Each press is one step (150% down to 50%), and every seat's code and picture move together, so a sheet that ran three inches long comes back onto the page without losing anything off it. Measured on a real 25-boy 5×5 chart with photos, codes and the activity footer: **1052px of page used at 100%, 966px at 60%** — the same sheet, comfortably inside the paper. It also goes the other way: a small shiur, or a Landscape sheet, can have **bigger codes than before** — worth doing if you scan from across the room. **What the sizes actually mean on paper:** 100% is exactly the size every sheet prints today, and 60% is a code about ⅜ of an inch square, which a handheld scanner still reads comfortably. Below that you are into territory that depends on your printer, so **print one card and scan it before you print a set**. **The seating chart never resizes itself.** You laid that room out; nothing on it changes behind your back, and a sheet you have set up prints exactly as it did before this update. **List (A–Z) is the opposite, and now sizes itself.** It is the automatic layout — it orders itself alphabetically and takes its columns from the paper — so it now **shrinks the codes on its own, step by step, until the whole class lands on one page**, never going bigger than the size you set and never below 60%. When it has shrunk, the readout says so (*auto 70%*), so you can see what you are getting. If even 60% will not fit — usually a long activity-codes block rather than the boys — it puts your own size back rather than handing you two pages of tiny codes, and the existing fit warning under the preview explains what to try. **The + button tells the truth about its own limit:** when a code already fills its half of the card — which is the normal state on a 4-column chart in *QR code + photo* — pressing + cannot make it bigger, so the button is greyed with a note pointing at fewer columns or Landscape rather than quietly doing nothing. **Data:** one new field, `data.seatPrintOpts.iconScale`, backfilled to `1` in `load2fix()` — 1 being the size every sheet already prints at, so **a save made before this existed comes off the printer identical to the way it does today; nothing repaginates for anyone**. The value is clamped to 0.5–1.5 on load, because it is multiplied straight into the sheet's sizing: a zero, a negative or a string in a hand-edited backup would otherwise collapse every code on the page, and the codes are the sheet. Purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` change, `migrateData()`/`load2fix()` in sync with `test-migration.html` and four new checks there covering the backfill, a saved size surviving a reload, and repair of a zero, a string and an out-of-range value (266 checks, 0 failures).

- **The seating chart on your screen now offers the same four cards the printed sheet does — including the boy's picture and his code together.** The printed sheet gained a photo-beside-the-code card (below); the live chart could not do it at all. It had one **⛶ Show codes** button, on or off, and turning it on *replaced* every boy's face with his QR — so a rebbi scanning off the smartboard could no longer see who he was scanning, which is the one thing the chart is for. That button is now an **Avatar** picker carrying **the same four choices, in the same order, with the same names** the class sheet uses: **QR code**, **QR code + photo**, **Student photo**, **None**. Pick *QR code + photo* and each seat shows the face and the code side by side with the name underneath, exactly like the printed card. **It is the same setting for the whole Dashboard** — List mode gets the identical card, because the class sheet has always built its List and its seat grid from one card and the screen now does too. **The codes have not changed**: a student code on screen is the same code that prints on his label and the same code the sheet carries, so scanning the screen and scanning paper still do exactly the same thing. **The two Activity-code modes are deliberately screen-less** — a merged `PA:` code is for scanning straight down a printed grid with no arming step, and on the live chart you already arm an activity by tapping it. **Arranging still never shows codes**: drag seats and the cards fall back to faces, then return to the mode you picked when you press *Done arranging* — the picker keeps showing your choice throughout. **It still disarms on a restart**, the same as before: the two code modes reopen as *Student photo* — faces, names and scores — so you never come back to a smartboard showing QR codes where the class expects names. *Student photo* and *None* carry no codes and so are remembered normally; they are a way of looking at the class, not an armed state.

- **Scores can now stay on the chart when the codes come on.** A **Points** toggle sits beside the Avatar picker, matching the class sheet's own Points checkbox and independent of it and of the card mode. Until now the live chart showed each boy's score whenever codes were off and *silently dropped it* whenever they were on, with no way to keep it — so a rebbi scanning off the screen could not see where anybody stood. It defaults **on**, which is what every chart shows today in the state it opens in, so nothing looks different until you turn it off. Works in List mode and the seating chart alike.

- **Data (both of the above):** two new fields, `data.seatCardMode` (`"qr"` / `"qrphoto"` / `"photo"` / `"none"` — the same vocabulary `data.seatPrintOpts.avatarMode` already uses, read through the same two helpers so a combined value cannot be understood by one renderer and missed by another) and `data.seatShowPoints`. **Purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` change, and nothing can be lost.** The old `data.seatScanMode` boolean is still written and still backfilled — it is kept exactly in step, it simply stopped being what the chart reads — so an existing save, a backup file and the Google Sheet all still carry the field they always did. A save predating these fields derives its mode from that boolean and, since the restart behaviour turns a code mode back into *Student photo* anyway, **every existing rebbi opens on precisely the chart he opens on today**. `migrateData()`/`load2fix()` stay in sync with `test-migration.html` (261 checks pass, including the corrupted-data scenarios).

- **Shulchani Mode now comes with the coins themselves as codes — and you set an activity's worth by picking a coin, not by counting Prutot.** Turning Shulchani Mode on used to change the *words* and nothing else: the Activities tab started saying "Prutot" where it had said "Points", and your activities stayed at whatever they were. Since the three that ship with the app are worth **1** each, that made every one of them worth **one Prutah** — the smallest coin there is — putting a Darkon **1536 scans** away and leaving most of the currency somewhere no boy in the class would ever reach. **Two things change, both only inside Shulchani Mode.** First, **the seven denominations are now scannable codes in their own right** — a **+ Dinar** and a **− Dinar**, a **+ Maah** and a **− Maah**, and so on for all seven — printed together under their own **Coins** heading on your class sheet, so a rebbi with the sheet in his hand can give a boy a Sela without first working out that a Sela is 768 of anything. They arrive the first time you land in Shulchani Mode and they are ordinary activities from that moment on: rename them, re-value them, delete the ones your classroom doesn't use. **The seven deductions arrive switched off on screen** — fourteen buttons is more than the Record tab wants, and taking back a Darkon is not a tile to reach past by accident — but **they all still print**, and the **Show** tick beside any of them puts it on the Record tab. Second, **an activity's value is now entered as a coin and a count** — "1 Maah", "3 Isar" — with the Prutot it comes to shown beside it, on both the Add form and every activity in the list. The stepper that made you type **192** to mean a Dinar is gone from Shulchani Mode; **points mode keeps it exactly as it is.** A value that isn't a whole number of any single coin still reads back correctly (200 shows as 25 Isar), so nothing you have already set becomes uneditable. **Your three starter activities get sensible coin values on the way in** — On time becomes **1 Isar**, Davening nicely and Learning nicely **1 Maah** each — but **only if you never touched them**. An activity you renamed or re-valued yourself keeps your number, always. **Data:** one new field, `data.shulchaniCoinsSeeded`, backfilled `false` in `load2fix()`, plus a `coin` marker on the seeded codes. Purely additive — no `DATA_VERSION` change, nothing existing renamed, converted or removed. **Switching back to points never deletes a coin code**: they carry the `coin` marker and are simply not shown, not scanned and not printed while balances are counted in points, so a rebbi can move between the two modes as often as he likes and find his codes exactly where he left them either way. A printed coin code scanned while Shulchani Mode is off says so plainly rather than quietly awarding a bare 192. **A rebbi already running Shulchani Mode gets the coin codes on his next load, and his activity values are left completely alone** — he has been scanning with what they are worth for weeks, and a routine load is not the moment to change that under him; only deliberately switching the mode on re-values the untouched starters. The migration harness carries the same seeder and passes: a points-mode save comes through with no coin codes and its values untouched, an already-Shulchani save gets exactly fourteen, and loading it again never doubles them.

- **Menchmark now notices when you haven't backed up in a while, and says so once — because until now it genuinely had no idea.** Your scores live in this browser, and the one thing that gets them back if that browser is ever cleared is a backup file. The app has always had the button; what it never had was **any record that you had ever pressed it**. No date, no timestamp, nothing — so a rebbi six weeks past his last backup and a rebbi who backed up this morning looked exactly the same to the app, and it could not have warned either of them. It can now. **One amber line across the top of the page, no more than once a week**, saying how long it has been, with **Back up now** — the same download you already use — and **Not now**. **It sits above whichever tab you are on**, so it is not hiding on the one tab you had no reason to open, and it **never prints**. **It is deliberately hard to trip.** It stays quiet for the first week, quiet on a class with no students in it yet (a brand-new install has nothing to lose, and a warning is not the first thing you should see), and quiet in preview mode, where nothing is being saved anyway. **Not now** silences it for the rest of the sitting — it comes back another day, but it will not follow you from tab to tab. **Backing up marks it, wherever the backup came from**: the Download button on Backup & Sheets, and the two safety copies the app already saves for you by itself before a Shulchani-mode switch. The standings CSV and the Log CSV deliberately **don't** count — those are reports to read, not files you can restore from, and letting a report silence the warning would quietly leave a rebbi with no way back. **Data:** two new fields, `data.lastBackupAt` and `data.backupNudgeSince`, both backfilled in `load2fix()`. **There are two rather than one on purpose.** If the app only stored "last backup" it would read *never* for every existing save, and every rebbi already using Menchmark would be met by a warning the moment he loaded this update — for something he may well have been doing by hand all along, which the app simply cannot see, since it can't look at files on your computer. So the second field stamps **the moment you first load this version**, and the countdown starts from there: **nobody is warned on day one, and the first reminder is a full week away.** Nothing is converted, nothing is renamed, nothing existing is read destructively, and no `DATA_VERSION` change — a save from before this update opens exactly as it always has, only now the app can keep count. `migrateData()`/`load2fix()` stay in sync with `test-migration.html`.

- **A boy's picture and his QR code in the same card, side by side — you no longer have to choose.** The class sheet's **Avatar** picker made the photo and the code alternatives: a seat could show one or the other, so a rebbi who wanted to *recognise* a boy and *scan* him had to print two sheets and look between them. Two new options, **QR code + photo** and **Activity code + photo**, put both in one card — **face on the left, code on the right, name underneath** — so you find the boy by looking and score him from the same card. **Side by side rather than one above the other, deliberately.** A seat card is about 1¼ inches wide with a ⅝-inch code centred in it, so close to half its width was already sitting empty while its *height* was the thing that pushed a sheet onto page 2. Stacking the picture above the code would have spent the scarce dimension to save the plentiful one. Across, it spends the empty width instead: **on a 6-column chart the picture is effectively free** — a card grows by about one pixel — and it is only the wide cards, 4 and 5 columns, that pay roughly a quarter inch per row for a noticeably bigger face. **And the picture is a usable size**, which was the point: it scales with the card, from about half an inch on a 6-column chart up to ¾ inch at 4 columns or in Landscape, and it is **cropped square here rather than round**. A circle throws away the corners of an already small photo — at this size that is most of a boy's hair and chin — so the square crop shows a bit over twice the face of the photo-only mode at its smallest and about five times at its largest. Round avatars are unchanged everywhere else in the app. **The codes are the same codes.** *QR code + photo* prints the identical student code *QR code* always did, and *Activity code + photo* prints the identical `PA:<student>:<activity>` merged code *Activity code* always did — verified by comparing the generated images, not assumed — so a code scanned off a photo sheet is indistinguishable from the same code scanned off the sheet you print today. Both new modes work in **List (A–Z)** as well as the seating chart, because the two layouts share one card. **The three modes you already use are untouched, down to the page height** — a code on its own, a photo on its own and *None* keep the exact markup and therefore the exact printed size they have always had, so **a one-page sheet a rebbi has set up stays a one-page sheet**; the side-by-side row exists only when both are actually being printed. If a photo sheet does stop fitting, the existing warning under the preview says so (confirmed against a 48-boy 12×4 sheet). **Data:** no new field and nothing converted — `data.seatPrintOpts.avatarMode` is the same string it has always been, and this only adds two values it may hold. Every value an existing save can carry still means exactly what it meant, so **no rebbi's Avatar choice is changed by this update**; a value that isn't recognised (a corrupt save, a typo) still falls back to *QR code* as before. Purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` change, and `migrateData()`/`load2fix()` stay in sync with `test-migration.html`.

- **The class sheet prints as a plain alphabetical list too, not only as a seating chart.** A **Seating chart / List (A–Z)** switch now sits at the front of the **Seating chart &amp; print** toolbar. **List prints the whole class alphabetically by last name** — the same order the Dashboard's List mode and the Students tab already use, so the three never disagree — with no desks, no *Front of room* and no empty seats. It is the sheet to print before you have arranged anybody, and the one to print when the page is really just for reading names off. **The cards are identical either way**: same QR code, same photo, same points, so a code scanned off the list is the same scan as off the chart. Avatar, Points, the Activities footer and Portrait/Landscape all still apply; **Rows/Cols and Rebbi's view are hidden in List**, because both describe a room — how many desks across, and which end of it you are standing at — and a list has no room. **Printing a list never creates a seating chart.** Opening this page in Seating chart still seats anyone unplaced so the sheet is ready, and switching back to Seating chart does the same — but a class you only ever printed a list for is left with no chart at all, exactly as you left it. **Data:** one new field, `data.seatPrintOpts.layout`, backfilled to `"seating"` in `load2fix()`; anything that isn't exactly `"list"` — a save predating the field, a corrupt value — becomes `"seating"`, so **no existing rebbi's sheet is switched to List for him**. Purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` change.

- **List and Seating chart are now one switch, on their own line — they are a mode, not two more buttons.** The two sat as the first pair in a single wrapped strip alongside **Show codes**, **Show activities**, **Rebbi's view** and the layout controls, so the one control that decides *what you are looking at* had exactly the same weight as the ones that only decorate it — and on a narrow screen it could wrap into the middle of the row. They are now a **segmented pair on their own line above the toolbar**: one control with two states, List on the left, Seating chart on the right, the active one filled in. Everything else stays in the toolbar underneath, where it reads as what it is — options that apply *within* whichever mode you have picked. **Nothing about what either mode does has changed**, both still remember your choice exactly as before, and the pair shrinks to match on the full-screen chart.

- **A class you haven't arranged yet shows your class list, not a grid of empty desks.** List / Seating chart is one setting shared by every class, but each class has its own chart — so a rebbi who arranged his first shiur and left the panel on **Seating chart** would switch to a newly-imported class and be met with rows of boxes reading "Empty seat" and **not one of his students on the screen**. The unplaced boys were only ever drawn while arranging was switched on, so there was nobody to tap, and nothing saying why. That class now shows **the class list instead — alphabetically by last name**, exactly as List mode has always sorted, with a line above it saying there is no chart for this class yet and a **⚙ Set up seating chart** button that opens the chart and starts placing boys in one press. **Your setting is not changed** — it still says Seating chart, so the class you *did* arrange still opens straight into its chart, and the moment you seat one boy in the new class it stops being empty and stays on the chart you just built. A chart holding only boys who have since been deleted or moved class counts as empty too, which is the one other way this used to happen. **Pressing "Seating chart" on a class with no chart takes you into arranging** rather than appearing to do nothing. **Nothing about any seating chart is created, changed, or deleted by any of this** — it only decides which of the two existing views is drawn. It can be switched off in **Settings → Scan Tab — Tap Panel**, which puts the empty grid straight back. **Data:** one new field, `data.seatListFallback`, backfilled to `true` in `load2fix()` for existing saves as well as new ones — the state it replaces is a dead end nobody chose, so there is nothing to preserve. Purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` change, and it reads `data.seating` without ever writing to it.

- **Recognize date range: Today, Custom (this week / this month / pick dates), and All-time — and it's now remembered.** The Leader Board's range picker used to reset to All-time on every reload no matter what you'd chosen, so it never stayed on the range you actually wanted for the rest of the period. **Today** is new outright — see just what's been earned since the day started, useful for a rebbi checking in mid-lesson. **Custom…** is now a bucket rather than a single calendar picker: pick **This week** or **This month** for an instant, no-typing range, or **Pick dates…** for the existing From/To calendar — the old Custom range date fields are unchanged and still drive it exactly as before. Clicking **Custom…** re-opens on whichever of the three you used last. **All-time stays the default** for anyone who has never touched the control, so nothing changes until a rebbi actually picks something. **🏆 Contest is unaffected**, and deliberately never becomes the remembered choice — it is driven by whether a contest is actually running rather than a saved preference, so it can never go stale once a contest ends. **Data:** two new fields, `data.standRange` (the active range) and `data.standRangeCustomSub` (the last-used Custom sub-choice), both backfilled with safe defaults (`"all"` / `"week"`) in `load2fix()` — purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` change, and a save from before this update opens exactly as it always has.
- **The printed class sheet lays its codes out the way the chart on screen does — including the one-row option.** The chart under the Dashboard got **▤ One row / ▦ Grouped** and a **1 → 2 → 3 wide** control; the sheet did not, so the same codes came off the printer in a shape you had no say over: three sections stacked one under the other, each running the full width of the page. Both controls now sit on **Print my class sheet** too, next to the Activities-footer tick, worded exactly as they are on the chart. Grouped now prints the sections **side by side in boxes** rather than stacked — the same shape as on screen, and it takes far less of the page, which matters because this block running onto page 2 is the failure this sheet has always been one code away from. **The two are set separately on purpose.** A smartboard and a sheet of paper are different shapes, and a rebbi may well want three across on the board and a single row in his hand. **Which** codes appear, what section each one is in and what order they come in stay shared, because that is what **Choose codes** governs and the board and the paper must never disagree about it. **Data:** two new fields on `data.seatPrintOpts` (`actGrouped`, `actCols`), backfilled in `load2fix()` to grouped/2-wide — which is what every existing sheet already prints, so nothing changes for anyone until he presses the button. Purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` change.
- **The separate code arranger is gone from the class sheet page — everything it did is on the sheet itself.** Choosing, re-ordering and moving codes were already there; the last two reasons to open the old box have joined them. **Click a section's name to rename it** — "Points" becomes "Middos" and it changes on the printed sheet, the chart and the activity menu together; clearing it or typing the built-in name back puts it as it was, so a rename is never a one-way door. **All** and **None** sit on the codes block they act on. The picker still exists and is unchanged — it is what **⚙ Choose codes** on the seating chart and **Choose codes** on Print: Activities open, since neither of those pages has a sheet to edit.
- **One chart on the class sheet page, not two.** The page carried a seating editor at the top *and* the printed preview underneath — the same chart drawn twice, so you arranged boys in one box while looking at the other to see what you had done. The editor is gone and **the preview is the editor**: drag a boy from one desk to another, press a seat's **✕** to empty it, and he drops into a *Not yet seated* tray under the sheet to be dragged or tapped back. **Rows and Cols moved up to the toolbar** — they were the one thing in the old box with nowhere else to live — and changing them still never moves a boy you have already placed. Both charts share one set of drag handlers now rather than the sheet having its own, which is what made two boxes necessary in the first place; and dragging works correctly in **Rebbi's view**, where what you see is reversed but what gets written is not.
- **The mock-up of the class sheet is now the thing you edit.** You could already choose and re-order your codes, but only in a separate arranger full of little chips standing in for the real cards — you changed the list, then looked back at the preview to see what you had done. **The codes on the preview are the controls now:** drag one to re-order it, drag it into another section, and press its **✕** to take it off. Anything you take off drops into a **tray under the codes** — click a name there to put it straight back, or drag it back where you want it. An empty section still shows on screen with a place to drop into, so a section you have cleared out isn't a dead end; it prints as nothing at all. It is the same list and the same order underneath as **Choose codes**, so the two can't disagree — this is the same edit made on the sheet in front of you instead of on a list describing it. **None of the handles print** — the ✕, the tray and the empty-section outlines are all screen-only, so the page that comes off the printer is exactly what the mock-up shows minus the furniture.
- **Every code can be switched on and off from wherever you are looking at codes — including Print: Activities, which had no switch at all.** The on/off choice has always applied to the activity menu (all four code surfaces read the same list), but that page gave you no way to *change* it: a rebbi printing his menu saw ten of his thirteen codes, with **◂ Previous Phrase**, **Next Phrase ▸** and **−Custom** simply absent — they start off — nothing on the page explaining it, and the only switch three tabs away underneath the class sheet. **Choose codes** now sits in that page's toolbar too, opening the same picker, and turning a code back on adds it to the menu in front of you. Between this and the seating chart's own button, **all four surfaces that show codes now offer the switch** — the class sheet, the activity menu, the chart, and the picker itself. The picker's wording follows: it now says plainly that the one list governs all of them, and the bin reads "not on any sheet or the chart" rather than naming one. **Also fixed while here: All and None only redrew half of what they changed.** They rebuilt the sheet but not the seating chart, so emptying the list left the chart still showing every code until something else happened to redraw it. All four surfaces are now redrawn from one place, so the next one added cannot quietly be left out.
- **Choose codes is now reachable from the seating chart itself, not only from the print page.** Arranging on the chart only ever moved *students*, so adding, removing or re-ordering the codes underneath meant going to Run → Print my class sheet — a strange errand when what you want to fix is on the smartboard in front of the class. **⚙ Choose codes** now sits in the chart's own toolbar, alongside Grouped and the width button, and opens **the same picker** — the actual one, not a copy of it, so there is no second list to keep in step and a code you drag on the chart has already moved on the sheet.

- **The activity codes under the seating chart are now grouped, labelled, and yours to arrange — and they finally tell you what they're worth.** The strip of codes along the bottom of the chart was one long wrapped row: every code the same size, in whatever order they happened to be added, with **nothing but a name on each one**. "Chesed" and "Disrespect" looked identical until after you had scanned one at a boy. **Every code now carries its value** — a green `+2 pts`, a red `−1 pts`, or, for the ones that don't score at all, what they actually do (`cycles attendance`, `marks a bathroom pass used`). Same green/red the Record tab already uses, with the tracked codes in the app's ice blue, which is neither. **And the codes are grouped into sections** — Points, Tracked, Navigation — laid out as small grids side by side instead of one endless row, each headed and counted. **These are the same three sections your printed class sheet has always used.** That is the whole point of doing it this way rather than inventing a second arrangement: move a code in **Choose codes** and it moves on the screen and on the paper together, so the chart on the smartboard and the sheet in your hand can never disagree. **If you preferred the old single row, it is still there** — **▤ One row** in the chart's toolbar switches back, and **▦ Grouped** switches in again. Next to it, a width button cycles **1 → 2 → 3 wide**, setting how many codes sit across each section; 2 is the default, 1 suits a phone held mid-scan, 3 a wide smartboard. Both buttons only appear while the codes are actually showing. **Sections can be renamed.** The headings are now editable in **Choose codes** — call them "Middos", "Consequences", whatever your classroom calls them — and the new name appears on the screen and prints on the sheet. Clearing a name puts the built-in one back, which is also how you undo a rename without having to remember what it used to say. **The section *keys* never change**, only what they are called, so renaming can't strand a code you had already filed somewhere. **And removing a code is now a bin rather than a checkbox.** Codes you turn off leave their section and wait in a **🗑 Removed** tray at the bottom of Choose codes; drag one back out, or press ↩, and it returns to the section it was in before — it keeps its place rather than being dumped at the end. Dragging a code out of the bin and onto another code both restores it and files it, in one gesture. **Removing takes a code off the sheet *and* off the chart**, because those two have always shown the same set, and making you remove the same code twice in two places would be a way for them to drift apart. **Nothing about how anything scans has changed** — tapping a code arms exactly what scanning it arms, through the same single entry point as before. **Data:** three new fields, all purely additive and backfilled in `load2fix()` — `data.codeSectionNames` (sparse; a section nobody renamed stores nothing), `data.actFooterGrouped` and `data.actFooterCols`. No `DATA_VERSION` change, and nothing existing is read, renamed, converted or removed: which codes are off and where each one sits still live in the same `data.printExcluded` / `data.printCodeSections` / `data.printCodeOrder` this release does not touch, so **a rebbi who already had codes turned off or dragged into place opens this version with exactly the arrangement he left** — the only difference is that his removed codes are now shown in the bin instead of sitting in place unticked. The migration harness asserts those three print stores come through a load byte-for-byte identical, and the new fields survive a corrupted value (a stored `0`, `99`, `"abc"`, `null`, or an array where an object belongs) by falling back to their defaults rather than reaching the layout.

- **The Points, Timer and Picker launcher buttons are now floating buttons you can drag anywhere — and stay on screen no matter how far you scroll.** They used to live in the header's subtabs row, which scrolls out of view on a long roster or a tall tab; now they float on top of everything, in a default bottom-right stack, and can be dragged to wherever is out of the way for a given class — near the board, out of the way of the seating chart, wherever. Each one remembers where you dropped it. **Nothing about what they open changes** — tap opens/closes the same floating Points scanner, Timer or Picker as before, and a tap that turns into a drag doesn't also fire the tap. The Settings toggle that hid them (**Header Shortcuts**) still hides all three, just as floating buttons instead of header icons. **Data:** `data.fabPos` is new (one saved `{left, top}` per button, keyed `scan`/`timer`/`picker`), defaulting to empty and backfilled in `load2fix()` — purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` change, and a rebbi who never drags one keeps the same default corner stack forever.

- **A one-scan way to run the Picker, for the 2-3 quick picks a rebbi actually does mid-lesson.** A new **CTRL:PICK** code (on the printed class sheet, next to Undo Last Scan): scan it once and the Picker opens and spins immediately — wheel or seating-chart mode, whichever you used last; scan it again and it spins again, for however many picks you want. It never closes the Picker itself — there's no exhaustion logic to trip over with a normal-size class, since typical use is a handful of picks, not the whole roster — closing stays exactly what it already was, a tap on the Picker's floating button or its own ✕.

- **❄️ Freeze — put a student's points on hold for a set time, right from the scanner.** Freeze is a new kind of activity — seeded as **"❄️ Freeze 1 min"** — that behaves exactly like any other activity (a button on Record, a printable QR code) but, instead of scoring, starts a countdown on whichever student it's applied to. **While a student is frozen, positive points are refused** — scanning or tapping an activity that would award them flashes "❄️ Frozen — can't give points" instead of scoring. **Deductions are never blocked** — a demerit still lands normally, freeze or not, and the freeze itself is untouched either way. **Re-applying freeze stacks time rather than restarting it** — scan "❄️ Freeze 1 min" on an already-frozen student and he gets 2 minutes, exactly like scanning any other activity twice adds twice. **Tapping a frozen student is the one place with a deliberate extra step**: it always opens a small dialog — Pause/Resume, nudge the time by ±30s/±1 min, or End the freeze outright — rather than silently extending it, since this is a consequence action, not a reward, and a stray double-tap shouldn't be able to quietly add to a student's time-out.
