Scanning stays fast and silent, exactly as every other activity does. **A small ❄️ mm:ss badge sits in the corner of a frozen student's tile**, in both the Record tab's list and the seating chart, ticking down live; the tile flashes a distinct icy blue — never the green of a good scan or the red of a bad one — both when a freeze is applied and again when it runs out on its own (never on a manual End). A student's personal QR code (Run → Labels → Personal pages) also gets a Freeze card, same as any other activity. **Data:** one new store, `data.freezes` (keyed by student id, holding the running/paused countdown), backfilled to `{}` in `load2fix()`; one new seeded activity carrying a `freezeSeconds` field, following the exact same one-shot pattern as Homework Checked/Bathroom Pass/Attendance — delete it and it stays deleted, and it never comes back on a later load. Nothing existing is renamed, removed, or converted; the `migrateData()`/`load2fix()` changes are additive only, no `DATA_VERSION` bump, and `test-migration.html`'s drift check plus all 220 built-in scenarios — including corrupted-`data.freezes` recovery and re-migrating a student mid-freeze — pass.

- **Per-activity seating charts — the merged code, laid out in the seat grid.** The class sheet's Avatar picker gets a new option, **Activity code**: pick one activity and every seat's QR becomes that activity applied to that exact boy — the same `PA:<student>:<activity>` merged code the per-student pages already print, just arranged spatially in the seating chart instead of stacked per boy. Grab the sheet for the activity you're running and scan straight down the rows; no arming code first, no separate student scan after. It works identically whether the activity awards points (positive or negative) or is one of the tracked activities (Homework Checked, Bathroom Pass, Attendance) that reroute instead of scoring — the merged code already handles both, so "a +1 sheet," "a −1 sheet," and "an Attendance sheet" are all the same feature, just a different activity picked. **Each sheet prints a colored name badge at the top** (e.g. "Sitting Nicely · +1") so a stack of sheets for different activities can be told apart at a glance while bouncing between them — the color is derived deterministically from the activity's id, since activities carry no stored color of their own; nothing about how an activity is defined changes. **The Activities-footer checkbox is hidden while in this mode**, since the merged code already carries the activity and a footer offering to arm a second one would be misleading. **Data:** `data.seatPrintOpts.activityId` is new, defaulting to `""`, backfilled in `load2fix()` — purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` change, and every existing avatar mode (QR code / Student photo / None) behaves exactly as it did.

- **Scan your class straight off the screen — no printed sheet needed.** Open the seating chart full screen and press **⛶ Show codes**: every boy's QR code appears in his seat, with the activity codes along the bottom, so a rebbi can start scanning on day one, before anything has been printed. **The codes are the same codes.** A student code on screen is the identical code that would print on his label, and the activity codes are the same set the class sheet carries — including your choice of which ones to leave off it. Scanning the screen and scanning paper do exactly the same thing. **Everything is tappable as well as scannable**: tap a seat to score that boy, tap an activity to arm it, precisely as if you had scanned either. **The names stay under the codes**, so if a code will not read you can still tap. **It switches itself off when you leave the chart**, the same way arranging does — you will never come back to a smartboard showing codes where the class expects names.

- **⛶ Show codes now also works in List mode, and the activity codes are their own toggle.** Show codes used to be seating-only, since seat squares are what codes were being drawn into — a rebbi without an arranged chart (or who just prefers the alphabetical list) had no on-screen way to scan at all. Both toggles now sit in the toolbar for List and Seating chart alike. **Show codes** swaps each student's card over to their QR (List's cards show QR + name in place of the avatar and score, exactly the same codes as the seating chart and the printed sheet). **Show activities** is new and independent — it puts an activity strip underneath, so you can arm an activity by tapping it, whether or not student codes are also showing. **The activity strip only carries QR codes when Show codes is also on** — turn on Show activities by itself and each activity is a plain tappable pill, the same `.act-btn` look the Dashboard's own activity buttons already use, not a QR meant for a scanner that isn't reading the screen. **↩ Undo last scan** comes along with either toggle, so a scan view built from either one (or both) is a complete scanning surface, not a partial one. Two flags, `data.seatScanMode` and the new `data.seatActivitiesMode` — backfilled `false` in `load2fix()`, no `DATA_VERSION` change — shared by both views, and both switch themselves off when you leave the chart, the same armed-state contract as arranging.

- **Undo is now where you are, not where you left it.** The **↩ Undo last scan** button used to live only on the Recent scans panel, which can be moved or scrolled well off the screen. It is now also at the top of the Dashboard, beside Customize layout, and at the bottom of the seating chart — because scanning off the full-screen chart hides both of the others behind it, and a mis-scan wants fixing without leaving the view you are working in. **All three are the same button** doing exactly the same thing: there is one undo in the app, shown in three places.

- **Raffle: leave a boy out of the draw.** A new **Who's in this draw** list on the Raffle tab, with a tick beside every name. Untick a boy and he is not on the wheel for the next draw. **Nothing is deducted and no points change** — his tickets are still his, and ticking him back restores exactly what he had. **It is collapsed by default and stays that way**, because the Raffle tab is usually on a screen the whole class is watching, and a list of names with boxes beside them is not something to open by accident. **It works however your tickets are counted** — every-point-a-ticket or students-choose — where before there was no list at all in the first of those. It writes the same removal the after-a-win **remove that student** option already used, so **↺ Un-remove everyone** puts back removals from either route, and there is only ever one thing to undo.

- **Groundwork for the Gradebook: one shared way of recording "this student, this thing, at this time" (Phase 2a — no visible change yet).** Attendance, Homework, the Tracker and Bathroom Passes are four separate hand-built versions of the same idea, which is why they each behave slightly differently and why none of them can show you a single picture of a student. This release adds the shared foundation they will all sit on — `data.trackedItems` (the list of things being tracked) and `data.trackedData` (the record of every mark, kept as a timestamped history per student rather than only "what it is right now", so nothing is ever written over). **Nothing looks different in the app yet and nothing you do changes:** Attendance, Homework, Passes and the Tracker all still work exactly as they do today, through their own tabs, storing into their own records, untouched. The new foundation is seeded and empty, waiting for the Gradebook screen (2b) to be built on top of it. Four items are set up ready: **Attendance** (present / late / absent), **Homework**, **Bathroom Pass** (one per week, as now), and one item per tracked activity you already have, each keeping its name. **Homework becomes a four-way cycle rather than a checkbox** — *unchecked → full credit → partial credit → no credit*, then back to unchecked. "Unchecked" and "no credit" deliberately mean different things: unchecked is *not collected yet*, no credit is *handed in but below standard*. A checkbox could not tell those apart, which is exactly the gap this closes. **Your existing data:** the new records start empty rather than being converted from the old ones. This is a deliberate clean start for the summer, while the beta group is small — the old Attendance, Homework, Tracker and Pass records are **not deleted, not modified, and not read**; they stay exactly where they are and keep running their tabs, so nothing is lost and a conversion can still be written later if it turns out to be wanted. Purely additive: no `DATA_VERSION` change, nothing renamed or removed, and the migration harness now asserts byte-for-byte that all four old stores come through a load untouched.

- **Do something to a whole group of students at once, from Run → Students.** Every student row now has a tick box, with **Select all shown** at the top that follows whatever the search and class filter are showing — filter to one class, tick the box, and you have that class. Two things can then be done to everyone ticked. **Assign to class** sets the class for all of them in one action, instead of opening twenty-five students one at a time to move a shiur. **Give points** hands the same amount to everyone selected, with a reason you type — the case it was built for is the day the scanner broke and a whole class is short the points they actually earned: select the boys, enter the amount, say what it was for, done. Negative amounts work too, for a correction that needs to come off. **It is a real scan, not a shortcut round the back.** Every point in Menchmark arrives as a logged event, and History, Undo, Trends and the contest merge all read that log — so a batch award writes **one ordinary log entry per student**, carrying the reason you typed, exactly as though each boy had been scanned. It shows up in History, it shows in each student's breakdown, it goes to your Google Sheet, and **you can undo it**. Nothing sets a total directly. Students who are not in the class currently chosen in the top-right corner are skipped rather than silently included, and you are told who and why. The selection itself is not saved — it is a thing you are doing right now, so it clears when you reload, and no new data is stored for it.

- **Bathroom passes: set how many each student gets.** The limit was fixed at one per week and is now a number you choose, on the Passes tab next to the list it governs. The tab's own wording, the summary line, the per-student badge and the refusal message when a student scans all follow whatever you set — a student on a cap of 3 who has used two sees "2 of 3 used" and is told "1 left this week" rather than being turned away. **Give back** now hands back a single pass rather than clearing the student, so a miscount can be corrected a step at a time. **Everything about existing passes is unchanged:** the default is 1, which is exactly how the app has always behaved, and anyone who had used their pass before this update keeps that use. **Passes still only clear when you press Reset** — nothing rolls over on its own, deliberately; automatic weekly rollover is being designed properly for when Passes moves onto the shared tracked-item engine. **Data:** two new fields with safe defaults (`data.passCap`, `data.passCount`), backfilled in `load2fix()`, no `DATA_VERSION` change. They deliberately sit *outside* `data.passes` so that store stays byte-for-byte identical through a load, which the migration harness asserts and which the tracked-item conversion will depend on.

- **The Gradebook — one grid for everything you track, in Run → Gradebook (Phase 2b).** Phase 2a built the shared record but left it invisible; this is the screen that reads it. Students run down the side, the things you track run across the top, and **the columns come from your tracked items themselves** — add an item and it appears here on its own, with nothing to wire up. That is the whole payoff of the foundation laid last release. **Two filters, because a grid without them is unusable in a real shiur.** *Choose items* picks which tracked items appear as columns — not a nicety, it is what lets the thing fit on a printed page at all. *Dates* is a range, not a single day (Today / Last 7 days / Last 30 days / a range you type), so you can look at three weeks of homework instead of one summary number. **The grid changes shape to suit what you asked for**, without you choosing a layout: one item over a range gives you a column per date; several items on a single day gives you a column per item; several items over a range groups the dates under each item's name and adds a **Total** for each. Only days that actually have something recorded become columns, so three weeks of homework is however many days you actually collected it, not twenty-one empty boxes. Totals read the way each kind of item wants to be read — counts add up, passes show uses, and for a list-type item like Attendance the total shows only the **exceptions** (`L2 A1` — twice late, once absent), since tallying every "present" would just restate the number of columns. Cells use one letter per value with a **key printed underneath**, so a page you hand to someone else explains itself. **Printing prints exactly what is on screen** — the item checkboxes and the date range *are* the print settings, there is no second setup to fill in and no way for the two to drift apart. It prints landscape, with the chrome stripped and the type shrunk to fit; if you have chosen more columns than a page holds, the screen tells you so before you print rather than after. **Nothing about how anything is recorded has changed.** This screen only reads. Attendance, Homework, the Tracker and Passes still work exactly as they do today, on their own tabs, storing into their own records, which this screen never touches. It lives under **Run** because a gradebook is private record-keeping rather than something to put on the board — the eventual split into a class-facing book and a private one is a later phase, and one book in the safer place is the right thing until then. **Because the shared record only starts filling when scanning writes into it (a later phase), the grid will be empty at first** and says so plainly instead of looking broken. **Data:** five view preferences only — which items are hidden, the date mode, the two custom dates, and whether the item picker is open. Purely additive, backfilled in `load2fix()`, no `DATA_VERSION` change, and nothing existing is read, renamed, converted or removed. The hidden-items list deliberately stores what to **hide** rather than what to show, so a tracked item added later still turns up as a column by itself.

- **Your attendance history now appears in the Gradebook (Phase 2c — attendance only).** The Gradebook shipped last release with an Attendance column that was always empty, because Phase 2a deliberately started the shared record from scratch rather than converting anything. This fills it in: every attendance mark you have already taken is copied across into the shared record, so opening Run → Gradebook shows the weeks you actually took, not a blank grid. **Present, late and absent all come across as themselves** — "late" is a real thing you recorded, with its own button and its own status in your Google Sheet, and it is not folded into either of the other two. **Your Attendance tab is completely unchanged** and still works exactly as it does today; nothing has been retired or moved, and this update only *reads* the attendance records — it does not alter, rewrite or delete a single one of them. **One deliberate omission, and it matters.** The Attendance tab marks everyone Present automatically the moment you open it, so you only have to tap the exceptions. That is a good way to take attendance and a bad thing to keep forever: on a day you opened the tab and never marked anyone, the app holds a "Present" for every boy that **nobody actually recorded**. Those are assumptions, not observations, and a gradebook that cannot tell them apart from real marks is worse than one with a gap in it — so they are **left out**. Only marks with a real time on them come across. Every exception you ever tapped — every late, every absent — comes across in full. **The one case this cannot see through:** on any day you pressed **Send Attendance**, the app stamped a time onto those auto-filled records in order to send them, which makes them indistinguishable from marks you made yourself. Those days convert in full, auto-fills included. There is no way to tell them apart after the fact, and inventing a rule to guess would be worse than saying so plainly. **You can see exactly what happened to your own data**: `test-migration.html` now reports the counts for any backup you drop into it — days walked, marks converted, and how many auto-filled assumptions were left out. **Data:** purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` change. `data.attendance` is untouched and still owns the Attendance tab; the copies are written into the tracked item `ti-attendance` alongside it. **Timestamps are copied, never re-issued** — the Google Sheet decides what to re-send by comparing a record's time against the last send, and the Attendance Log does not de-duplicate, so a rewritten timestamp would push your history into your Sheet a second time as duplicate rows. Nothing here writes a timestamp at all. Running it twice cannot double anything: each mark is matched on its own date and time against what is already stored, so a restored backup or a second load adds nothing. Homework, the Tracker and Passes are **not** part of this and are still parked exactly as they were.

- **The Gradebook is now readable at a glance, and prints one page per thing you track.** The grid was correct but hard to use: undifferentiated rows, uncoloured cells, and a single sheet of paper asked to hold 58 columns. Six changes, all presentation — nothing about what is recorded or stored moves. **Rows alternate light and slightly darker**, so your eye stays on the right boy all the way across a wide grid; the names column and the totals column stripe with everything else. **Cells are colour-coded** — green for present and full credit, brass for late and partial, red for absent and no credit, with brass being the same shade the Attendance tab already uses for a late boy. The colour is always *in addition to* the mark, never instead of it, so nothing is lost if you can't tell the tints apart or you print in black and white. Your own scales stay uncoloured, because the app has no way to know whether one of your options is the good one or the bad one, and guessing would be it telling you something about your classroom that it doesn't know. **Each date now shows the day of the week underneath it**, so *Jul 16* is also visibly a Thursday. **Weeks are grouped Sunday to Friday** with a rule down the first day of each week and alternate weeks shaded a shade apart in the header — so a fortnight reads as two weeks rather than eleven columns. **A short "Who" column is pinned on the right** carrying each boy's initials, so the far columns can still be read without scrolling back to the names; two boys with the same initials get told apart automatically, and the full name is still there on hover and for a screen reader. **And printing now gives each tracked item its own page** — Attendance on one sheet, Homework on the next — each with its own heading, its own dates and its own key, so a single page handed to a menahel explains itself. That is what makes the thing printable at all: what was one 58-column sheet that fitted nowhere is now four pages of about sixteen columns each. The row stripes and the colours are printed rather than dropped, and come out as distinguishable greys on a mono printer. **The items you tick and the dates you choose are still the only print settings** — there is no second setup to fill in — and since each item now gets its own page, switching one off just removes a page rather than making room.

- **"Print class sheet" — one page that is everything you need to start scanning, and you can now find it.** The single most useful thing in the app was also the hardest to reach: student codes sitting in their seat positions with every activity code underneath, buried behind an unlabelled 🖨 button on the Dashboard, titled "Print: Seating chart" — a name describing how it is laid out rather than what it gives you. It is now **its own entry in Run**, named for the outcome, and it works on day one. **For a new rebbi the activity codes are included from the start.** They were an off-by-default checkbox, so the one-page-does-everything sheet only happened if you happened to find and tick it. **If you are already using Menchmark, nothing about your sheet changes** — whatever you have the activities footer set to now is what it stays. You print the same sheet every Monday and it will not quietly become a different one on a Monday you didn't ask for. The checkbox is still there, and it is now on a page you will actually come across, which is the real fix. **And it seats your class for you.** Before this, a rebbi who had imported his roster but never opened the seating chart printed a page of twenty-four boxes reading "Empty seat" — a sheet that looked real and had not a single student code on it. Anyone without a seat is now placed in roster order, the page tells you it happened, and **anyone you had already placed by hand stays exactly where you put them** — it only ever fills empty places, never moves a boy you positioned yourself. The seating is saved rather than being a one-off for the paper, so the chart on screen and the sheet in your hand always agree. **The grid is also sized to your class** rather than to a fixed 4×6 that was invented before anyone knew how many boys you have — a class of four gets a 2×2, sixteen gets a 4×4, instead of twenty-odd boxes reading "Empty seat" eating most of the page. **A chart you have arranged yourself is never resized**, empty places included: a gap in a row usually means something — an absent boy's seat, an aisle, the shape of the room — and moving it would break the map that is the whole reason to print a seating chart at all. **And the activity codes underneath now show their full names.** They were being cut short to fit — "Davening…", "Learning…", "Homewor…", "Bathroom…", "Undo Last…" — because each label was held to a single line inside a fixed-width card and trimmed with an ellipsis wherever it didn't fit. The whole job of that footer is letting you read a label at a glance while you scan, so a shortened name defeated it; labels now wrap onto a second line instead. **You can also fix the seating without leaving the page.** The chart editor — same rows and columns, same drag-to-rearrange, same one you already know — now sits underneath the sheet, so when the preview shows a boy in the wrong desk you move him right there and watch the sheet redraw, instead of going off to find the Dashboard. It is the same editor, not a copy, so the two cannot fall out of step. **Drag-to-rearrange switches itself off when you leave**, so a seating chart left in arranging mode can't be nudged by a stray touch on the smartboard during the next lesson. **Choose which codes go on it** (#117): a rebbi's activity list grows all year, and printing all of it defeats the point of a one-page sheet. Tick and untick, and it is **remembered** — the two Teach-mode phrase codes and −Custom start off, leaving the seven codes actually used in week one, and anything you add later appears by itself. Turning a code off leaves it off every printed sheet, and never stops you tapping it on the Dashboard. **Two smaller things:** the sheet used to head itself "All Classes" even for a rebbi with one named class who had never touched the class selector — it now uses the real name; and Portrait/Landscape is remembered instead of resetting to Portrait on every reload. **The other print pages are unchanged** — student labels, per-student pages and point cards all still work exactly as they did, from the same buttons. This is a front door, not a replacement.

- **The class sheet now prints properly when the chart needs more than one page.** A six-row chart broke badly: seat rows were **split across the page break** — a row starting on one page and finishing on the next — and the activity codes ended up stranded at the bottom of page two, away from the chart. Since the whole promise of this sheet is that one page carries everything you scan from, codes on page two are worse than no codes. Now **a seat is never split** by a page break, so a chart that genuinely needs two pages breaks cleanly between rows, and the **codes block is never split** either. **The codes stay underneath the chart, always** — the sheet is a seating chart with the codes below it, and that does not change with the size of your class. When everything fits, that is one page exactly as before. When the chart is too big for one page, the seats fill the first page and the codes follow on the last one, so you always know where they are. **And the warning that should have caught this now works.** The app checks whether your chart will fit before you print, but it was measuring the small preview on screen against the size of a sheet of paper — two different things, since a seat card is more than twice as tall in print as it is in the preview. It reported a comfortable fit on a chart that overflowed by about three inches, so nobody was warned. It now works out the real printed height, and tells you before you waste the paper.

- **Take attendance with the scanner: scan a boy to cycle him through present, absent and late.** Attendance is now an activity you can arm like any other — a button on the Record tab and a code on your printed class sheet — so you can take the roll from wherever you are instead of going to the Attendance tab first. **Everyone starts present**, which is how the tab already worked, so you only scan the exceptions. Scan a boy once and he is **absent**; again and he is **late**; again and he is **back to present**. **The cycle wraps on purpose:** scan the wrong boy and you fix it by scanning him again, rather than putting the scanner down and finding him on the tab. **Tapping a name on the Attendance tab does exactly the same cycle**, so the screen and the scanner can never mean different things — and the Present / Late / Absent buttons are still there when you want to set a state directly. Sending to your Google Sheet is unchanged. **One thing worth knowing about your Sheet:** changing a boy’s status is a new observation, so it sends that change — but a boy you did not touch is never re-sent. Tapping the state a boy is already on now does nothing at all, where before it quietly sent his row to the Sheet a second time. After changing one boy on a day you have already sent, the next send is **one row**, not a repeat of the whole class.

- **Fixed: your Google Sheet’s log tabs could get their header row written twice.** Sending attendance sends one row per boy, all at once, and Google runs those at the same time rather than one after another. Each one checked "is this tab empty? then write the header" — so when two arrived together, both found it empty and both wrote a header. You would see the column titles twice at the top of a brand-new **Attendance Log**. Only the header was ever affected: the rows underneath are one per boy either way. The same check sat in front of the **Log**, **Pesukim Log**, **Tracker Log** and **Homework Log** tabs, so all five are fixed together. **This is a change to the Apps Script**, so it only reaches your Sheet once you paste the new script and do **Manage deployments → New version** — until then nothing about your Sheet changes. If you already have a doubled header, delete the extra row by hand; nothing else needs touching.

- **The migration test harness now checks itself for drift against `app.html`, automatically, as part of every run.** `test-migration.html` keeps hand-copied duplicates of `migrateData()` and `load2fix()`, and until now nothing enforced that they stayed current — only a "keep in sync" comment and a command someone had to remember to run. That failure mode was silent and the worst possible shape: a stale harness cheerfully tests *old* migration code and reports all green, so a genuine regression in `app.html` passes the one gate meant to catch it. The harness now fetches `app.html` on each run, extracts both functions, normalizes them (strips comments, collapses whitespace — `app.html`'s copies are indented inside the app's IIFE while the harness keeps its own at column 0, so a raw byte diff is always noise), and compares. A mismatch is a loud red failure card naming which function drifted, both normalized character counts, and the first point of divergence with an excerpt from each side — because an edit that swaps one character for another leaves both counts identical, and a count alone would point nowhere. The extraction and normalization are deliberately character-for-character the same as the manual command in CLAUDE.md § "Validate before every commit" check 3, so the two can never disagree about whether the copies match. **If `app.html` can't be fetched** — most commonly because the harness was opened straight from disk via `file://`, where browsers block `fetch` — the check reports "could not run" as an amber card and the summary bar turns amber rather than green, with instructions for serving the repo over http. A check that was skipped is never allowed to look like a check that passed, which is the exact trap this whole feature exists to close. Harness-only: no application code, data model, or teacher-facing behavior is touched.

- **Instant (no-spin) toggle on the Picker, Spinner, and Raffle** — get an immediate result without the animation, shown until the next pick, remembered per tool. Requested by a rebbi who uses the Picker for fast repeated turn-taking and didn't want to sit through a 4–6 second spin every time. Each tool has its own saved checkbox (⚡ Instant), so you can make the Picker instant for quick turns while the Raffle still spins for the class. Each tool reveals its result in the way that suits it: the **Picker** shows the student straight away in both modes (the wheel snaps to them; the seating chart highlights the seat with no casino-flash build-up), the **Spinner** shows the item immediately, and the **Raffle** still announces the winner prominently with the same 🎉 Winner reveal and the same after-win options (keep / remove ticket / remove student) — only the wheel animation is skipped. The result stays on screen until the next pick replaces it. **Off by default in all three tools**, so nothing changes unless it's turned on. Purely presentational: who gets picked is unchanged — the same draw over the same entries, so ticket weighting, skip-had-a-turn, out/passed eligibility, and round-reset all behave exactly as before. New `data.pickerInstant` / `data.spinnerInstant` / `data.raffleInstant` booleans, backfilled to `false` in `load2fix()` (additive only — no `DATA_VERSION` change and nothing to migrate).
- **Menchmark is now installable as a PWA (Chrome app) with persistent storage and full offline support; QR library inlined so the app is dependency-free.** The QR code library (previously loaded from a CDN) is inlined into `app.html`, so the app has no external dependencies and works fully offline. A web app manifest (`manifest.webmanifest`, icons reused from the existing Menchmark mark) and a service worker (`sw.js`) make it installable ("Install Menchmark" from the browser) — it then runs in its own window and the browser grants **persistent storage** (via `navigator.storage.persist()`), which resists automatic data eviction. Storage-protection status is surfaced in Run → Backup & Sheets. The service worker serves HTML **network-first** so a new deploy always reaches users (no stale-app trap), with a versioned offline cache (`CACHE_VERSION`) and a "new version ready — reload" prompt. Additive only: no data-model or storage changes, existing `qrPointsData_v1` data is untouched, and the app behaves identically when not installed. **Note:** persistent storage does not override an admin-forced clear-on-exit or Guest-mode wipe on locked-down managed Chromebooks, so the Google Sheet backup remains the ultimate safety net (and is unchanged/undemoted).

- **Seating-chart student picker** — a new mode in the top-corner 🎡 Picker, alongside the existing wheel. Flashes through the eligible seats casino-style, decelerates, and lands on one student; already-picked students are dimmed (fair rotation made visible). **Tap a seat to cycle its eligibility** — in → out → skip today → in — so the rebbi can set who's available right on the chart, even before attendance is taken. Had-a-turn and eligibility are two **independent** facts with **one signal each** — had-a-turn is shown by dimming (a grey wash + faded name), eligibility by a single small OUT/PASSED pill — so all six combinations (picked/not-picked × eligible/out/passed) are distinguishable without repeating the same signal, and cycling eligibility never hides that a student already had a turn. Chart and summary read the same underlying state. Picker-only — tapping never changes attendance; "out" is merely pre-seeded from today's attendance. Shares the wheel's "already had a turn" turn-state and the "Reset round" button (reset clears turns and "skip today", keeps "out"). Built as a reusable, context-agnostic engine (`SeatPicker`) with clean inputs (cells / used / elig / config / interactive) and callback outputs (`onLand` / `onEligChange` / `onExhausted`), so future features (Jobs, etc.) can reuse it with their own data, turn-state, and eligibility. No new stored data — turn-state is the same in-memory `pickedSet` the wheel uses, and the eligibility overlay is in-memory too.
- **6-7 celebration:** optional full-screen animation when a student's total lands on exactly 67 points (plain-points mode only; off by default — toggle in Manage → Settings). Silent by design; two SVG hands do the weighing motion with a small confetti burst, then it fades out on its own or on a tap. Fires every time a total lands on 67, not just the first. New `data.sixSeven` boolean, backfilled in `load2fix()`.
- Quick Start guide (`quick-start.html`) with a Quick Start button in the app header — a 15-minute zero-to-first-scan walkthrough for beta rebbeim, opened in a new tab from the subheader next to the help button.
- Animated brand-story intro page — a scroll-driven GSAP sequence taking the "s" out of "mensch" and turning it into a scanner, ending on a split-screen frame (dark payoff line above, "Try Menchmark free" invite below). Includes a "▶ Play" button that smoothly auto-scrolls the whole animation so users can just watch (cancels the moment they scroll themselves). Now the site's front door (see Changed); also reachable from the landing page footer as "Watch the intro".

- **Auction: leave a prize out of "Divide evenly."** Every prize in the Student-mode ticket list (Run → Auction) now has a checkbox on the left. Untick a prize and both **Divide this student's tickets evenly** and **Divide ALL students evenly** skip it — nothing is placed on it, so a rebbi can hand-place tickets on one special prize while still auto-splitting the rest. **Ticked is the default and matches today's behavior exactly**, so nothing changes unless you untick something. Already-drawn prizes show a disabled, checked box, since a drawn prize is already excluded from dividing. **Data:** one new field per auction, `divideExcluded` (a set of prize IDs to skip), defaulted to empty in `curAu()` the same way the auction's other per-class settings already are — purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` change, and an auction with no `divideExcluded` divides exactly as it always has.

- **Print class sheet → Choose codes: drag-and-drop, sorted into Points / Tracked / Navigation.** The picker that decides which codes print underneath the seating chart was a flat, unordered checklist. It's now three labeled groups — **Points** (activities that award points), **Tracked** (the rerouted ones: Homework Checked, Bathroom Pass, Attendance), **Navigation** (+Custom, −Custom, Undo, the Teach-mode phrase codes) — and every code is a small draggable chip with its own tiny QR preview. Drag a code up or down to reorder it within its group, or drag it into a different group entirely; both are remembered. **The printed Activity Codes footer now prints the same three labeled groups, in the same order, empty ones skipped** — dragging in the picker changes what actually comes off the printer, not just how the picker looks on screen. A code's group defaults to what it actually is (a rerouted activity is Tracked, a control code is Navigation, everything else is Points) unless a rebbi has dragged it somewhere else, so nothing needs setting up — the picker and the printed sheet look exactly as they did before this shipped until someone actually drags something. **Data:** two new fields, `data.printCodeSections` (sparse — only codes moved out of their computed default group get an entry, same spirit as the existing `data.printExcluded`) and `data.printCodeOrder` (the rebbi's chosen order, codes not yet in it fall back to their natural order), both backfilled in `load2fix()`. Purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` change; a save that predates this feature renders the identical sheet it always has.
- **Activities: a universal "Show" checklist, on the Activities tab.** Every activity — including Homework Checked and Bathroom Pass — now has a checkbox on the left. Uncheck it and that activity disappears from **every live arming surface**: the Record-tab buttons, the sticky scan bar's activity dropdown (the one that follows you to the Dashboard, the seating chart / Class list, and almost every other tab), the floating scan panel, and the fullscreen QR panel. **Printing is deliberately untouched** — the Activity Menu, class-sheet footer and Avery codes still offer every activity's code regardless of this checkbox, so a code you've hidden from the screen can still be printed and scanned off paper. This is the mirror image of the existing print-codes picker (#117), which hides a code from print only and leaves the screen alone; the two checklists are independent and don't affect each other. **Checked is the default and matches today's behavior exactly** — nothing disappears until you uncheck something. **Data:** one new field per activity, `hidden` (boolean), backfilled to `false` in `load2fix()` — purely additive, no `DATA_VERSION` change.

- **Seating chart: "A–Z by name" and "🎲 Shuffle" — re-seat the whole class in one click.** Everything else on the class-sheet page moves **one boy at a time**: drag a seat, ✕ to empty one. That is right for tuning a chart and hopeless for the two things rebbeim actually do to a whole room — putting a brand-new class into a sensible starting order, and breaking up a seating plan that has settled into cliques. Two buttons on the toolbar now do it in one go. **A–Z by name** seats everyone alphabetically **by last name**, filling desks left to right, front to back — the same sorting the List layout and the Students tab already use, so A–Z means one thing across the app. **🎲 Shuffle** deals the whole class into random desks. **Both ask first**, because both replace an arrangement you may have built by hand and a seating chart has no undo. **Only where each boy sits changes** — no points, scans, tracked records or history are read or touched by either button, and the confirm box says so, since "reset" and "shuffle" both sound alarming on a page whose cards show points. **Your room keeps its shape:** the grid grows if the roster no longer fits and never shrinks, so a chart you have arranged around an aisle or a doorway comes back the same size. The one exception is a chart **nobody has ever arranged**, which gets sized roughly square to the roster rather than keeping the invented 4×6 — the same thing the auto-seater already does, so a class of twelve doesn't print inside a page of empty desks. **Both charts redraw together**, the print mock-up and the live Dashboard one, exactly as dragging a seat already does. The buttons are hidden in the **List (A–Z)** layout, where there is no room to arrange. **No saved data changes and there is no migration** — these write the same `seats` array that dragging a boy has always written.

### Fixed

- **The printable class sheet sometimes came out as an empty 4×6 grid of "Empty seat" boxes for a class that has boys in it — and sometimes came out correctly, for the same class.** The auto-seater (which seats anyone who has no desk yet, so a rebbi who has only imported a roster still gets a usable sheet) ran in exactly two places: opening the **Seating chart & print** tab, and flipping the layout switch back from **List (A–Z)**. Those were assumed to be the only two ways to reach the sheet. They are not, and both of the paths that were missed drew the sheet without ever seating anyone — `seatLayout()` then *invents and stores* a blank 4×6 for whichever class it is asked about, which is where the 24 empty desks came from:
  - **Changing class in the header while already on the tab.** That handler redraws the sheet directly, so a rebbi watched a full chart turn into a page of empty boxes by doing nothing but picking his other shiur.
  - **The live chart's own "🖨 Print seating chart" shortcut** (⚙ Display → Print, on the Dashboard or the full-screen chart), which prints without ever going through the tab at all — so the sheet came out of the printer empty.
  - **Why it looked random:** it healed itself. Leaving the tab and coming back ran the auto-seater and filled the chart, so the very same class printed correctly or blank depending on nothing but how the rebbi had reached the page.
  - **The fix moves the auto-seater to where the sheet is *drawn* rather than where it is *arrived at***, so every path — including any nobody has thought of yet — is covered by one piece of code instead of a list of entry points that has to stay complete. It runs **at most once per class per session** there, which matters: the sheet also redraws after every ✕ on a seat, so an unconditional version would have re-seated the boy a rebbi had just emptied out, a second after he clicked the button that empties him, making ✕ look broken and the "Not yet seated" tray unreachable. The two original entry points still run unconditionally, so leaving the tab and coming back still seats a boy added to the roster since — exactly as before.
  - **Also fixed, found in the same place:** the **Rows** and **Cols** boxes disagreed with the chart directly underneath them. Auto-seating an untouched chart reshapes the grid — roughly square to the roster, instead of the invented 4×6 — but the spinners were filled in *before* that happened, so eight boys drew a 3×3 chart under boxes reading 4 and 6, and the next nudge of either spinner then resized the room from the wrong numbers. They are now re-read after seating.
  - **Nothing about the data model changed** and there is no migration — this only decides *when* the existing auto-seater runs. It writes the same `seats` array dragging a boy has always written, and it still never moves a boy who already has a desk. **Browser-verified** against a seeded two-class roster: both broken paths now produce a full sheet (and the live chart is redrawn to match, so paper and screen can't disagree after printing), ✕ holds across redraws and across a class switch away and back, re-entering the tab still re-seats as it always did, and **List (A–Z)** still writes no seating chart at all for a class that has none.

- **A roster with more than one class opened on "All classes" by default, and the global scan bar's Recent list mixed every class together regardless of which one was selected.** Both surfaces exist specifically so a rebbi juggling two periods can work with one class at a time, and both were quietly ignoring that split. `load2fix()`'s one-shot seed for `data.currentClass` (it only ever runs once, guarded by `typeof data.currentClass!=="string"`) now defaults to the first class — alphabetically, matching `groups()`'s own ordering — instead of `""` ("All classes"), whenever the roster already has classes on it; a roster with no `group` values yet still seeds `""`, unchanged. Because the guard is one-shot, this only changes what a *new* install or a freshly-imported backup opens on — any existing save already has `currentClass` seeded as a string from a prior load, so nothing changes for a rebbi already using the app, and a rebbi who deliberately switches to "All classes" keeps that choice on every future load either way. Separately, `renderRecent()` (the Recent list inside `#globalScanBar`) was reading straight from `data.log` with no class filter at all — a scan in Class B showed up in Class A's recent-scans strip. It now filters through the same `studentInCurrentClass()` check every other scan surface (Points, Store, Coin Deposit, etc.) already uses before taking the most recent 12, and shows everything when `data.currentClass` is `""` ("All classes"), same as before. No data model change — `data.currentClass` already existed; only the value its one-shot seed picks is different.

- **Avery 5911 business-card printouts (point cards, student labels, personal codes, activity-menu labels, Shulchani coin cards) were shifted left of the real Avery 5911 stock.** `LABEL_SPECS.avery5911` split the sheet's horizontal 1" of slack as 0.5" side margins plus an invented 0.5" gap between the two columns — visually centered on screen, but not what the real template does. Avery's actual layout (confirmed against their published 5911 spec) puts the two columns flush against each other with no gap and a 0.75" margin on each side instead — the same "rows touch, no gutter" pattern this code already used vertically (`rowGap:0`), just not applied horizontally. Changed `padS` from `0.5` to `0.75` and `colGap` from `0.5` to `0`; `padT`/`rowGap` (top margin, row spacing) were already correct and are untouched. Since `LABEL_SPECS.avery5911` is the single geometry source `renderAverySheetGeneric()` uses everywhere, every printout using that format now lines up with real Avery 5911 stock, not just the one that prompted the report.

- **Google Sign-In failed live with "Error 400: redirect_uri_mismatch."** `signInRedirectUri()` built the OAuth redirect from the page's *current* URL (`location.origin + location.pathname`) — but this app resolves to two different live paths depending on how a rebbi opened it: Cloudflare's clean-URL rewrite 307-redirects `/app.html` to `/app` for a normal browser tab, while the installed PWA's manifest `start_url` (`./app.html`) launches straight at `/app.html` with no redirect at all. Google requires an exact string match on `redirect_uri`, so Google Cloud Console needed *both* `https://menchmark.app/app` and `https://menchmark.app/app.html` registered, and only one was — found live, 2026-08-18, testing sign-in against the real Firebase project for the first time. Fixed by canonicalizing `signInRedirectUri()` to always return `/app.html` (the PWA's own start_url, guaranteed to exist) regardless of which URL the sign-in was started from; Cloudflare's redirect plus the browser's standard fragment-preservation across a redirect carries the OAuth response through to `/app` correctly either way. This is also the URI used for the Firebase Auth token exchange's `requestUri` and for Drive's OAuth redirect (`buildDriveAuthUrl`), both of which call the same function, so all three now agree by construction instead of by coincidence. **Only one redirect URI needs to be registered in Google Cloud Console going forward: `https://menchmark.app/app.html`.**

- **The live deploy was silently stuck one PR behind for nearly a day — every Cloudflare build since PR #299 was failing with "Asset too large."** `menchmark.app` runs on a Cloudflare Workers-with-static-assets project that has no committed `wrangler.jsonc`, so every build re-runs Wrangler's interactive auto-setup and installs `wrangler` itself as a devDependency into `node_modules`. With no `.assetsignore`, the deploy step (`assets.directory: "."`) tried to upload the entire repo root — `node_modules` included — and `node_modules/workerd/bin/workerd` (bundled by Wrangler for local dev simulation) exceeds Cloudflare Workers' 25 MiB per-asset limit, hard-failing the deploy at the very last step. Because the failure was in *deploying*, not building, nothing in this repo's own CI caught it — `validate` and GitHub Actions were green the whole time, and the dashboard's own "Latest build failed" banner was the only signal. Found by comparing byte-identical content between the live site and `git show <commit>:app.html` across recent merges, then confirmed against the Cloudflare Pages dashboard build log. Fixed with a new root `.assetsignore` excluding `node_modules/`, `functions/node_modules/`, `.git/`, `.wrangler/`, `.github/`, and `.claude/` from the asset upload — none of those were ever meant to be served. **This is a build-pipeline fix, not an app change** — nothing in `app.html` or any other served file is different.

- **Printing from the full-screen seating chart's 🖨 Print shortcut no longer prints the live overlay itself.** `#view-seats.seats-fullscreen` carries an unconditional `display:block!important` (it has to stay visible regardless of which tab is "active," since the pop-out never leaves the Dashboard) with higher CSS specificity than the print stylesheet's `.view{display:none!important}`. `triggerSeatChartPrint()` correctly swaps the active view to `#view-printSeats` before calling `window.print()`, but that swap only toggles the `.active` class — it never touched the fullscreen overlay sitting on top, so what actually went to the printer was a screenshot-style capture of the live chart instead of the dedicated print-ready sheet underneath it. Printing from the Print tab itself was never affected, since that page isn't wrapped in the fullscreen overlay to begin with. Fixed with a `body.print-seating #view-seats.seats-fullscreen{display:none!important}` rule that out-specifies the unconditional one for the duration of the print action only (the `print-seating` body class is already added right before `window.print()` and removed on `afterprint`), so the overlay reappears untouched once printing finishes.

- **The seating chart's "may not fit on one page" warning now appears above the preview, not below it.** The warning tells you to change controls that sit at the top of the page — Code size, photos, the activities footer, Landscape — but it was rendered underneath the full-page preview, which is at its tallest at exactly the moment the warning fires. You could print a two-page sheet without ever knowing the app had tried to warn you. It now sits directly under the toolbar, next to the controls it names. Nothing about the printed page changes — the warning was screen-only either way.

- **The class sheet's "doesn't fit on one page" warning now suggests the orientation you're not already in.** It always said "…or switching to Landscape," even when the sheet was already printing landscape and still overflowing — advice that told you to do the thing you'd already done. The suggestion now flips with the current orientation: "switching to Landscape" while in Portrait, "switching to Portrait" while in Landscape.

- **Opening the full-screen seating chart from anywhere other than the very top of the Dashboard hid the header entirely — brand, the Record/Recognize/Reward/Review/Run tabs, class picker, everything.** The overlay's top offset (`syncSeatsFsTop()`) is measured from the sticky scan bar's on-screen position, which is correct only if the page hasn't scrolled — but the seating-chart card usually sits well below the fold, so a rebbi scrolling down to reach the "expand" button had already scrolled the (non-sticky) header off the top of the page before clicking it. Once the overlay opened, `document.body.style.overflow="hidden"` froze the page exactly there, and the fixed overlay's top offset was measured from that same scrolled position — so the header stayed permanently off-screen, unreachable, for as long as the chart was open, with no way to scroll back up to see it. Fixed by scrolling to the top of the page as the very first thing the ⛶ click handler does, before anything is measured or frozen — the overlay's offset is now always taken from a `scrollY:0` frame, so the header is exactly what sits above it, matching what the overlay's own CSS comments already assumed. Browser-verified: opening from `scrollY:460` now lands at `scrollY:0` with the header's full 62px visible above the chart.

- **Switching away from the Record tab and back closed the full-screen seating chart, even though nothing about leaving it was a deliberate close.** `activateTab()` has always torn down the pop-out's overlay classes on every tab switch (it has to — the overlay can't stay mounted over a different tab's view), but nothing ever put it back when you returned to the Dashboard. `data.seatsFsOpen` — set true by ⛶, cleared only by the explicit ✕ — was still tracking "the rebbi had it open," but only the app's own boot sequence ever read it, so the flag quietly went stale the moment you clicked away and came back: the chart just stayed closed, indistinguishable from a deliberate exit. Fixed by giving the boot-time reopen logic a name (`seatsWantFsReopen()`, covering the existing "How Record Opens" setting the same way it always did) and calling it again from `activateTab()` itself, right after the Dashboard's own tab-entry rendering, whenever the tab actually changed *into* "scan" — so returning to Record now reopens the chart exactly the way reloading the app already did, through the ⛶ button's own click path (guarded against re-firing on `closeSeatsPopout()`'s own internal `activateTab("scan")` call, which just set `data.seatsFsOpen=false` and must not immediately undo its own close). No data model change — `data.seatsFsOpen` already existed and is still only ever written by ⛶ and ✕. Browser-verified: open full-screen → switch to Recognize → switch back to Record — chart reopens, header included; explicit ✕ close → switch away and back — stays on the plain Dashboard, as it should.

- **Auction ticket purchases now show up in History and reach your Google Sheet.** Every other way of spending points — the Store, a raffle prize — has always left a record. Auction never did: placing tickets deducted real points immediately, but that spend only ever lived in the auction's own private state, invisible in History and never synced to the Sheet. Drawing a prize locks every ticket placed on it, win or lose — that's now also the moment a permanent "Auction: [prize]" entry is written for everyone who had tickets on it, at the points they actually spent. Nothing is deducted twice; this only makes a spend that already happened visible. (Flat-budget auctions, where tickets aren't paid for with real points, still get the record with no point change — an audit trail of who bid, not a charge.)

- **The Mishnayos tab no longer silently teaches Pesukim content.** If you had no Mishnayos text sets yet, switching to the Mishnayos tab cleared the set dropdown — correctly, since there was nothing to pick — but then went ahead and taught whatever Pesukim set happened to still be active, under an empty selector. A rebbi with only Pesukim sets could open Mishnayos and see real pasuk text with no indication anything was wrong. It now shows "No Mishnayos sets yet — add one on the Text tab." instead, and the same guard covers the Pesukim tab if it's ever the empty one.

- **The Prize Store's "N prizes not yet picked up" count no longer gets stuck.** Clearing the purchase history (or refunding the last unreceived purchase) left the old pending count on screen — the code updated the count only when there was still a purchase log to read, so an emptied log skipped the update entirely. The count now refreshes every time, including down to zero.

- **Removed Trends' "Send totals to Sheet" button.** It pushed to a second, separate "Student Totals" sheet tab that only updated when you remembered to press it — while the Apps Script has, this whole time, already been keeping a live "Leader Board" tab current on every scan. The button's only real effect was a second copy of your totals that could quietly go stale; nothing it did was needed. (The identical button already came out of Backup & Sheets earlier — this was the other half.)

- **Your COM/serial scanner reconnects by itself again — and when it can't, it finally says why.** A rebbi reported having to reconnect his scanner in Settings every time he reopened the app. Auto-reconnect was already built and was supposed to handle exactly that, which made the report worth chasing.

  **The cause: the app was looking for the scanner by counting, not by name.** On reopening, it asked the browser which serial devices this computer had given it permission for, and reconnected **only if there was exactly one.** One single leftover permission — an older scanner, a Bluetooth-SPP device, a virtual COM port some other program installed — and the count came back as two, and the app quietly did nothing at all. It didn't try, and it didn't say it had given up. The screen just read "Not connected.", exactly as if auto-reconnect had never existed, so the only reasonable conclusion was that reconnecting by hand each morning was simply how it worked.

  **Now Menchmark remembers *which* scanner it was.** When you connect, it records that scanner's USB identity and looks for that one specifically next time — however many other serial devices your computer happens to remember. If it can't find that exact scanner but only one device is remembered, it still connects to it, which is the old behavior kept for the case where it was right.

  **And every remaining way this can fail now names itself in Settings, instead of showing a bare "Not connected."** Three messages, because they need three different responses from you:

  - **Several devices remembered and none is your scanner** — it won't guess, because opening the wrong port could seize a COM port another program is using. It tells you to click "Connect scanner…" once, and remembers your choice from then on.
  - **Running a downloaded copy of `app.html`** — this one was never fixable and pretending otherwise wasted people's time. A file on your computer has no permanent web address, so the browser has nowhere to file the scanner permission and forgets it every launch. It now says so plainly, and points at `menchmark.app`, where it does stick.
  - **Found it but couldn't open it** — usually the port is still held by the last session or another program. Unplug, replug, connect once.

  **If you already use a serial scanner, nothing is asked of you beyond one last manual connect.** Your existing setup has no scanner identity recorded yet; the first time you connect after this update, it's saved, and auto-reconnect works from then on. Keyboard-style USB scanners — which is most of them — were never affected by any of this and need no setup at all.

  Saved data is untouched apart from one new field holding that scanner id, which starts empty and is filled by your next connect.
- **Fetch & generate now brings the *whole* perek — not one lone pasuk.** In the Pesukim/Mishnayos import, tapping a chapter to fetch it from Sefaria and build flashcards was quietly treating the *chapter number as a verse number*. Choose Perek 6 and it fetched only "6:6" — the single sixth pasuk of the sixth perek. Choose chapters 6 and 7 and it fetched "6:6-7" — two verses of one perek — instead of the two whole perakim you picked. So the feature looked like it worked but generated almost none of the text you asked for.

  **What changed.** A tapped chip is now understood as a whole chapter: pick Perek 6 and you get *all* of Perek 6, start to finish. Because a long perek can be dozens of pesukim, the text is fetched and sent to the AI in small batches so it can't time out on the way — the status line counts the parts as it goes.

  **Picking more than one perek makes one flashcard set per perek.** Tap 6 and 7 and you get two sets — "Ch. 6" and "Ch. 7" — each imported on its own and named for its perek, with the last one loaded and ready. (Two perakim can't share a single set: they both have a "pasuk 1", and the two would collide. Keeping them separate is what makes each perek's numbering come out right.) A single perek still drops its CSV in the box first so you can eyeball the AI's work before importing, exactly as before.

  **And you can now take just part of a perek.** When you have a *single* perek selected, a small "only these pesukim — from / to" appears. Leave it blank for the whole perek; fill it in for a range like 3–9 and it fetches and builds only those, keeping their real pasuk numbers (the set is labelled "6:3-9"). This is the gentle way to work: a short range is a handful of pesukim, so it's quick and it sips the AI's free daily allowance instead of gulping it. The range hides itself the moment you pick a second perek, since a from/to can't span two perakim.

  **It's also gentler on the AI now, in two ways you don't have to think about.** Each request to the AI now carries a fuller batch of pesukim, so a whole perek usually goes in one or two calls instead of many — that alone uses far less of the AI service's limited free allowance. And the little service that talks to the AI now **remembers every passage it has already built**: the first rebbi to generate a perek pays for it, and everyone after gets the very same result back instantly and for free. Since the Hebrew of a pasuk never changes, this is safe, and it means that once a perek has been made, no one spends any of the allowance on it again.

- **A frozen boy can be marked present again — and can still lose a point.** Freeze was only ever meant to stop you *giving* points to a boy for a few minutes. It was never meant to take him out of the class for everything else, and when you scan his code it never did: a demerit still lands on a frozen boy, and so does attendance, homework, a pass or a tracker mark.

  **Tapping his tile did not agree.** Tapping a frozen boy — on the Dashboard class list or the seating chart — opened the freeze box no matter what you had armed. So a rebbi taking attendance down the seating chart came to the frozen boy, tapped him, and got the freeze box instead of a mark. Nothing said the mark had not been taken. He was simply skipped, and the roll was wrong. The same happened with a demerit: the one thing a freeze is *not* supposed to stop, and tapping could not do it.

  **Now the tap does what the scan does.** If a tracked activity is armed, or you have a demerit armed, tapping a frozen boy records it exactly as scanning him would. Everything else still opens the freeze box: nothing armed, a points activity armed, or a Freeze activity armed. So the box is still there whenever you want it — Pause, adjust the time, or End freeze — and it still takes a deliberate tap to reach, which is the point of it.

  **And the reverse hole is closed too.** The floating scan panel, the whole-class **Give to all** button and the Students-tab batch award had no idea freeze existed. A boy the scan bar had just refused could collect the very same points a moment later from any of those three. They now honour a freeze the way every other surface does — and because the two batch buttons award silently, they say afterwards **who** was skipped for being frozen, rather than quietly leaving him out of the count.

- **The Quick Start guide was sending you to a tab that had been renamed.** It told you to back up in "Run → Backup & Sheets"; that tab has been **Backup & Restore** for a while now, so the one instruction in the guide whose whole job is stopping you from losing your class pointed at a name that isn't on the screen. It now says the right thing.

  Two things the guide had never caught up with have been added to that same section, because both of them protect a rebbi better than remembering to press a button ever will: **backing up to a folder automatically** (choose a folder once — or a USB stick you leave plugged in — and it saves into it by itself, with no internet and nothing to remember), and **installing Menchmark as an app** (its own window, storage that resists the browser clearing your data, and it works fully offline).

  The printing step also now mentions **Run → Seating chart & print** — the one sheet that carries your boys' codes in their seat positions *and* every activity code together, which is what you actually clip to a board to run the day. It was reachable, but a rebbi following the Quick Start had no reason to know it existed. That tab is also where you arrange your room, which the guide never said either.

  **`docs/scanner-setup.md`** still told you to click into the "Scan" tab and go to the "Settings" tab — pre-redesign names. Now **Record** and **Run → Settings**, matching what is on the screen.

- **Two open Menchmark windows no longer erase each other's scans.** This was the most serious thing found in a deliberate hunt for silent failures, and it needed nothing unusual to happen: the app open on your desktop *and* on the smartboard, or a tab left over from the morning behind today's one. Each window kept its own working copy of the class and wrote **the whole copy** back on every scan — without ever looking at what the other window had written. So a window that had been open since 9:00 and scanned one boy at 9:05 put its 9:00 memory back over everything the other window had recorded in between. Hours of scans could vanish, **with no error and nothing to notice in either window** — the scans simply weren't there the next time anyone looked.

  Now the windows keep each other up to date. The moment one window saves anything, every other open window picks the change up and folds it into what it is showing — scores, History, the seating chart, attendance, all of it, within a moment and without you touching anything. A window's save is built **on top of** the other window's scans instead of over them. Both windows also show an amber notice saying the app is open twice: everything is kept either way, but scanning from one window at a time is still the tidiest way to work.

  **Two honest edges.** Two scans in the *same instant* can still race — one window at a time is the advice for a reason, and truly ending that needs the accounts-and-sync rebuild that is already planned. And if you use **"Clear all data"** in one window, the other window deliberately does *not* wipe itself in sympathy — it shows the notice and keeps working, because a window silently emptying itself mid-lesson is exactly the kind of surprise this fix exists to end.

  Nothing about the saved data's format changes and there is no migration — this changes *when a window reads*, not what is written.

- **Turning Shulchani Mode off now puts your activity values back.** If you tried Shulchani Mode and then switched back to ordinary points, three of your activities were quietly left at their coin values — and stayed there.

  **What was happening.** Coins only make sense if a scan is worth a real coin: at one Prutah each, a Darkon is 1,536 scans away and almost the whole currency is out of reach. So the first time you switched to Shulchani Mode the app raised the three activities it ships with — **On time** from 1 to 8 (one Isar), and **Davening nicely** and **Learning nicely** from 1 to 32 (one Maah each). That part is right, and it still happens.

  What was wrong is that switching **back** to points did not undo it. The mode went back to points; the values did not. So "Learning nicely" carried on awarding **32 points a scan instead of 1**, and there was nothing on the screen connecting that to a switch you had already flipped back. Turning the mode on and off a few times to see what it looked like was enough to do it, and because the raise only ever happened on the *first* switch, the change did not line up with anything you had just done — it simply looked as though the numbers had drifted on their own.

  **What changed.** The three values now follow the mode in both directions. Switch to Shulchani and they go up to their coin values; switch back to points and they return to exactly what they were before, every time, however often you go back and forth.

  **Anything you set yourself is left alone — that rule got stricter, not looser.** The app only ever touches an activity it has not been asked about: one still called by its original name and still sitting at the value the app itself put there. If you have set "On time" to 16, that is your number and switching modes will not move it — and from then on the app stops managing that activity's value altogether rather than putting a change back later.

  **If this already happened to you, it repairs itself.** Saves where the values are already stuck are recognised on the next load and the original value is recorded, so the next time you switch back to points they return to 1 as they should. **Nothing is changed when the app loads** — loading only makes a note; the values move when *you* move the switch. Activities you created yourself were never affected, and no scores, history or scanned codes are touched by any of this.

- **A missing Sheet link no longer makes every single scan raise a silent error behind the scenes.** If auto-sync to your Google Sheet was switched on but no sheet link had been saved yet — an easy state to be in, since the switch and the link are saved separately — then every scan, every undo, every note and every attendance mark quietly failed an internal send and left an unhandled error behind it. Nothing was lost and nothing looked wrong, which is exactly the problem: the app was generating a steady stream of errors nobody could see, drowning out any real one that might appear among them.

  Now the send failure is caught where it happens, once, with a single plain warning in the browser's console naming the cause ("check the sheet link under Backup & Sheets") instead of one error per scan. Your scans were always safe on the device — this changes nothing about what is saved, only about the app failing loudly-but-invisibly a hundred times a day. The attendance re-send button, which genuinely waits to hear whether its send got out before marking the day as sent, still hears about failures exactly as before.

- **The downloaded offline copy no longer wipes your work every time you open it.** This is the most serious thing fixed so far, and it only ever affected the **"Download separate offline copy"** file — not the normal app, not the installed app, not anyone's Google Sheet.

  **What was happening.** That downloaded file carries a snapshot of your class inside it, so it opens ready to use instead of blank. But the line that loaded the snapshot ran **every single time the file was opened**, not just the first time. So it put your class back exactly as it was on the day you downloaded the file — and everything you had scanned since was overwritten. Close the file at the end of the day, open it the next morning, and the day was gone. Silently, with nothing to tell you it had happened.

  **What it looked like if it happened to you:** points, attendance and homework jumping back to how they were on the day the file was made, and boys you had removed showing up again.

  **What changed.** The file now loads its snapshot **only when there is nothing to lose** — the very first time it is opened on that computer. After that it leaves your saved work alone, permanently. If you open a *different* downloaded copy on a computer that already has work saved in it, that work is now kept as well, and a message at the top of the app tells you plainly what happened and offers to save the other file's snapshot for you, so you end up with both rather than losing one.

  **⚠️ A copy you already downloaded is still the old, unsafe one.** The fix lives in the app, so it can only protect files downloaded from now on — it cannot reach into a file already sitting on a computer. **If you are running an offline copy you downloaded before today, download a fresh one and use that instead.** If you are not sure whether you are using an offline copy, you are almost certainly not — it is the file you would have had to save and open by hand.

  **The safest way to work without internet is still to install Menchmark as an app** (Backup & Sheets → *Install Menchmark as an app*). That keeps everything in one place, saves normally, and has never had this problem. The downloaded copy is for computers that cannot get online even once.

- **The Custom amount box now refuses the inputs that used to slip through it.** Three things could go quietly wrong with the little number box next to Custom (add) and Custom (subtract), and each has been closed at every place an amount can enter the app — the box itself, the two buttons, a scanned personal code, a scanned CTRL:CUSTOM code, the floating scanner's own amount box, and the seating chart's Give points window:

  - **A stray huge number is no longer a legal award.** There was no upper limit at all: an extra digit, or a barcode scan landing in the box, could hand a boy 999,999,999 points in one press. Amounts now top out at **1,000** — deliberately roomy, since Shulchani prutot make three-digit awards ordinary, but no longer big enough to end the class economy in one mistake.
  - **Junk in the box no longer arms a 0-point Custom.** If the box held leftovers a scanner had typed into it (or a plain 0), arming Custom armed *zero* — and every scan then wrote a log entry that moved nobody's score. Invisible on screen, but each one still used up a press of "Undo last scan", so undoing a real mistake meant pressing Undo through entries where it looked like nothing was happening. The buttons now fall back to the last good amount, exactly as the box itself already did when emptied.
  - **The box always shows the number that was actually armed.** Typing "-5" armed +5 (the box is a size; the two buttons are the direction) while the box went on showing -5. Whatever the box commits — a minus sign dropped, leading zeros stripped, an over-limit amount pulled back to 1,000 — is now written back into the box, so what you see and what the next scan gives can never be two different numbers.

  Nothing about normal use changes: type 5, press Custom (add), scan — identical. No saved data changes and there is no migration.

- **The points scanner no longer leaves a false error message behind when you switch away from the app.** It was meant to pop itself out into its own window whenever you switched to another tab or program, so it would stay in front of you. It never actually could: browsers only allow a window like that to open in response to a button press, and switching away from a tab is not a button press. So the attempt failed every time and left *"Couldn't open pop-out window"* sitting in the scanner for you to find when you came back — an error about something you had never asked for.

  The attempt has been removed. **Popping the scanner out by pressing ⧉ works exactly as it always did** — that is a button press, so the browser allows it. There is simply no longer a broken automatic version of it failing quietly in the background.

- **Adding a boy with a name you already have now asks first — and deleting one always tells you exactly which boy it means.** Two Moshe Cohens is a real thing in a Yeshiva, so the app has never forbidden it. But it also never *mentioned* it: pressing Add twice (or Enter twice) quietly made a second student with the same name, and from then on every list that names students — the delete confirmation, History's student filter, the Gradebook's rows — showed two identical entries with nothing to tell them apart. The paste-a-list importer has always checked for this; the Add button and Rename now do too. **It is a question, not a refusal:** "You already have a student named X — add another with the same name?" OK means yes (they really are two boys — give them different classes or an initial); Cancel means it was a slip.

  **The Delete confirmation now names the class and the points as well as the name** — "Delete Moshe Cohen (5A, 12 pts)?" — because with duplicates allowed, "Delete Moshe Cohen?" answers the question for *neither* of them. The class and the total are the two things you can check against the boy you actually mean before pressing OK.

  **Names are also capped at 80 characters** at every door a name comes in through (Add, Rename, the paste importer). Far past any real name — the cap exists because a barcode scan or a pasted paragraph landing in the name box used to become a "student" whose name was wider than the screen, stretching every list in the app.

  Nothing changes for students you already have — no saved data changes and there is no migration.

- **A whole batch of points can now be taken back in one action, instead of one boy at a time.** Giving points to several boys at once — from the tick-boxes on Run → Students, or the new "Give to all" — writes a separate line for each boy, which is what keeps History honest and lets you undo any single one of them. But it also meant that undoing a mistake made to twenty-five boys took twenty-five separate presses, which nobody is going to do. **A wrong batch was harder to take back than the single scan it replaced** — the opposite of what the feature was for.

  Now, pressing ↩ on any line that came from a batch asks whether you want to reverse **the whole batch**, and tells you how many boys that is: *"OK — undo all 25 · Cancel — undo only this one"*. Choosing to undo just the one still works exactly as before, so nothing you could do previously has been taken away.

  It works from **every** ↩ in the app — Recent scans, the History tab, the scan strip and the contest views — because they all run the same undo underneath.

  **"Undo last scan" deliberately does not ask.** That button, and the printed Undo code, are what you reach for mid-lesson with a scanner in your hand, and a question box landing in the middle of scanning would be worse than useless. There, one press reverses one scan, exactly as it always has.

  **Nothing is deleted, and your existing history is untouched.** Undoing writes a correcting line for each boy — the same way every undo in the app has always worked — so the original lines stay where they are and the record still shows what happened and what was reversed. Points you gave before this update are unaffected; they were not part of a batch as far as the app is concerned, and they undo one at a time exactly as before.

- **Pop-up messages no longer freeze the whole app until someone clicks them.** Every "done" and "that didn't work" message in the app used the browser's built-in pop-up — the grey box that stops *everything* behind it: the raffle wheel mid-spin, the countdown timer, a scanner mid-burst, all frozen until someone physically reaches the screen and presses OK. On a projector or a smartboard, that someone is you, mid-lesson.

  All of those messages — about forty of them, from "Backup restored." to "Add some prizes first." — now appear as a **card at the top of the screen** instead. It says the same thing, it has the same OK button, and it stacks politely if two arrive together.
