The differences: **the app keeps running underneath it**, and if you are across the room it **dismisses itself after twelve seconds** instead of holding everything hostage.

  **Questions still ask the old way.** Anything that needs an answer from you — "Delete this student?", "Reset all scores?", type-CLEAR-to-confirm — is unchanged, deliberately: a question that guards something destructive *should* stop the world until it is answered. Only the messages that just *tell* you something have stopped blocking.

  No saved data is involved and there is no migration — this changes how messages are shown, nothing about what is stored.

- **Your history now goes back about ten times further, and an undo can no longer run out of history to undo.** The app kept only your most recent **500** scans; it now keeps **5,000**. In ordinary use 500 was three or four school days — far less than the word "History" suggests — and once a scan dropped off the end it was gone from more than the list. **Undo finds a scan by looking for it in that history**, so a scan that had aged out could not be reversed at all: pressing ↩ simply did nothing, with no explanation. The Contest tab's scan-by-scan breakdown reads the same history, so it quietly stopped counting old scans too. 5,000 is roughly a month and a half of ordinary use, and it is the whole reason the count went up: batch awards write one entry per boy, and undoing them writes another each, so a 25-boy award and its undo used to eat a tenth of everything you had. **Nothing you have already recorded is affected** — this only changes how much is kept from here on, and no saved data is converted or moved. Your points, totals and Trends were never affected by this at all; they are counted separately and have always been complete.

- **If this device ever runs out of storage, the app now says so instead of silently not saving.** Saving could fail — a full device, a locked-down school Chromebook — and when it did, nothing said anything: every scan still flashed green and beeped, and none of it was being written down. A rebbi could scan a whole morning into nothing. Now, if space runs out, the app **drops the oldest history entries to make room and keeps going**, and tells you plainly how many it dropped — your roster, every boy's score, and today's marks are the last things to go, and those older entries are still on the Log tab of your Google Sheet if you sync. If it cannot save even after that, a red banner says your recent scans were **not** saved and tells you to back up to your Sheet before closing the tab. This was worth doing now because keeping ten times more history makes each save bigger, and a bigger save is likelier to be the one that hits a limit.

- **A new tracked activity did not appear on the Dashboard until you reloaded the app.** Adding one with "+ Add activity" on the Tracker tab saved it correctly and it showed up on the Tracker tab straight away — but the row of activity buttons you actually scan with, on the Dashboard, carried on showing the old list. Going to another tab and coming back did not help; only closing and reopening the app did. So a rebbi who added "Chazan" and went to use it found it simply wasn't there, with nothing to suggest the problem was only on screen. The button now appears the moment you add it. Nothing was ever lost — the activity was saved the whole time, and any you added earlier are already there.

- **Undoing a scan left the old number sitting on the seating chart and the class list.** The undo itself always worked — the boy's score was corrected properly and everything else in the app agreed — but his own card kept showing the score he had *before* the undo, sometimes for a good while, until something unrelated happened to redraw the panel. So the one card a rebbi was looking at was the one place still showing the mistake he had just fixed, which is a hard thing to trust. His card now updates the moment the undo lands, on both the seating chart and the class list. Found while putting contest points on those cards; it has been the case since long before contests, and applies to every scan, contest or not.

- **"Mark all Present" wiped out the absences you had just marked. It is now "Mark the rest Present" and leaves them alone.** The button wrote *present* over every boy in the class, so a rebbi who marked three boys absent and then reached for it — reasonably enough, to fill in everyone else — silently lost all three, with no undo and no warning, and the day then read as a clean full house. It now fills only the boys nobody has marked yet; anyone you deliberately set to absent or late stays exactly as you set him. It is also the plainest way to say "I took attendance and everyone is here," which matters for the fix below.

- **The app could not tell "everyone was here" from "I never took attendance" — and sending the day quietly turned the second into the first.** An unmarked boy shows as present, which is deliberate and good: you only tap the exceptions. But a day nobody had touched *also* showed a full present roll, and pressing Send Attendance stamped every one of those boys with a fresh timestamp and pushed the lot to the Sheet, indistinguishable from a roll you had actually taken. A day you never got to would arrive in the Sheet as perfect attendance, in your name. Now the Attendance tab says **"not marked yet"** on the summary line while a day is still untouched, and sending such a day asks first — naming the date and the number of boys it is about to record as present. Confirming is the point: saying yes is you making the statement, rather than the app making it for you. A day you have marked normally sends exactly as before, with no extra click. Nothing about how attendance is recorded, stored or synced changed, and no existing record was altered.

- **A boy's attendance history counted days nobody ever took as days he was present.** The By Student view walked every stored day and tallied the status it found — including the auto-filled *present* on days attendance was never taken at all, which the app wrote just from the tab having been opened. So a rebbi checking whether a boy has been showing up saw a present tally padded with days that never happened. Days nobody marked are now left out of that count entirely. A day you did take still counts its unmarked boys as present, because there that is your actual statement.
- **On a class sheet printing the boys' faces, Code size only went down. Once you had pressed −, + could not take you back.** Turn on an **Avatar** setting that includes the photo, press **−** a few times, and **+** went grey and stayed grey — the size you started at was gone, with no way to get it back short of guessing at Landscape or a different number of columns. **What was happening:** each picture is capped both by the size you set *and* by how much room its card has, and once the photo shares a card with a code the card is usually the smaller of the two. Measured on a real sheet — 24 boys, 6 columns, portrait, **QR code + photo** — the picture draws 51px against an allowance of 76px, so **every setting from 70% upwards prints exactly the same sheet**, and + was switched off across that entire range on the correct reasoning that pressing it would change nothing. What that missed is that − walks *into* that range from above: pressing it at 100%, 90% and 80% moved the number while the page stayed identical, only at 60% did the picture visibly shrink — and + then brought you back precisely one step, to 70%, before going dead again. **Now the whole "same sheet" range is treated as one position.** The readout shows the size the cards are actually full at rather than the larger number stored behind it, − always makes the next press visibly smaller, and + always walks back up to where you were — while still stopping, as it did before, at the point where a bigger number would print an identical page. The tooltip on a stopped + still tells you the way to genuinely bigger codes: fewer columns, or Landscape. **Your printed sheets are unchanged** — every size prints exactly what it printed before; this is which sizes the two buttons will move between. **No saved data changes and there is no migration.**

- **In the photo-and-code seat cards, the picture could quietly grow into space the code gave up — which made rows taller instead of shorter.** The face and the code split each card in half, but the two halves were free to lend width to each other, and their size limits are deliberately different (the face is allowed to be a little larger than the code). So whenever the code hit its limit first, the leftover width went to the *photo*, which then set the height of the row: on a real 25-boy sheet, asking for **smaller** codes at 90% produced a page **26px taller** than at 100%, and tripped the "may not fit on one page" warning while doing it. The two halves are now genuinely independent — neither can reach into the other's — so **the cards get shorter every single time you press −**, which is the whole point of the button. Found while building **Code size** above; at the sizes the sheet printed before, the two behave identically, so an existing sheet is unchanged.

- **Making your classroom narrower threw boys out of their seats without saying so, and the print page then invented a much longer chart to put them back.** Set your seating chart to fewer columns — say a 4×6 room down to two columns — and the grid went from 24 desks to 8. **The other sixteen boys lost their seats on the spot**, with no warning, no list of who, and no way back. Then, opening 🖨 Print quietly re-seated all sixteen by stretching the chart downward and *saving* it, so a rebbi who asked for a two-column room got a **twelve-row sheet he never built** — and found that same twelve-row chart waiting for him back on the Dashboard. That is the "why does my printed chart have so many more rows than I set up?" that was reported. Underneath, the seats are stored as one long list and nothing was re-reading it against the new width, which broke it a second way too: **the boys who did keep a seat still moved**, because the same position in the list means a different desk once the room is a different width. **Now every boy keeps his own row and his own column.** Changing the width no longer shuffles anybody, and **nobody is ever unseated** — when the room gets narrower, only the boys whose column has genuinely stopped existing move, and they are re-seated further down in reading order rather than dropped. Rows grow by however much that takes, so the chart stays honest about holding your whole class. **The print page no longer rewrites your layout** to make things fit, because there is nothing left for it to fix. Your deliberate gaps — an aisle, an absent boy's desk — are still preserved. **What to expect:** twenty-four boys in two columns really is twelve rows deep; that was always going to be true, but now you see it on the Dashboard the moment you make the change, instead of being surprised by it on paper.
- **Nudging Rows or Cols could silently delete seats.** The Rows box refused anything above 12, but the app itself could store a taller chart than that — a class of thirty in two columns needs fifteen rows. The box then showed a number it would not accept, and **the next touch of either spinner snapped it back to 12 and destroyed six boys' seats.** Rows now goes up to 60, and more importantly the chart is allowed to keep whatever height it actually needs: if you ask for fewer rows than your seated boys occupy, it grows back instead of dropping anyone, and the spinner shows you the height that really took effect. Columns still stop at 12, which is wider than a sheet of paper can carry.

- **A frozen boy stopped being marked as frozen the moment you turned codes on.** The ❄️ badge sat on his card in the ordinary seating chart, but the old **Show codes** view built its cards down a separate path that never drew it — so the one view a rebbi uses while actually scanning was the one view that did not tell him a boy was frozen, and a scan aimed at him would behave in a way the screen gave no warning about. Every card on the chart is now built by one shared piece of code, so **the badge shows in all four Avatar modes**, exactly as it already did in List mode, which had never had the bug. Presentation only — freezing, the countdown, the codes and what a scan does to a frozen boy are all completely unchanged.

- **The List layout printed much bigger, emptier cards than the seating chart — same card, half again as wide.** On the class-sheet page, **List (A–Z)** and **Seating chart** are deliberately the identical card: same code, same photo, same points, so a code scanned off either sheet is the same scan. Only the arrangement was meant to differ. But List laid itself out in a fixed **4 columns** (6 landscape) while a seating chart's default room is **6 columns across**, so the same card came out about **177px wide on the list against 116px on the chart** — holding the same 60px code, with all of that extra width spent as blank space around it. Nothing had more padding; the *tracks* were wider, and on paper it read as a list that had been padded out. **List now sizes its columns by the card instead of counting them**, landing on 6 across portrait and 8 across landscape — the same tile as the seating chart's, which is the point: switching layouts should rearrange the cards, not resize them. A short roster still gets full-size cards packed from the left rather than four enormous ones stretched across the sheet. **The preview and the paper agree**, both before and after, which is the rule this page is built on. **No saved data changes and no migration** — this is one line deciding how many columns the list is drawn in.

- **Homework Checked, Attendance and the Bathroom Pass can no longer be deleted from the Activities tab — they can be hidden instead.** Those three are not really buttons; each one is the way you record into a whole tab. Deleting **Attendance** did not just remove it from the Scan screen, it removed the only way to take attendance by scanning — and the Attendance tab stayed exactly where it was, still showing every day you had already taken, with nothing left that could add to it. Worse, it did not come back: each of the three is set up **once**, the first time the app runs, and the app remembers it has done that, so a delete was permanent and there was no obvious way to undo it. **The Delete button on those three rows is now replaced by "Built in — uncheck Show to hide it"**, pointing at the **Show** checkbox already sitting at the front of the same row. Unchecking it does everything a rebbi was reaching for Delete to do: the activity disappears from the Scan screen buttons, the scan bar dropdown and the floating panel, immediately — and it is a checkbox, so it goes back with one click, and none of your records are involved either way. **Everything else on that tab is unchanged.** Tracked activities *you* added — Called on, Chazan, Line Leader, anything from **+ Add activity** on the Tracker tab — still have their Delete button and still delete. So does **❄️ Freeze**, which owns no tab and keeps no record of its own. Point activities are untouched entirely. **No saved data changes and there is no migration** — nothing is added to your save and nothing is converted; this is which button is drawn on three rows.

- **❄️ Freeze no longer appears in the Tracker's activity list.** It was offered there alongside Called on and the rest, with a **+1** next to every boy's name — and pressing it recorded "frozen once more" into the Tracker's log. That is a running count of how many times a boy has been frozen, which is exactly the record removed from the Gradebook's side of the app last release, arriving here by the same route: Freeze is marked internally the same way the other non-point activities are, so it was swept into a list it does not belong in. **Freeze is not a tracked activity.** It starts a countdown and keeps no tally, and a per-boy freeze count is a decision that has not been made and should not arrive as a side effect. It is also of no use where it sat — a **+1** in the Tracker never started an actual freeze; it only wrote a number nothing displayed. **Freeze itself is completely unchanged**: same countdown, same badge, same codes, same Activities-tab row, and scanning a boy with Freeze armed works exactly as before. **Nothing of yours is hidden.** If you did tap +1 on Freeze at some point, that Freeze stays in the Tracker list with its entries intact and undoable — it drops out of the list only when there is genuinely nothing behind it. **Freeze is recognised by the fact that it starts a countdown, not by its name**, so a Freeze you renamed, and a second one at a different length, are both covered. No saved data changes and no migration.

- **A boy who wasn't seated yet couldn't be dragged onto a seat that was already taken — the seat lit up as if it would accept him, and then nothing happened.** Setting up a chart, the natural move is to pick a name out of **Not yet seated** and drop it straight where you want him, even if someone is sitting there — that's how you say "these two are the wrong way round." Dragging one *seated* boy onto another already worked and swapped them, so the same gesture worked in one direction and silently failed in the other, which reads as the drag being broken rather than as a rule. It was made worse by the seat highlighting brass on hover exactly as a valid target does: the chart said "drop here", took the drop, and discarded it with no message. **You can now drop a boy from the tray onto any seat, taken or not** — whoever was sitting there goes back to **Not yet seated**, where you can place him next. Tapping works the same way, which matters on a smartboard or tablet: tap a name, tap any seat. Previously tapping a taken seat did nothing at all, for the same reason. The old two-step way round — press ✕ to empty the seat first, then drag — still works exactly as before if that's the habit you've built. **Nothing is lost when a boy is displaced:** the "not yet seated" list isn't stored anywhere, it's simply worked out fresh each time as "everyone who isn't currently in a seat", so a displaced boy reappears in the tray the instant the chart redraws — he is never dropped from the class, and his points, photo and history are untouched. **No saved data changes and there is no migration** — this is one line of behaviour in how a drop is handled. The fix is in the single shared piece of code both charts use, so it works identically on the live seating chart and on the class-sheet mock-up in the print page.

- **Marking bathroom passes off the full-screen class sheet looked like it was doing nothing, and then every pass appeared at once the moment you closed the sheet.** Arm **Bathroom Pass** on the scan bar, pop the class sheet out to full screen, and tap a few boys: each tap was recorded correctly and beeped, but the scan bar across the top did not move. Tap five boys and the bar still showed whatever was there before you started. Close the full-screen sheet and all five passes appeared together, as if they had only just happened. Nothing was ever lost — every pass was written the moment it was tapped — but there was no way to tell that from the screen, which is the whole reason the bar is there. A rebbi standing at the front of the room with the sheet on the smartboard had no confirmation that the tap had registered, and the natural response to a button that looks dead is to press it again. **What was happening:** the Bathroom Pass is the one activity of its kind that also writes a line into your scan history, so it genuinely belongs on the bar — but the tap finished without ever telling the bar to redraw. The line sat in the history, correct and complete, simply not yet on screen, and stayed that way until something unrelated happened to redraw the bar. Closing the full-screen sheet is one such thing, which is why they all arrived together at that moment. **Now the bar redraws on the tap itself**, so each boy's pass appears the instant you tap him, while you are still on the full-screen sheet — and the Dashboard's **Recent scans** panel, which reads the same history and was equally out of date, is refreshed at the same time. **Nothing else changes.** A boy who has already used his pass this week is still refused, still with the same message, and still without adding a second line to your history; and giving points is completely untouched. **Note that Homework Checked and Attendance behave differently and are not affected by this** — they keep their own records rather than writing to your scan history, so they never had a line to show on the bar in the first place.

- **The app had quietly started keeping a tally of how many times each boy was frozen. It no longer does, and any tally already made is removed.** Nobody asked for that record and nothing ever displayed it — it appeared as a side effect of how ❄️ Freeze was built. Freeze doesn't award points, so it is marked internally the same way Homework Checked and the Bathroom Pass are; and the setup step that prepares the Gradebook makes a counter for every activity marked that way. Homework and the pass are deliberately left out of that step. Freeze was added later and was never added to the list, so it got a counter of its own: a per-boy count of freezes, sitting in your save, waiting for the recording half of the Gradebook to start filling it in. **This is not a record this app is willing to create by accident.** A count of how often a boy was frozen is the first step toward a running tally of a boy's behaviour, and that is a deliberate, separate decision that has not been made — it is not something that should arrive because of the order two features happened to be built in. **What changes for you:** nothing you can see, and nothing you do differently. Freeze itself is completely unchanged — same countdown, same badge, same codes, same everything. **Nothing of yours is deleted.** The counter is removed only where it is genuinely empty, which today is everywhere, because nothing has ever written to it; if anything *had* been recorded against it, it is kept instead, records and all. It is removed once — if you deliberately make a counter like that yourself later, it stays. **Freeze is now recognised by the fact that it starts a countdown, not by its name**, so a Freeze you have renamed, and a second one you have set up at a different length, are both covered. **Data:** one new field, `data.freezeCountItemsDropped`, backfilled in `load2fix()`. No `DATA_VERSION` change; your activities, your freezes and every other tracked item are untouched. The migration harness now runs 261 checks with none failing — including that a renamed Freeze is still skipped, that a counter holding real records is never removed, and that a re-created one survives the next load.
- **A boy with no photo got a smaller QR code than everyone else on the same sheet.** In the new **QR code + photo** and **Activity code + photo** modes, the photo and the code sit in a row inside each card — and that row was sizing itself from whatever it happened to contain rather than from the card. For a boy with a photo it measured the picture and came out full size; for a boy with **no** photo it measured his two initials of text instead and came out a third narrower, **which shrank his QR code from 64px to 44px** while every other code on the page stayed full size. His initials box was small and cramped too. Both now take their size from the card, so **every seat prints identically whether the boy has a picture or not** — same photo box, same code, same row height, verified across a class where one boy has no photo. It was a real defect on the live site rather than a theoretical one, and it got there because the roster used to check that feature gave *every* boy a photo, so the one case that breaks was never on screen. Presentation only — no code payload, setting or saved data is affected, and a code that scanned before scans the same.

- **Every student appeared twice in List mode.** The Class list drew each boy's avatar, name and score, and then drew all three again — so a class of twenty read as forty, in pairs. With **⛶ Show codes** on it was worse: each card carried the avatar, name and score *and* the QR code and the name a second time underneath. One card per boy now, in list and scan view alike. Nothing about tapping, scoring or the codes themselves changes — a card that was tappable still is, and the QR is the same code it always was.

- **The preview was not the page — it was about 300px wider, and the seats printed half an inch taller than it showed.** The mock-up was a card that stretched to about 1100px on screen, while the sheet prints 8.5in (816px) with 0.3in margins; on top of that the seats were sized twice, once for the screen and again, differently, in the print stylesheet — 1.5in tall on paper against about 1in on screen. So the same class could sit three-across in the preview and wrap to four rows on paper, and a sheet that looked like it fitted came out over two pages. **The preview is now the page**: same width, same margins, same box, and it follows Portrait/Landscape so a landscape sheet is laid out against a landscape page. Every measurement that decides how things wrap is now written **once** and used by both, so what wraps on screen wraps on paper. On a narrow window the preview scrolls sideways instead of being squeezed — squeezing it would put back the very mismatch this removes.
- **The "may not fit on one page" warning never appeared, and the sheet jumped about while it drew.** The warning was working from a hard-coded guess at the page — written back when the preview and the paper genuinely were different documents (#142) — and that guess was still quoting the old inch-and-a-half seats, so it had stopped describing anything real. It measures the actual page now, which it can do again because the preview *is* the page. It was also being computed a fraction too early: the QR codes arrive as images, an image has no height until it has decoded, and the whole sheet was briefly short — which both fooled the check and made the preview visibly jolt as the codes popped in. The code boxes now hold their square shape from the first moment, so nothing jumps and the measurement is right. Checked at the boundary in both orientations: a sheet 21 points too tall warns, one 16 points inside does not.
- **Three rows of boys and three rows of codes now fit on one landscape page.** The sheet was carrying a lot of air: inch-and-a-half seat boxes, 10px grids, 16px above the codes block, and padding inside every code card. A pass over all of it — seats, gaps, headings, section boxes and cards — leaves the same information in noticeably less room. A nine-boy class in a 3×3 with all thirteen codes now uses about **670 of the 758 available points of height** in landscape, so it fits with room to spare instead of pushing the codes onto a second page; portrait has a third of the page left over. Nothing was removed and nothing shrank to the point of being hard to read — the QR codes are untouched.
- **Setup and "Arrange seats" were two switches for one thing.** Opening ⚙ Setup on the seating chart gave you rows, columns and a print button — but seats still would not move until you found a second button inside it and pressed that too, which reads as the panel being broken. It is one switch now: **⚙ Setup & arrange** opens it *and* makes the seats draggable, and **✓ Done arranging** ends both. Everything that already cancelled arranging — turning on Show codes, leaving the print page — now closes Setup with it, so the two can never end up disagreeing. **Rebbi's view moved out to the toolbar** with the other view buttons, where it belongs and where you can still reach it: it is deliberately unavailable while seats are being dragged, which inside Setup would now have meant never.

- **Typing a custom amount after pressing "Custom (add)" fed your digits to the scanner instead of the box.** Pressing **Custom (add)** or **Custom (subtract)** armed Custom and then threw the cursor at the scan box — so a rebbi who pressed the button first and typed his amount second was typing into the scanner's input, six inches from the field he was looking at. The number never reached the box, and the scan buffer filled with digits that then came back as "unknown code". The cursor now lands **in the amount field, with the number selected**, because the amount is the only thing left to decide at that point; press **Enter** (or click anywhere else) and it commits and hands the cursor on to the scan box. Typing the amount first and then pressing the button works exactly as it always did. **Scanning boys straight from there still works** — a scanner fires its whole code in one fast burst ending in Enter, which is a thing a person typing an amount never does, and a student code always carries letters or a dash while an amount never does; a burst that looks like a code is handed to the scanner's own handling, and the amount box is put back exactly as you left it. That last part matters because a number field silently *accepts the digits inside a student code* and refuses the rest, so without it a stray scan would have left a real-looking wrong number sitting in the box. Reported by a beta rebbi.

- **The full-screen seating chart left the Dashboard/History subtabs row showing above it, wasting almost 50px it used to reclaim.** The overlay's top offset was always measured off the bottom of the sticky scan bar, which sits below the header, the subtabs row, *and* the scan bar itself — so the popout only ever started below all three, even though the subtabs row does nothing useful once the chart covers `<main>` (its only two destinations, Dashboard and History, are either where you already are or unreachable behind the overlay). The subtabs row is now hidden for as long as the popout is open, and the offset is measured after it's hidden rather than before, so the chart's top edge sits right below the header and scan bar, same as it used to. Closing the popout restores the subtabs row exactly as before. Presentation only — no data or behavior beyond the popout's own layout changes.

- **The shared development checkout now re-syncs itself to `main` automatically.** Work happens in parallel isolated worktrees that merge their own PRs, so the shared folder never moved on its own and could sit dozens of merges behind while every check looked green — indistinguishable, when you opened the file, from a failed deploy. A hook on session start and session end now fast-forwards it. It only acts when that folder is on `main` with nothing uncommitted; if branch work is parked there it changes nothing and just reports how far behind it is. Dev-only — no app behavior changes, nothing touched in the app or its data. See `.claude/hooks/sync-main-checkout.sh`.

- **A CI check now runs the syntax/CSS/migration-sync validators on every PR into `main` and every push to `main`.** These are the same checks CLAUDE.md has always documented as manual pre-commit steps; they weren't run anywhere automatically, which is how the crash below shipped unnoticed. Dev-only — no app behavior changes. See `.github/workflows/validate.yml`.

- **The app failed to load at all — a broken merge, not a code bug.** PR #167 (the print-code drag-and-drop picker) merged with an unresolved conflict against the Freeze feature still sitting in the file: the `menuLabelItemsAll()` return statement closed correctly, but the leftover lines from the other side of the conflict were left dangling right after it — a bare `: freezeActivity(a) ? ... : fmtDelta(a.pts)};` with no opening ternary to belong to. A stray `:` there is a JavaScript syntax error, and since all of `app.html`'s logic lives in one `<script>` block, one syntax error anywhere in it stops the entire block from parsing — nothing in the app worked, for every rebbi, the moment this reached `main`. **The fix restores both features' intent together**: freeze activities are now correctly counted as `tracked` (grouping with Homework Checked/Bathroom Pass/Attendance in the print-code picker, not with point activities) and correctly get their "starts a _ freeze" description back in the picker and on printed sheets — the freeze-timer PR's changes here were the ones actually lost in the bad merge, not the print-code PR's. No data model touched; this is a pure code fix.

- **Sending attendance was losing most of the class.** A shiur of twenty would land seven rows in the Sheet, with the wrong names against them, and rows visibly rearranging themselves as they arrived. The app was sending the Sheet a **separate message for every single boy**, all at once. Google runs those at the same time rather than one after another, and each one worked out which line to write on before any of the others had written — so they all picked the same line and wrote over each other. The header fix in the last release protected the column titles only; the rows underneath were never protected. **The whole class now goes in one message and is written in one protected operation**, so twenty boys means twenty rows, in order, with the right names. **Nothing about how you take attendance changes**, and the same rule as before still applies: only boys whose status you have actually changed are sent, so changing one boy still sends exactly one row. **This is a change to the Apps Script**, so it reaches your Sheet once you paste the new script and do **Manage deployments → New version**. **Until you do, attendance still works** — the new script understands both the old and new way of sending, so nobody is left unable to send while they catch up. If your Attendance Log already has missing or wrong rows from this, they cannot be recovered automatically; the day's attendance is still correct inside the app and can be re-sent after redeploying.

- **A section with every tab switched off no longer strands you on a page you cannot get back from.** Hiding tabs one at a time under **Settings → Tabs shown** until a whole section is empty would leave the app able to send you to a tab inside that section — a page that is switched off, in a section no longer in the row along the top, with no way back. It now falls back to the Dashboard, which can never be hidden. Nothing about hiding tabs changes; this is only about where you land when a section has nothing left in it.

- **The Gradebook no longer marks absences in red.** A boy with six absences printed as six red cells, and the Gradebook is a page a rebbi may well turn toward a parent. Red does not say *absent*; it says *guilty*, and a term's record should not open an argument about the boy before anyone has read a single date. **Absent and no-credit now sit in a quiet slate grey instead** — the same information, without the verdict attached. Late and partial credit keep their amber, present and full credit keep their green, so nothing has become harder to tell apart: slate and amber are further apart on screen than the old red and amber were. **The three shades are also deliberately held at the same visual weight**, so absences read as *noticeable* rather than as the loudest thing on the page — a record you can scan, not an accusation you have to defend. **And they now come out identical on a black-and-white printer.** This was caught by actually printing one: the shades were chosen to look right on screen, and the absent one happened to be a little darker than the others, which does not show on a monitor but on paper made every ✗ the heaviest mark on the page — the same problem as the red, arriving by a different route. The three are now matched so that a mono printer renders them as one flat shade, and the ✓ · L · ✗ do the work, which is what they were always there for. On a colour printer nothing changes. **Nothing about what is recorded changes**, and the cells still read ✓ present · L late · ✗ absent exactly as before: the mark in the box is what carries the meaning, and the colour behind it was only ever there to help you find it faster. The empty states are untouched — a day with nothing recorded is still the faint dot, and an unchecked homework is still a genuinely empty box. Presentation only; no saved data is touched.

- **Three things wrong with how the Gradebook draws, all visible the moment it had real attendance in it.** The grid shipped with nothing recorded against it, so none of this showed until the attendance conversion filled it in. **The Student column no longer has scrolled dates showing to the left of it.** Scroll a wide grid sideways and a narrow strip of the columns you had scrolled past stayed visible down the left-hand edge, overlapping where the student names should have been — the names column was doing its job, but stopped about 19 pixels short of the edge the card actually cuts off at, and the dates showed through the difference. The table now scrolls inside its own frame, whose edge lines up exactly with where the column stops, so there is no strip left for anything to show in. Fixed for the header as well as the rows, in both grid shapes. **The key underneath the grid no longer slides away when you scroll.** It was inside the part that scrolls sideways, so scrolling right to see later dates carried the key off to the left with it — by a few columns in, the Attendance key had gone entirely and the Homework one was cut off mid-word. It now sits still underneath the grid where it belongs, whatever the grid is doing. **And the cells now use a tick and a cross, so the exceptions are the thing you see.** The grid used to be letters throughout, and worse, the same letter could mean two different things in adjacent columns — Attendance showed **P** for *present* while Homework showed **P** for *partial credit*, with both explanations sitting in the same key looking like a contradiction. Attendance now reads **✓** present · **L** late · **✗** absent, and Homework **(empty)** unchecked · **✓** full credit · **P** partial credit · **✗** no credit. On a page of mostly-present boys the lates and absences stand out instead of having to be read letter by letter. **A value only gets a symbol where the symbol is genuinely obvious** — *late* and *partial* keep letters, because neither has a natural mark and both would be misread in the Total column, where the code sits directly against its count. **✓ and ✗ meaning the good and the bad outcome in both items is deliberate**, and is not the muddle that was fixed: they mean the same *kind* of thing wherever they appear, whereas *present* and *partial credit* were two unrelated facts sharing a letter. **Codes are fixed to the wording they stand for**, so they never shift because of what else you happen to be showing — turn Attendance off and Homework's codes are unchanged, and a page printed today matches one printed last week. **Your own scales still work**: an item with your own list of options falls back to initials as before, and if one of those would clash with a code already on screen it grows until it doesn't. **A homework marked *unchecked* now shows a genuinely empty box, while a day with nothing recorded at all still shows the faint dot** — those are different facts and used to look identical. The key underneath spells all of it out, including the empty one, in a separate labelled box per item. **Screen readers now read the real word**, not the mark: every cell announces "Attendance, Jul 17, present" and every total "Homework, total, full credit 8, partial credit 4" — better than the old letters, which were announced as bare letters. Presentation only — nothing about what is recorded, stored or printed changes, and no saved data is touched.

- **A saved contest now keeps its own results, instead of quietly becoming an empty label.** "Save to history" only ever stored a contest's name, dates and mode — never anyone's scores. Every time you opened a past contest, the app worked the numbers out again from your scan history, and that history only holds the most recent 500 scans. So a contest you deliberately saved would show its full results for a few days, then start showing *less* than really happened as older scans dropped off the end, and eventually "No scan data found for this contest" — with nothing wrong on screen to tell you the numbers had been shrinking all along. A saved contest now **stores each student's total at the moment you save it**, so it stays exactly right however long ago it ran and no longer depends on the scan history outliving it. **Contests you have already saved are rescued on this update**: whatever of their scans is still in your history is added up and stored properly. Anything already dropped off cannot be brought back — and the card now says so plainly rather than implying nobody scored. Re-saving a contest that is still running refreshes its stored results, and a contest still in progress always shows live numbers rather than a frozen snapshot. **What is kept and what is not:** the totals are saved, the scan-by-scan list underneath them is not — it stays tied to your real scan history, because that is what the ↩ undo arrows act on, and a list you could not undo from would be worse than an honest note saying the detail has aged out. Undo itself is completely unchanged. **Data:** two new fields on each saved-contest record, backfilled on load, no `DATA_VERSION` change and nothing existing altered.

- **Ending a contest no longer wipes everyone's points and your whole scan history.** This is the most serious thing fixed so far, and it destroyed real data. The "End contest?" dialog offered two buttons, and **OK ended the contest *and* set every student's score to zero *and* deleted the entire scan history** — every entry in History, for everyone. The dialog never mentioned the history at all. Worse, it stated the opposite of what happened: it promised the contest's own results would stay available afterwards, with the option to merge them into regular scores — and both of those are worked out by reading the very history OK had just deleted, so pressing it destroyed exactly what it said it would keep. A contest run in "contest only" mode lost its points outright. And **Cancel was not a way out** — the contest ended whichever button you pressed; Cancel only declined the wipe. A rebbi who opened that dialog by accident could not back out of it. **Now: ending a contest ends the contest.** One plain question, nobody's points touched, results stay on the Contest tab, Merge still there when it applies — and **Cancel genuinely cancels**, leaving the contest running. The old dialog wording is deleted rather than reworded, because it described something that was never true.
- **The "Reset all scores" button in Settings now tells you what it really does.** It always deleted the full scan history along with the scores, and its warning never said so — it only mentioned that the roster and activities stay. The new confirmation says plainly that it clears **every entry in History for everyone**, that Undo and contest totals are worked out from that history and go with it, **how many students are affected** (all of them, every class, not just the one selected), and — the reassuring part nobody was being told — that your **Trends history is kept**: lifetime per-activity totals and weekly history are stored separately and are not touched. It also suggests downloading a backup first. The button still does the same thing; it now asks honestly, and afterwards confirms what was cleared and what was kept.

- **The full-screen seating chart no longer shows two "Undo" controls back to back.** With **⛶ Show codes** on, the codes footer already carries an "Undo Last Scan" QR tile (tappable, same as a scanned code) — and the separate **↩ Undo last scan** button used to render directly underneath it regardless, so the same action appeared twice in a row. That button now only shows when the codes footer is hidden, where it's the sole way to undo without leaving the chart; turn codes on and its job is already covered by the tile.
- **A student's own Attendance code was silently logging a fake 0-point award instead of taking attendance.** When Attendance became a scan-to-cycle activity (present → absent → late), the two-step arm-then-scan path was updated to cycle it properly — but the merged personal code (`PA:<student>:<activity>`, the one printed on per-student pages and on the new per-activity seating charts) was never given the matching branch, and fell through to the ordinary points path instead. Scanning it did nothing to the boy's actual attendance and quietly wrote a meaningless "Attendance +0" entry to History. **Homework Checked's and Bathroom Pass's personal codes were already correct** — only Attendance's was missing the reroute. It now cycles present/absent/late exactly as the arm-then-scan path always has, and if the Attendance tab or full-screen view happens to be open it updates live, same as everywhere else attendance is taken. No data model change; this is a scan-handling fix only. Anyone who has already scanned a student's personal Attendance code should check that boy's point History for a stray "Attendance +0" entry and disregard it — it carries no real attendance information and his actual attendance for that day was never recorded.
- **The seating chart showed two identical "↩ Undo last scan" buttons stacked at the bottom.** A previous fix taught the button to hide itself while **⛶ Show codes**'s QR footer was showing its own Undo tile — but once Show codes and Show activities became independent toggles sharing one `renderScanUndoRow()` helper (so List mode could reuse it too), that helper started rendering unconditionally in the seating chart as well, right underneath the older button, which was never removed. With both toggles off — the ordinary seating chart — that left two of the exact same button, one on top of the other. The stale inline copy is deleted; `renderScanUndoRow()` is now the single source of the button in seating mode, matching how List mode already calls it. Presentation only; no saved data or behavior beyond the extra button is affected.
- **Chazaroom: the AI-prompt maker's own format template was ambiguous about what its answer fields should contain, and Gemini specifically was reading it literally — importing questions whose answers were just the letters "A"/"B"/"C"/"D" instead of real text.** The template line mixed clearly-bracketed placeholders (`<time limit in seconds>`, `<correct option number 1-4>`) with bare, unbracketed text for the answer fields (`"Option A"`, `"Option B"`, `"Option C"`, `"Option D"`) — nothing marked those as "replace this," so a model reading it literally could echo the placeholder text back as the actual answer. Every field is now consistently bracketed (`<answer option A>` etc.), and the prompt now says explicitly never to output the literal words "Option A" or a bare letter as an answer. **Also added a live safety net, not just a better prompt** — CSV import (used for any pasted CSV, not only this feature's own output) now checks each imported question's four answer fields for exactly this pattern and, if found, still imports normally but tells the rebbi plainly how many questions have a bare-letter answer that needs fixing before playing, rather than silently landing him in a game where every question reads "A B C D." Verified live: a CSV mixing one bad row with one real one imported both and correctly flagged only the bad one; an all-clean import showed the normal message with no warning.
- **Chazaroom: the lobby's roster chips could shrink to nearly invisible, game pills could balloon into oversized blobs, and the page still visibly changed width going from the lobby into the first question — three follow-on bugs from the fixed-height board work.** All three trace back to the same flexbox layout added to make the board fill the viewport with no page scroll:
  - `#calibGrid` (the lobby's joined-student chips) shared its flex-shrink weight with the hint text and the anonymous-count line — under real pressure from `.rebbi` (the lobby's other real flex child, the camera/aim-pad card, also competing for the same space) that let the chips shrink all the way to `min-height:0`, hiding them almost entirely rather than just trimming them. It now gets the same dedicated treatment `#pillBar` already had (its own flex-grow share, a real internal scrollbar) instead of sharing the plain-shrink pool.
  - Both `#pillBar` and `#calibGrid` were given real `flex-grow` so board-foot/the button row lands at a consistent position regardless of pill-row count — but neither container overrode `align-items`/`align-content` (both default to `stretch` on a flex-wrap row), so a short class in a tall box stretched its one row of chips or pills to fill all the leftover vertical space, turning them into oversized blobs instead of staying pill-shaped. Both now pin `align-items:flex-start;align-content:flex-start`.
  - `max-width:none` (full width, no side margins) was still `body.playing`-only — the lobby (`mode==="calib"`) rendered under the site's normal 1150px-capped, centered layout right up until the first question loaded and `body.playing` flipped on, producing a visible side-margin jump into the very first question — the other half of "the whole page gets bigger or smaller based on the beat," alongside the height fix from earlier. Both states now share the wider layout — but that alone wasn't enough: the base `.wrap{margin:0 auto}` rule was never reset, and `margin:auto` on a flex item's cross axis overrides `stretch` and sizes it to content instead of filling the row, so `.wrap` stayed narrow and centered even with `max-width:none` applied until `margin:0` was added alongside it.
  Verified live at both extremes: a 6-student lobby (previously-hidden-chip territory) shows all chips fully visible; a 5-student game board (previously-blob territory) keeps pills at their normal compact size instead of stretching; a 25-student class with a 3-line question still holds every earlier full-height guarantee (zero page scroll, tiles/buttons always fully visible, pill row scrolling internally); and `.wrap` now measures the full body width in both the lobby and the game rather than a narrower, centered box. Zero console errors throughout.

- **The subtab row's Sound on / Quick Start / ? icons collapsed to the left edge of the header instead of staying pinned on the right, whenever a group was down to one visible subtab.** `renderSubTabs()` deliberately leaves `nav#subTabs` empty in that case (a single-tab group shows no subtab pills), and `nav.subtabs:empty{display:none}` removes it from the flex row entirely — which also removed the `flex:1` spacer that was pushing `.subtabs-icons` to the right in every other state. With the nav gone, the icons were the row's only remaining flex child and sat at the left instead. Fixed with `margin-left:auto` on `.subtabs-icons`, so it stays pinned right regardless of whether the subtab nav is present, populated, or empty. Presentation only — which subtabs are hidden, and the single-tab-shows-no-row behavior itself, are unchanged.

- **One class's scans were showing up in another class's history — and, worse, "Undo last scan" could reverse a scan that belonged to a different class entirely.** A rebbi with two shiurim keeps one log, and almost every surface that reads it was reading *all* of it. So the scan bar under the seating chart in Shiur B showed the boys of Shiur A, the History tab listed both classes mixed together, and pressing **↩ Undo last scan** while Shiur B was selected took back the last scan made anywhere — which, if the previous shiur had just finished, was one of *their* boys' points, in a class that was no longer on screen. Nothing announced it: the points came off a boy the rebbi could not see, and the row explaining it was on a tab he was not looking at. Recent scans was the one surface already scoped correctly; everything beside it was not.
  - **Every scan-history surface is now scoped to the selected class**, through one shared pair of helpers (`logEntryInCurrentClass()` / `visibleLog()`) rather than a filter re-written per view: the seating chart's scan strip and its History dropdown, the History tab (both its rows and the activity dropdown that narrows them), the Coin Deposit history, and the Contest tab's "History" view — that last one was the only one of the contest tab's three views that wasn't already narrowed, since Class List and By Student both walk `activeStudents()` and so were scoped by construction.
  - **Undo is scoped the same way, and that is the half that actually protected points.** `undoMostRecentScan()` — the "Undo last scan" buttons on the Record tab and the Dashboard, and the printed `CTRL:UNDO` card — now looks only within the selected class. This is the one undo where the rebbi never picks a row: he presses a button and takes whatever it finds, so it has to find something in the class in front of him. A tracked scan held for undo from another class is dropped rather than kept, so it can't surface later when he switches back. The per-row ↩ buttons needed no change — they undo the row they're attached to, and those rows are now scoped.
  - **"All classes" is untouched and still reaches everything**, which is the mode that exists for exactly that. The scoping only engages once a specific class is picked.
  - **A scan whose student has since been deleted is still shown, in every class.** There is no class left to test it against, so display and undo deliberately share one predicate — if undo skipped those while the scan bar still listed them, "Undo last scan" would reverse something other than the row sitting at the front of the bar.
  - **Read-path only: no new fields, no `DATA_VERSION` bump, no migration, and no stored number changes.** `data.log` is written exactly as before and every entry is still kept — only which entries a given screen *shows*, and which one the blind undo reaches for, has changed. Contest totals and the saved snapshots they feed are deliberately left global: those numbers belong to the contest, not to whichever class happens to be selected while looking at it.
  - **Browser-verified against a seeded two-class roster** where the newest scan overall belonged to the *other* class — the case that made this a points bug rather than a display one. Undo in Shiur A reversed Shiur A's own newest scan and left Shiur B's score untouched; undo in Shiur B reversed its own; undo under "All classes" reversed the newest remaining anywhere. Recent scans, the scan strip, the seats History dropdown, the History tab, Coin Deposit and the contest History view each showed only the selected class and the full set under "All classes". No console errors.

### Changed

- **The Gradebook is hidden for now, because it was showing you attendance that stopped updating.** The grid was only ever filled once — by the conversion that ran the first time you loaded the version that introduced it — and nothing has written to it since. So it showed your attendance **as it stood on that day**, and every day you have taken since was simply missing from it, with no gap, no "up to" date and nothing on the page saying so. A short record and a complete one look identical, which is the whole problem: it did not look broken, it looked like a working page with the wrong numbers on it. The other items on it — Homework, Bathroom Pass and any of your own tracked activities — have never had anything behind them at all, for the same reason; their real records are safe where they have always been, on the Homework and Passes tabs, which are unchanged and still correct. **Nothing has been deleted and nothing has been lost.** The tab is hidden, not removed: every row it had converted is still in your save exactly as it was, the receipt of what came across is still there, and the page itself is untouched and waiting. **It comes back when the recording half is built** (#123) — the part that writes to it as you scan, which is what makes the numbers true — and not before, because a tidier-looking version of a page that is not being updated is the same problem with better formatting. **If you want it back in the meantime it is yours to take**: Run → Settings → Tabs shown → tick Gradebook, and it stays on through every future load — the app will not hide it on you a second time. **Data:** one new field, `data.gradebookHiddenSeeded`, backfilled in `load2fix()`. Purely additive — no `DATA_VERSION` change, nothing renamed, converted or removed, and `data.trackedItems` / `data.trackedData` are not read, written or reshaped by any of this, so the records already in them come through byte-for-byte identical. Unlike the print-options fields, this one is deliberately **not** in `defaults`: the flag is missing from a brand-new install and from every existing save alike, so **everyone** stops seeing the stale grid on their next load rather than only new rebbeim — a rebbi who has been running it for weeks is exactly the one it has been misleading. The migration harness carries the same seed and adds twelve checks for it, including that an existing save is hidden, that a deliberate un-tick is never re-hidden, and that tracked-item data comes through untouched.
- **Student photo prints at a usable size on the class sheet — about an inch instead of under half an inch.** Choosing **Student photo** printed a 40px circle: 0.42in in a card 1¼in wide, so most of the card sat empty and the picture came out **smaller than the boy's name printed underneath it**. At that size a printed face is not reliably a face, which defeats the only reason to print one. The photo now **fills the card up to a 1in cap** and is **cropped square rather than round**, the same as in the paired photo modes — between the two, it shows roughly five times the face it did before. It **scales with the card** rather than jumping to a fixed size, so a chart with a lot of columns shrinks the picture to fit instead of overflowing the seat. **Round avatars are unchanged everywhere else in the app** — this is the printed seat card only. **This does make a photo-only sheet taller**, and that is the deliberate trade: a seat grows from about 72px to about 128px, so a chart of six rows takes roughly 3½in more page and a sheet that only just fitted one page may now want two. **The fit warning under the preview is what tells you**, and switching to *QR code + photo* or back to *QR code* returns the sheet to its old height. **QR code, None, and both "+ photo" modes are untouched** — their printed size is exactly what it was, so this changes the pagination of the one mode it is about and no other. No setting changes and nothing is stored differently; a rebbi who prints photo sheets just gets a bigger picture.

- **The setup wizard is shorter, and it now ends with your class sheet in the printer.** It used to claim five steps, actually show four, and finish on a "You're all set!" screen that left you looking at an empty dashboard — with the three things you actually needed to start (your activities, your printed codes, your first scan) all still ahead of you and nothing telling you that. It is now **four real steps: Class Basics → Students → Points or coins → Activities**, and the last button takes you straight to **Run → Print class sheet**, the one page that gets you scanning. Nothing is deleted and nothing is lost — everything that left the wizard is still exactly where it was in Settings.
  - **Students: one name per line, and the class comes from step 1.** You no longer type your class next to every boy. Whatever you named the class on the first screen is the class every boy goes into, and that class is selected in the header dropdown from the start. **If your list is "Cohen, Yossi" rather than "Yossi Cohen" — the way most school office exports come out — it gets flipped around for you**, and the preview under the box shows the names exactly as they will be saved so you can catch it if a line was meant to stay as typed. Only a single comma (or a single tab, from a two-column spreadsheet paste) is treated this way; a messier line is left exactly as you typed it rather than guessed at, because guessing wrong renames a boy. **This replaces the old `Name, Group` column** — the group column had nothing left to do once the class comes from step 1.
  - **Activities are now part of setup.** The three starters (On time, Davening nicely, Learning nicely) are shown already filled in, and you can reword them, change the points, remove any you won't use, or add your own — before you print, so your first sheet carries your codes and not the samples. Remove them all and it tells you the sheet will print without activity codes rather than letting you find that out on paper.
  - **"Points or coins?" is now asked as a plain question, and it is asked before activities** — so you're choosing point values in the unit you actually picked.
  - **The seating grid sizes itself to your roster.** The old fixed 4×6 grid held 24 boys, so a class of 30 printed its first sheet with six boys listed as unplaced. It now grows to fit before you ever see the page.
  - **Learning setup and the COM-port/scanner step were removed from the wizard.** A keyboard-wedge scanner needs no setup at all, and Serial mode still lives in Settings, where the one-time "Connect scanner" tap has to happen anyway. Learning setup (Pesukim / Mishnayos / Shorashim) is unchanged and still fully available.
- **"Print class sheet" is now "Seating chart & print" — because arranging your room and printing it are the same page.** The tab was named for printing alone, so a rebbi looking for his *seating chart* had no reason to click the one tab that holds it, and the chart looked like it lived somewhere else. It doesn't: the preview on that page **is** the editor — drag a boy into a different desk, ✕ to empty his seat. Same page, same everything, it just says so now.
- **The paragraph on that page about auto-seated students is replaced by a ⚙ Rearrange seats button.** It used to say how many boys had been seated for you and then give directions to the Dashboard to move them — directions that pointed *away* from the page you were already standing on, which is the only place seats actually drag. The count was never something you had to act on. The button scrolls straight to the chart and highlights it, which on a full class sits below the controls. **Nothing about how boys get seated changed** — an unarranged class is still auto-seated in roster order so the sheet is never a page of empty desks, and anyone you had already placed still stays exactly where you put him.
- **Review starts hidden for brand-new setups, and turns back on with one checkbox.** A rebbi setting up for the first time gets the smaller app; **Settings → the Review checkbox** brings the whole group back whenever he wants it, and it says so on screen during setup. **This only affects setups that go through the wizard.** If you are already using Menchmark, nothing about your tabs changes — and **restoring a backup or your Google Sheet no longer imposes it either**, so a rebbi coming back from a backup keeps the tabs he had. (Previously the wizard could hide Review on a restore, because it saves its progress on every screen — including simply opening the Restore screen.)
- **The code sections are yours now — add them, name them, delete them — and they start as Positive, Negative and Navigation.** They used to be a fixed trio the app chose for you: *Points*, *Tracked*, *Navigation*. You could rename them and drag codes between them, but there were always exactly three and they always meant what we said they meant. Now the list is yours: **+ Section** adds one, its ✕ removes it, and typing in a heading renames it — on the printed sheet, the smartboard chart and the activity menu together, because all three read the one list. **The starting three split by what a code actually does to a boy's score**, which is the distinction the colours on the cards were already making: a gain, a deduction, or neither. So *Points* — which held praise and consequences side by side — becomes **Positive** and **Negative**, and everything that scores nothing shares **Navigation**: Homework Checked, Bathroom Pass and Attendance alongside Undo, Pick and the phrase codes. **Freeze sits in Negative** even though it scores nothing, because it is unmistakably a consequence. **+Custom and −Custom follow the same rule as everything else** and split across Positive and Negative rather than both hiding under Navigation. A rebbi who wants his tracked codes in a box of their own adds a section and drags them in — which is the whole point of the sections being his. **Deleting a section never deletes codes**: whatever was in it moves to the first section, and the last remaining section can't be deleted, so a code always has somewhere to be.
- **Data (`DATA_VERSION` 4 → 5, and this one resets something — read this).** Sections now live in `data.codeSections` as `{key,label}` in print order; the old fixed trio and the separate `data.codeSectionNames` rename overlay are gone, since a section's name is simply its label. **What resets: the arrangement only** — which section each code sat in, its order within that section, and any section titles you renamed. Those per-code assignments named *Points* and *Tracked*, which no longer exist, so they could not have been honoured. **What does not reset: your codes, and which of them are switched on or off.** `data.printExcluded` is not touched, so a rebbi who spent time choosing his ten codes still has exactly those ten — they are simply grouped the new way, and re-dragging is seconds of work. It runs **once, ever**, as a version migration rather than a startup repair, precisely because it is destructive: a reset that ran on every load would wipe your new arrangement every time you reopened the app. Twelve new checks in `test-migration.html` hold it to that — including that an already-migrated save is never reset a second time, and that a corrupt or emptied section list falls back to the defaults instead of leaving codes with nowhere to go.

- **Contests are hidden for now, while the way they start and finish gets reworked — nothing about them has been removed.** The serious problems are fixed: ending a contest no longer wipes anyone's points, and a saved contest now keeps its own results. What is still awkward is everything around the edges — an ended contest gives you no way to start the next one without pressing "Clear", a finished contest cannot be used for the raffle it was run for, and a contest that has ended still looks like it is running on the Leader Board. That is more confusion than a rebbi should meet in something optional, so the whole feature steps out of sight until it is reworked. **Your contest data is completely untouched** — running contests, saved contests and their results, and every contest-tagged scan in your history all stay exactly where they are, and nothing in the app stops recording them. This is a visibility setting, not a deletion. It hides the Contest tab, the contest panel on the Leader Board, and the raffle's "Current contest" ticket option, all from one switch — so if you are mid-contest or simply want it back, **Run → Settings → what's shown in the menu → Recognize → Contest** turns all of it on again in one click, and it stays on from then on.

- **"Download for offline use" now says what it actually costs you — and installing the app is the recommended way to work offline.** The old wording was accurate about what the download *does* and quietly wrong about what it *means*. A downloaded copy is a **separate island of data**: it is a file on your computer rather than the website, so the browser gives it its own storage. Points entered in the downloaded copy never appear in the app you normally use, and points entered there never appear in it. Rebbeim lost work to exactly this — scoring a week into a downloaded file and finding it missing when they opened Menchmark again. Two things change. **Installing Menchmark as an app is now marked as the way to work offline**, because it works with no internet *and keeps one set of data* — it is the same Menchmark, same students, same scores, online or not. **The download stays**, reframed for what it is genuinely good at: a classroom computer that never goes online, or a spare machine you want a self-contained copy on. And it now carries one plain line saying the copy keeps its own separate data. Nothing about the feature itself changed — single-file portability is real and still there.
- Removed dead code from the setup-wizard bridge. Three lines survived the wizard redesign and tested toggles the wizard no longer offers: `features.teams` and `features.qr` (both compared `undefined` against `false`, so neither ever ran — and both named a group and a tab that do not exist, `groups.leaderboard` where the key is `standings`, and a `labels` tab that was never a tab), plus `features.chavrusa`, which was written but read by nothing and is still backfilled to `false` in `load2fix()` regardless. The live and correct `features.learning` line is untouched. No behaviour change today; the point is that Lean/Advanced mode is going to be built on this exact path and should start from keys that are known-good.
- Replaced casual 'kid/kids' with 'student/students' in user-facing copy.
- Merged Menahalim tab to 4 cards to match the other audience tabs.
- Restructured home page: reordered sections (hero → why → features → compare → audience toggle → testimonials → walkthrough → about → CTA), added a 3-tab audience toggle (Rebbeim/Menahalim/Substitutes) as the centerpiece, corrected the walkthrough to the real task-based setup flow, and consolidated the audience-specific standalone sections (Substitute, Menahalim, Shulchani Mode, No-Computer) into the toggle to shorten the page.
- Removed stale open-source language from meta description; added copyright notice to footer.
- **Removed the remaining open-source / MIT licensing language from the two places a rebbi actually sees it** — the app's own About dialog (Help → About) and the `beta.html` footer. The About text now reads "Menchmark is a classroom economy tracker for Yeshiva and Jewish Day School teachers" (was "a free, open-source classroom economy tracker") and credits "Built with ❤️ by Rabbi B. Steinerman" without the trailing "MIT License."; the beta footer reads "Built for the Yeshiva classroom." without "MIT Licensed." This finishes the same cleanup already done to the meta description above — those two strings were simply missed at the time. **Authorship credit and the "View on GitHub" link are deliberately kept**, so nothing about where the project lives or who wrote it is hidden; only the licensing claim in user-facing copy is dropped. Copy only — no behavior, no data, nothing stored.
- **The About dialog no longer calls Menchmark a "classroom economy tracker."** That phrasing was both off-positioning and too small for what the app actually does — it described points and prizes as though they were the point, when the reason to scan a boy is that he helped his chavrusa or held the door. Help → About now reads "Menchmark is a classroom assistant for Yeshiva and Jewish Day School rebbeim — middos and derech eretz, attendance, homework, and learning, all from one scan," matching the wording already used on the site. Also says **rebbeim** rather than "teachers", as every other surface does. The gradebook is deliberately **not** mentioned: its foundation is in (Phase 2a) but the screen itself isn't built, and the landing page still marks it Coming soon — the About text should not promise a rebbi something he can't open yet. Copy only.
- **The intro animation's "s" beat now uses a simpler, unbreakable version on phones; desktop keeps the full flying-letter drop.** The desktop sequence takes the "s" out of *mensch* by measuring live element positions and flying the letter down into the STRESS slot. That measurement is fragile on real mobile browsers — the address bar showing and hiding resizes the visual viewport mid-animation, which throws the position maths off and could leave the "s" landing in the wrong place or disappearing entirely, so a rebbi opening the site on a phone might see the story break rather than play. Screens 640px and under now get a version with no flying element and no measurement at all: the inline "s" turns red in place and fades, the hero fades out, and STRESS fades and scales in pre-positioned at screen centre. Same beats, same story, nothing to mis-measure. **Desktop is untouched** — the full drop, the "men"/"ch" gap-close, and the staggered letter reveal all play exactly as before. Presentation only, on the intro page alone: no app behavior and no stored data is involved.
- Updated home page "How It Works" walkthrough to the real task-based setup flow (was Sheet-first and inaccurate). The four steps are now Add your students → Set up what you reward → Print your codes → Start scanning, matching `quick-start.html`; Google Sheets is no longer implied as a required first step (it's an optional sync layer).
- **Intro animation is now the site front door (`index.html`); previous landing page moved to `home.html`; added Skip control.** Visiting the root now plays the brand-story intro, with an always-visible "Skip →" (top-right) and an end-of-story CTA that both lead to the landing page at `home.html`. Internal links updated accordingly (landing-page links in `beta.html`, `quick-start.html`, and the intro's own CTA now point to `home.html`; the landing footer's "Watch the intro" points to `index.html`).
- **Renamed tab groups to the 5 R's — Record, Review, Recognize, Reward, Run — and reordered to match the workflow.** The nav now reads Record (was Scan) · Recognize (was Standings) · Reward (was Rewards) · Review (was Learn) · Run (was Manage). Labels and order only — internal keys and saved state are untouched, so nothing migrates.
- Marked Chavrusa, Gradebook, and Quiz features as Coming soon on the landing page and setup wizard; updated hero mockup to the 5 R's tab names; corrected sync location wording.
- Rebuilt the landing-page hero mockup's nav as a horizontal tab bar matching the real app (Record · Recognize · Reward · Review · Run, gold active tab) instead of a vertical sidebar; dashboard content now uses the full card width.
- Replaced the hero mockup's invented dashboard (stat cards, Recent Activity feed) with the real Record-tab elements: Now-scoring armed pill, dashed scan box, Recent scans list with undo, and the live Leaderboard.
- Added small monochrome inline SVG icons to the five nav tab labels (scan-corners Record · trophy Recognize · gift Reward · open-book Review · gear Run) — hand-written line icons that inherit the tab's text color, no icon library. Nav bar only — labels elsewhere (e.g. the Settings show/hide list) are unchanged.

### Changed

- **The seating-chart picker now respects the "Skip students who already had a turn" checkbox instead of always skipping.** The seating chart previously skipped had-a-turn students unconditionally, with no way to turn it off, while the checkbox on the Picker panel governed the wheel only. Both Picker modes now share that one checkbox — no new setting. **It is now a saved preference (`data.pickerSkip`) and defaults to ON**, so the seating chart keeps the same skip-until-everyone-has-had-a-turn rotation it has always had, until the rebbi deliberately unchecks it. **Note:** because the two modes now share one setting, the *wheel* also starts each session with skipping ON — previously the checkbox was unsaved and reset to off on every reload. **Dimming now follows the setting:** previously-picked seats are dimmed only while skipping is ON, where dimming correctly reads as "out of the running this round". With skipping OFF those seats render as fully normal and available, because they genuinely are pickable again — dimming them would say something untrue. Only the *display* is gated; the underlying turn state is untouched, so switching skipping back on re-dims exactly who it did before, and the "Already picked this round" list keeps its full record in both states. "Reset round" continues to clear the turn state and un-dim everyone in both states. Data: new `data.pickerSkip` boolean; `DATA_VERSION` bumped 2 → 3 with a v2 → v3 migration that defaults it to ON for every existing save (plus a `load2fix()` backfill as a safety net). Purely additive — no stored field is read, renamed, or removed, so no saved data can be lost.

### Added

- **Bathroom passes can now be marked by scanning, from the Record tab or a QR code — not just by tapping on the Passes tab.** "Bathroom Pass" appears as a normal activity button on the Record tab (name only, no point value, like Homework Checked): tap it, scan a student, and their weekly pass is marked used. It also prints on the **Activity Menu** as a scannable code, and each student gets a personal **Bathroom Pass** QR code on their page under Print → Students, which marks that student's pass in a single scan with no arming step. **The one-pass-per-week rule is unchanged and now shared by every route:** scanning a student who has already used their pass is refused with a clear message, exactly as the Passes tab's own scan box already did — a scan never hands a pass back. Giving a pass back is still the Passes tab's per-student "Give back" button, the deliberate manual correction. Pass usage continues to live in its existing record; nothing about how passes are stored, counted, or reset has changed. **Data:** adds one tracked, 0-point "Bathroom Pass" activity (so it has an id for its button and QR codes) plus a one-shot seeding flag, mirroring how "Homework Checked" already works; `DATA_VERSION` 3 → 4. Purely additive — nothing is renamed, removed, or rewritten, existing pass records are untouched, and deleting the button keeps it deleted.
- **"Homework Checked" is now a normal activity button on the Record tab, and prints on the Activity Menu.** Marking homework no longer means going to the Homework tab first. It appears alongside Davening, Derech Eretz and the rest — tap it, scan students, and their homework is marked, exactly as scanning on the Homework tab does. Because it isn't worth points, the button shows just its name with no value line, and the "Now scoring" pill shows the name alone rather than a meaningless "0". It's also back on the **printed Activity Menu** as a scannable QR code (labelled "marks homework checked" instead of a point value), so a rebbi can arm it from the printed sheet and then scan students. Tracker activities remain excluded from both the buttons and the printed menu — unlike Homework Checked they have no equivalent safety net and would still land on the points path. The floating scan panel also still excludes all of them, for the same reason. **Already working, unchanged:** each student's personal "Homework Checked" QR code, printable from Print → Students under "Personal scoring pages", marks that student's homework in a single scan with no arming step at all.

### Fixed

- **The migration harness reported a permanent, misleading failure: a stale assertion in its "realistic teacher data" scenario.** Test 3 asserted that `navHidden.groups.print` *survived* migration, but `load2fix()` deletes that key on purpose — Phase 1 dissolved the "print" group and moved its tabs onto 🖨 buttons on their data-owner tabs.
